# Public release recon, 2026-11-19 (raw lens results)

Read-only recon for the public-release / deployment roadmap (roadmap 63).
Finished lenses: recon:ci_feasibility, recon:ci_release_research, recon:onboarding, recon:containers_inventory, recon:hygiene, recon:containers_research, recon:ops_security, recon:prior_plans.
Not finished when this was written: none.

## recon:ci_feasibility: What CI could check today, measured on this checkout (local, read-only)

I ran every check the brief listed twice: once in the working tree and once on a `git archive HEAD` export in scratch, using a throwaway HOME and a conf.yaml/qsub.sh copied from the templates. So every failure below is in committed code, not in the uncommitted roadmap_53 edit or the untracked _vol_toy.py.

Most checks already pass and are ready for CI:
- ruff check: clean
- check_boundaries.py: R1–R5 clean
- compileall: 432 files compile
- imports: all 396 modules import except the 4 torch modules under filterTilts/deepLearning, which by design run only inside the tilt_filter container
- the shipped copia-tutorial protocol validates with 0 problems
- the lock is in sync with requirements.in
- the qsub template's exit-marker block matches QSUB_EXIT_MARKERS
- the conf template parses into Config with no unknown keys

Two gates fail on committed code:
- `ruff format --check`: 48 committed files would be reformatted. 20 of them are services/jobs/* files whose assignments were aligned by hand, which the formatter undoes.
- `check_look_literals.py`: 11 ui/ files are 76 literals over the allow-list. The allow-list was written 2026-11-13 and broke within 6 days. Nothing runs the checks automatically, and that is the case for CI.

There are no workflows, no .github directory, no pre-commit hook and no Makefile. All the checks are documented in CLAUDE.md or the roadmaps and none is wired to anything.

`./crboost test` passes 5/5 here. On a template conf it gives 3 passed, 1 skipped and 1 failed. The failure is test_preflight_checks_pass, which by design checks a real site (tool paths, partitions, sbatch on PATH). So a marker split or a template-driven repo test is enough to run the static tier in CI. Roadmap 39 S4's "no pytest" is now too strict.

A cold `install.sh` build in a fresh directory took 5.8 s. It fetches uv 0.13.7, CPython 3.11.18 and 78 wheels, writes nothing to HOME and asks no questions. On ubuntu-latest it would run under dash: /bin/sh is bash on this cluster, so dash compatibility is untested.

Building the whole app with server_app.setup_app (constructed only, never served) works on the template conf with no SLURM call: 75 routes in 5.1 s. A start/curl/stop smoke job on ubuntu-latest is about 25 lines of shell, and the only hard input is a conf.yaml.

Everything except the cluster tier fits in about 1 minute on a free runner. I recommend one `./crboost ci` entry point that both the GitHub workflow and the maintainer run.

### Findings

Evidence:
- scratch outputs: <scratch>/recon/ci-today/{ruff_check.txt,ruff_format.txt,ruff_format_diff.txt,compileall.txt,install_cold.err,lock.out}
- HEAD export: git archive HEAD | tar -x -C .../recon/ci-today/head
- scripts: .../recon/ci-today/scripts/import_all.py, .../scripts/links.py

Recommendation: Wire rows 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 9-15 and 19 as gates now. Gate rows 2 and 4 after the decisions in CI-2 and CI-3. Rows 16 and 20 run on the runner only. Row 17 starts warn-only. Row 18 waits for roadmap 39 S5.

**CI-1 [high, effort S] No check is wired to anything: 0 workflows, no hook, no Makefile, no single entry point**

check_boundaries.py, check_look_literals.py, ruff and the pytest static tier exist only as commands in CLAUDE.md and the roadmaps. The repo has no .github/ directory (the Actions workflows endpoint reports total_count 0), no .pre-commit-config.yaml, no Makefile/justfile/tox/nox, and no non-sample git hook. Roadmap 39 S4 planned a lint.yml and is not built. The cost is already visible: check_look_literals.py and its allow-list landed in 23ddc8a on 2026-11-13, and three later commits pushed it over without anyone running it (CI-2). The `crboost` launcher already has the subcommand dispatch a `ci` verb would join (crboost:30-36: preflight, test, python).

Evidence:
- ls .github -> No such file or directory
- gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/workflows --jq .total_count -> 0
- ls .git/hooks | grep -v sample -> (empty); core.hooksPath unset
- crboost:30-36 (case: preflight|test|python|mcp)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:262-269 (S4, not built)

Recommendation: Add `ci) shift; cd "$here" && exec "$py" scripts/ci.py "$@" ;;` to crboost's second case block. scripts/ci.py runs each step as a subprocess with its own timing, prints the table, exits 1 if any step failed, takes --fast to skip the network steps (lock re-compile, link check), and runs every step even after one fails. The steps:
- ruff check .
- ruff format --check .
- check_boundaries.py
- check_look_literals.py
- compileall over the git-tracked .py files
- the import-every-module smoke (excluding filterTilts/deepLearning)
- pytest -m 'not cluster and not site' (or tests/test_repo.py)
- the lock in sync
- bash -n, plus shellcheck when it is on PATH

The workflow is then checkout, copy conf/qsub from the templates, `./crboost ci`, and the serve smoke. One source of truth that the maintainer runs before pushing. Optionally add a pre-push hook that calls `./crboost ci --fast`, documented rather than installed.

**CI-2 [high, effort S] check_look_literals.py fails on committed code: 11 ui files are 76 literals over the allow-list**

Over-allowance per file (now/allowed): ui/agent_plan_page.py 15/0, ui/analytics/readout_panel.py 8/5, ui/components/check.py 14/0, ui/components/path_text.py 10/0, ui/job_plugins/params_renderer.py 1/0, ui/pipeline_builder/io_config_component.py 42/38, ui/pipeline_builder/job_tab_component.py 13/10, ui/species/overview_tab.py 13/2, ui/species/rail.py 4/1, ui/species/tomograms_tab.py 6/0, ui/tomo_gallery.py 9/3. The allow-list (check_look_literals.json, 88 files) was last written in 23ddc8a (2026-11-13). The overruns come from 7e65445 (2026-11-18, roadmap 59), 74a2cf3 and fa0feb3 (2026-11-19). Same result on HEAD and on the working tree. The script needs no arguments; `--update` rewrites the allow-list to today's counts (check_look_literals.py:8-9, 38-42).

Evidence:
- check_look_literals.py:1-56
- ./crboost python check_look_literals.py -> exit 1, 11 lines
- git --no-pager log -1 -- check_look_literals.json -> 23ddc8a 2026-11-13

Recommendation: Before CI goes on, the maintainer chooses between converting the 11 files to --cb-* variables and accepting today's counts with `./crboost python check_look_literals.py --update`, committed on its own. Then gate on it.

**CI-3 [medium, effort S] ruff format --check fails on 48 committed files; roadmap 39 S4 assumes it is a gate**

49 files would be reformatted (48 committed plus the untracked _vol_toy.py), and 382 are already formatted. The diff is 133 hunks. 64 removed lines are hand-aligned assignments or annotations (backend.py:253-264 `self.x          = ...`; every services/jobs/*.py Field block such as class3d.py `job_type       : JobType = ...`). Most of the rest is long Field(...) calls exploded one argument per line, because skip-magic-trailing-comma=true (ruff.toml) cannot keep them on one line past 120 columns. Affected areas: backend.py, check_boundaries.py, crboost.py, crboost_protocol.py, 20 services/jobs/*.py, 4 services/tilt_series/adapters/*.py, services/templating/*, several ui/ files, and 2 filterTilts files. CLAUDE.md names `ruff format` as the house style, and roadmap 40 records the job files as 'hand-aligned and were edited by hand' (docs/roadmaps/roadmap_40-sta-kitchen.md:1430-1431), so the drift is deliberate.

Evidence:
- ./crboost python -m ruff format --check . -> '49 files would be reformatted, 382 files already formatted' (HEAD export: 48)
- ruff_format_diff.txt: 4083 lines, 133 hunks
- ruff.toml:22-23 (skip-magic-trailing-comma = true)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:265-267

Recommendation: The maintainer chooses one of three. (a) Recommended: one commit that runs `ruff format .`, its sha in .git-blame-ignore-revs (GitHub's blame view honours it), then gate. (b) `[format] exclude = ["services/jobs/*.py", ...]` to keep the alignment and gate the rest. (c) Lint-only CI with no format gate. Whichever is chosen, it lands before the workflow does, or CI is red from day one.

**CI-4 [medium, effort S] The static tier is an install self-test, but 3 of its 5 tests are repo invariants that pass on a template conf**

Results on a template-derived conf with a throwaway HOME:
- test_every_config_key_is_read: passes. It is in effect the 'conf.template.yaml parses into Config with no unknown keys' check.
- test_qsub_renders: passes, and needs config/qsub.sh.
- test_agent_api_verb_table: passes.
- test_ood_portal_answers: skipped (no ood.url).
- test_preflight_checks_pass: fails with 13 preflight failures: DefaultProjectBase /path/to/your/projects missing, 10 container_path /path/to/*.sif, cisTEM bin_path, and slurm_defaults/supervisor_slurm partition 'gpu' not in sinfo. On ubuntu-latest it would also fail on sbatch/squeue/sacct/sinfo/apptainer missing from PATH (preflight.py:185-197).

What the tier needs:
- config/conf.yaml, because ConfigService raises FileNotFoundError without it (services/configs/config_service.py:378-384).
- config/qsub.sh, because the module constant QSUB_TEMPLATE points there (services/computing/slurm_service.py:124).
- A clean HOME, because ~/.crboost/conf.yaml is layered on top (config_service.py:36, 390-400).

`./crboost preflight` creates both files from the templates but then returns 1 (preflight.py:250-255), so CI needs `|| true` or a plain cp. ConfigService already takes an explicit path (config_service.py:372), so a repo test can load config/conf.template.yaml directly. Collection also imports tests/test_cluster.py, which works. Roadmap 39 S4 says 'No pytest: tests/ is the install self-test' (roadmap_39:267). That is now too strict.

Evidence:
- tests/test_static.py:14-83
- pytest.ini:1-8 (addopts -m 'not cluster')
- preflight.py:58-69, 107-183, 185-227, 250-255
- HEAD+template run: '1 failed, 3 passed, 1 skipped, 14 deselected in 5.24s'
- ./crboost test (real conf): '5 passed, 14 deselected, 1 warning in 5.09s', 6.9 s wall

Recommendation: Add a `site` marker to test_preflight_checks_pass and test_ood_portal_answers. CI then runs `pytest -m 'not cluster and not site'` and `./crboost test` stays unchanged. Better still, add a tests/test_repo.py that needs no site:
- ConfigService(config/conf.template.yaml) with no load_warnings
- QSUB_EXIT_MARKERS present in qsub.template.sh, plus the 4 placeholders and `exit $EXIT_CODE`
- every protocol bundle validates
- every job's get_tool_name() is a template tool or 'crboost'
- the verb table
- setup_app builds

Runs in under 10 s.

**CI-5 [medium, effort S] A headless serve smoke test on ubuntu-latest is feasible; the only hard input is conf.yaml**

What startup does, from the code:
- crboost.py surveys ports from /proc (services/port_survey.py:126-161, Linux only, fine on runners) and binds with claim(). An explicit --port skips the 'already running here' check (crboost.py:133) and does not write ~/.crboost/ports.json.
- setup_logging writes ~/.crboost/logs/crboost-<port>.log (services/log_setup.py:29, 235).
- read_or_create_secret writes ~/.crboost/token (services/server_auth.py:48-60).
- server_app.setup_app builds CryoBoostBackend (server_app.py:143), which loads config (backend.py:256). That is the only hard dependency: no conf.yaml means FileNotFoundError.
- Startup handlers: LoopWatchdog, workers.warm_up (one spawned worker, services/workers.py:193-197), then PipelineMonitor._discover_and_recover. It reads prefs from the empty HOME and the template's DefaultProjectBase, logs 'base path missing; skipping' (pipeline_monitor.py:138-140), and ticks only over loaded projects (none).

Why missing SLURM is harmless: SlurmService._run_command wraps create_subprocess_exec in except Exception and returns (False, '', msg) (slurm_service.py:486-500), so the landing status strip's sinfo/sacctmgr calls (ui/landing_status_strip.py:160, 202) degrade instead of crashing. supervisor_owner's squeue catches OSError (services/supervisor_owner.py:158-162).

Measured, without serving: setup_app(24399) on the template conf with a throwaway HOME built 75 routes in 5.1 s and created only ~/.crboost/{secret,nicegui}.

Caveats:
- The socket listens from claim() (crboost.py:147) before the app is up. Early requests sit in the backlog, so curl needs --max-time and a poll loop.
- With no tty the banner prints the token and URL in plain text to stdout (crboost.py:56-58, 178). In a public Actions log, mask it.
- With no --host it binds every interface, IPv6 dual-stack (port_survey.py:330-341). Use --host 127.0.0.1.
- The Google Fonts link in the page head (server_app.py:132-138) matters only to browsers.

Evidence:
- crboost.py:121-189
- server_app.py:60-182
- services/server_auth.py:21-24, 48-60
- services/scheduling_and_orchestration/pipeline_monitor.py:102-150
- services/computing/slurm_service.py:486-500
- scratch run: 'constructed FastAPI 75 routes; startup handlers: 1', WALL 5.11 s

Recommendation: A CI step:
1. export HOME=$(mktemp -d); cp the conf and qsub templates.
2. Start `CRBOOST_PLAIN_LOG=1 ./crboost --port 24300 --host 127.0.0.1 > srv.out 2>&1 &`.
3. Poll `curl -s -o /dev/null --max-time 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://127.0.0.1:24300/` until 401 (the token gate answers), at most 60 s.
4. tok=$(cat $HOME/.crboost/token); echo "::add-mask::$tok".
5. `curl -fsS -H "Authorization: Bearer $tok" .../` and grep for the page title.
6. `curl -fsS -H ... /api/v1/tools` and check the verb list.
7. Check that `/?token=$tok` gives a 307 with a Set-Cookie.
8. kill -TERM, wait; graceful shutdown is 5 s (server_app.py:39).
9. Fail if grep -q Traceback $HOME/.crboost/logs/crboost-24300.log.

About 1 minute on a runner, cold env included.

**CI-6 [medium, effort S] install.sh works unchanged on ubuntu-latest: cold build 5.8 s, no prompts, nothing written to HOME**

What it downloads:
- https://astral.sh/uv/0.13.7/install.sh piped to sh with UV_UNMANAGED_INSTALL into .uv/fetch-$$, then moved to .uv/bin/uv (install.sh:48-66). This changes no shell profile.
- CPython 3.11.18 from python-build-standalone via `uv python install --no-bin` (29.2 MiB; install.sh:156).
- 78 wheels from PyPI (uv.toml: only-binary, compile-bytecode).

Measured on the HEAD export in /tmp (local disk, this login node's network) under `env -i HOME=tmp PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`: 5.76 s wall, exit 0, the throwaway HOME still empty. Sizes: env 807 MB, .uv/cache 692 MB, .uv/python 90 MB, .uv/bin 36 MB. Roadmap 39 quotes about 35 s on /groups, so NFS dominates there.

Portability: there is no CBE-only assumption in the build path. It uses GNU `mv -T`, `find -mmin`, sha256sum and /proc (clean only); all are present on Ubuntu.

/bin/sh is bash on this host (ls -l /bin/sh -> bash) but dash on Ubuntu, so the `#!/bin/sh` install.sh and crboost have never run under dash. Reading them, I found no bashisms; bash -n passes.

Caching: UV_CACHE_DIR is forced to $here/.uv/cache inside run_uv (install.sh:43-46), so an Actions cache must target that path. A 692 MB cache to save and restore is not obviously faster than a 6-20 s cold fetch. If cached, use key `uv-${{ runner.os }}-${{ hashFiles('requirements.txt','uv.toml','.python-version') }}` on paths .uv/cache, .uv/python and .uv/bin. The repo's Actions cache allowance is 10 GB.

Evidence:
- install.sh:18-181
- uv.toml:1-24
- install_cold.err: 'Downloading cpython-3.11.18 (29.2MiB)', 'Resolved 78 packages in 417ms', 'Installed 78 packages in 1.39s', WALL 5.76 s
- du -sh head/.uv/*: bin 36M, cache 692M, env 807M, python 90M
- ls -l /bin/sh -> bash

Recommendation: In CI, call `./crboost ci`, which runs install.sh implicitly, so the real install path is tested on every push, under dash. Start with no cache, measure on the runner, and add the cache only if the cold build there passes about 30 s. Add `shellcheck -s sh install.sh crboost` on the runner, which ships ShellCheck.

**CI-7 [low, effort S] Lock, compile and import smoke pass; drop or narrow compileall for the local entry point**

`./install.sh --lock` on the HEAD export gives a byte-identical requirements.txt in 0.69 s (it needs PyPI), so 'requirements.in and requirements.txt agree' is a cheap gate: run `./crboost lock` and `git diff --exit-code requirements.txt`.

compileall passes but takes 20.6 s in the NFS checkout and 0.9 s on local disk. Its -x regex filters files, not directories, so it still walks .uv/env. On the export it compiled the whole venv (30 s) until '/\.uv/' was excluded.

The all-module import smoke (396 modules) takes 2 s. The only failures are the 4 torch modules. drivers/denoise_train.py:19 prints a [WARN] to stderr at import time; that is harmless.

Evidence:
- install.sh:123-129
- lock.out; diff requirements.head.txt requirements.txt -> identical
- compileall timings: 20.6 s (checkout), 0.75-0.90 s (export, .uv excluded), 30.4 s (export, .uv included)
- import_all.py: '396 modules, 4 failed to import, 2.0 s'

Recommendation: In `./crboost ci`, compile only `git ls-files '*.py'` (or rely on ruff, which reports syntax errors) and run the import-every-module smoke. Run the lock check in CI and behind --fast locally.

**CI-8 [low, effort S] About 21 broken relative links in docs; README.md is clean**

301 .md files hold 320 relative links; 25 are flagged. Real breakages: docs/README.md -> preprocessing-metrics-inventory.md; docs/miss-alignment.md -> ../container_defs/MISS_ALIGNMENT_INTEGRATION.md; docs/roadmaps/README.md (4, moved picking_ui roadmaps and roadmap_05); docs/roadmaps/completed/README.md (10); 3 picking_ui completed roadmaps; roadmap_registry-consolidation.md; roadmap_task-status-state-machine.md. False positives: code-like targets `sid`, `iid`, `name`, `"templates"`. The 174 tracked memory/*.md files are scanned too.

Evidence:
- scratch links.py output: '301 md files, 320 relative links, 25 broken'
- git ls-files memory | wc -l -> 174

Recommendation: Gate links in README.md, docs/*.md and the install docs. Run docs/roadmaps/** and memory/** warn-only, or exclude them. Fix the about 21 real links in one commit. A public release should not ship a docs index (docs/README.md) with dead links.

**CI-9 [medium, effort S] The Claude Code MCP forwarder is outside every check, and its dependencies are unpinned**

agent/crboost_mcp.py is a uv script (`# /// script`, dependencies mcp>=2.3,<3 and httpx>=0.28,<1) run with `uv run --script` (crboost:34-35). It cannot be imported in the server env (ModuleNotFoundError: mcp_types), no test touches it (test_agent_api_verb_table covers only the server side), and each first run resolves the dependencies afresh with no lock.

Evidence:
- agent/crboost_mcp.py:1-5
- crboost:34-35
- import agent.crboost_mcp -> ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'mcp_types'

Recommendation: Commit a script lock (`uv lock --script agent/crboost_mcp.py` writes agent/crboost_mcp.py.lock in recent uv; check this against 0.13.7). Add a CI step that runs the forwarder through the same launcher with a smoke flag, or that imports it via `uv run --script ... --help`, so a broken mcp 2.x minor release is caught before users hit it.

**CI-10 [medium, effort S] Free GitHub security features are off; 0 Dependabot alerts despite an old requests pin**

repos API security_and_analysis: dependabot_security_updates, secret_scanning, secret_scanning_push_protection, non-provider patterns and validity checks are all 'disabled'. vulnerability-alerts answers 204 (enabled), yet the alerts list is empty and the dependency-graph SBOM endpoint answers 404, so the graph is probably not populated. requirements.in pins requests==2.31.0 'to match the cluster's python-bundle module', a rationale roadmap 39 S6 made obsolete because the env is now uv's own Python. requests 2.31.0 predates the fixes for CVE-2024-35195 (2.32.0) and CVE-2024-47081 (2.32.4). main is not protected.

Evidence:
- gh api repos/:owner/:repo --jq .security_and_analysis -> all disabled
- gh api -i repos/:owner/:repo/vulnerability-alerts -> 204
- gh api repos/:owner/:repo/dependabot/alerts --jq length -> 0
- gh api repos/:owner/:repo/dependency-graph/sbom -> 404
- gh api repos/:owner/:repo/branches/main/protection -> 404 'Branch not protected'
- requirements.in:3-14

Recommendation: In repo settings, turn on the dependency graph, Dependabot alerts and security updates, secret scanning and push protection; all free for public repos. Add a weekly or PR `pip-audit -r requirements.txt` step, or rely on Dependabot once the graph sees requirements.txt. Protect main by requiring the CI check, which still allows the maintainer to push directly if branch rules are set that way.

**CI-11 [low, effort M] No container check is possible yet: defs are unlabelled, duplicated and in places carry placeholder labels**

Template tools: cistem, cryocare, easymode, imod, isonet, miss_alignment, pymol, pytom, relion, tilt_filter, warp_aretomo. container_defs/ holds 15 .def files: 4 tools have an old and a new def (isonet2.def plus isonet2-a266ff4_*_sm100.def; pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def plus pytom-match-pick-0.14.0_*; relion5.0_tomo.def plus relion-5.0.1-*; warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_* plus warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_*). easymode has no def. chimerax_artiax_GL.def and pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def have no %labels. Several labels are placeholders ('Author YourName', 'Author User', an AI assistant named as author), and one def carries a personal e-mail address in a public repo. No def has the `crboost.tool` / `crboost.def` labels roadmap 39 S5 specifies. Building GPU images on free runners is out of scope for this lens: no apptainer on the ubuntu-24.04 image, and the images are 1-13 GB.

Evidence:
- container_defs/*.def %labels blocks (grep)
- config/conf.template.yaml:364-414 (tools)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:271-278 (S5)

Recommendation: After S5 adds the labels, a static CI test parses container_defs/*.def. It requires exactly one def per container tool with a matching `crboost.tool` label, requires %labels, and forbids placeholder authors. Until then, a test can at least assert every template container tool has a def or an explicit 'no def' entry, which flags easymode.

**CI-12 [low, effort S] Roadmap 39 S4 and §6 are stale and need rewriting before they become the CI plan**

S4 says 'Fix the 6 E501 lines first'; ruff check is clean now. It lists `ruff format --check .` as a gate, which fails on 48 files (CI-3). It says 'No pytest', but 3 static tests can run (CI-4). §6 item 4 says CI 'also runs python -c "import main"', but main.py no longer exists; the entry points are crboost.py and server_app.py. S4 also does not mention check_look_literals.py, which postdates it.

Evidence:
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:262-269
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:489-490
- ls main.py -> No such file or directory

Recommendation: Supersede S4 with this lens's table in the deployment roadmap and point roadmap 39 S4 at it.

**CI-13 [low, effort S] A release archive would carry no version: code_version() is branch@sha from .git only**

code_version() reads .git without a git binary and returns `<branch>@<sha8>` (`@<sha8>` when detached, e.g. a checked-out tag), and None 'outside a git checkout, e.g. an unpacked release archive' (services/code_version.py:46-49). It is stamped into every project save (services/project_state.py:1208), the driver header (drivers/driver_base.py:55), the server log (crboost.py:163) and the API (api/handlers.py:97). A tag-based release flow, whether a GitHub release tarball or `git archive`, therefore stamps nothing, and a tag checkout stamps only a sha, never 'v0.x'. This matters for CI because a release job would need to write the version.

Evidence:
- services/code_version.py:46-66
- services/project_state.py:1208, 1322
- drivers/driver_base.py:55

Recommendation: If releases ship as archives: a VERSION file written by the release workflow, or `export-subst` in .gitattributes with `$Format:%(describe)$`, read by code_version() as a fallback. Otherwise add the tag (packed-refs/tags lookup) to the stamp. Leave this to the releases lens; the CI side is one step in the tag workflow.

### Facts

- ruff check . passes with 0 findings on HEAD and on the working tree (ruff 0.15.12 from the env) (verified; ./crboost python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!' (also on the git-archive HEAD export))
- ruff format --check: 48 committed files (49 with the untracked _vol_toy.py) would be reformatted, 382 already formatted (verified; ./crboost python -m ruff format --check . on the tree and on the HEAD export)
- check_boundaries.py is clean (R1-R5) in 2.2 s on local disk and 3.6 s on the NFS checkout (verified; ./crboost python check_boundaries.py, exit 0)
- check_look_literals.py fails on HEAD: 11 files over the allow-list, 76 literals in total; the allow-list was last written in 23ddc8a on 2026-11-13 (verified; ./crboost python check_look_literals.py exit 1; git log -- check_look_literals.json)
- compileall of the 432 tracked .py files passes; 0.9 s on local disk, 20.6 s in the NFS checkout because -x does not stop the walk into .uv (verified; python -m compileall -q -x ... on the tree and the export)
- import crboost / backend / server_app succeed with a throwaway HOME and either a template conf.yaml or no conf.yaml at all; 392 of 396 modules import, the 4 failures being filterTilts/deepLearning (torch) (verified; scratch import_all.py on the HEAD export under env -i)
- ./crboost test on this checkout: 5 passed, 14 deselected, 6.9 s wall (verified; ./crboost test -p no:cacheprovider -q)
- Static tier with a template-derived conf.yaml/qsub.sh and a throwaway HOME: 3 passed, 1 skipped (ood), 1 failed (test_preflight_checks_pass with 13 site failures) (verified; pytest on the HEAD export under env -i HOME=tmp)
- Cold ./install.sh in a fresh directory: 5.76 s wall, exit 0, nothing written to HOME, no prompts; env 807 MB, uv cache 692 MB, CPython 90 MB, uv binary 36 MB (verified; env -i HOME=tmp PATH=/usr/bin:/bin head/install.sh; du -sh head/.uv/*)
- ./install.sh --lock reproduces requirements.txt byte for byte in 0.69 s (verified; diff of requirements.txt before and after on the HEAD export)
- server_app.setup_app(port) constructs (75 routes) on the template conf with a throwaway HOME in 5.1 s without invoking SLURM; it was not served (verified; scratch python -c on the HEAD export)
- The copia-tutorial protocol bundle loads and validate_protocol returns no problems (verified; services.protocols.discovery.list_protocols + apply.validate_protocol on the HEAD export)
- QSUB_EXIT_MARKERS is present byte for byte in config/qsub.template.sh (verified; python check on the HEAD export; services/computing/slurm_service.py:126-128)
- /bin/sh on this login node is bash, so the #!/bin/sh install.sh and crboost have never run under dash (verified; ls -l /bin/sh -> bash)
- The repo has 0 Actions workflows, no .github directory, main is unprotected, and secret scanning, push protection and Dependabot security updates are disabled; vulnerability alerts are enabled but 0 alerts are listed and the dependency-graph SBOM endpoint answers 404 (verified; gh api reads: actions/workflows, branches/main/protection, repo security_and_analysis, vulnerability-alerts (204), dependabot/alerts, dependency-graph/sbom)
- Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free for public repositories; the Actions cache allowance is 10 GB per repository (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions)
- The ubuntu-24.04 runner image ships ShellCheck 0.9.0 and has no apptainer or singularity; its tool cache includes Python 3.11.18 (verified; https://github.com/actions/runner-images/blob/main/images/ubuntu/Ubuntu2404-Readme.md)
- /bin/sh on Ubuntu runners is dash (likely; general Ubuntu default (dash as /bin/sh since 6.10); not checked on a runner)
- requests 2.31.0 is affected by CVE-2024-35195 (fixed 2.32.0) and CVE-2024-47081 (fixed 2.32.4) (likely; https://github.com/psf/requests/security/advisories (not fetched this session))
- uv can lock a PEP 723 script with `uv lock --script <file>`, writing <file>.lock (likely; https://docs.astral.sh/uv/guides/scripts/ (not fetched this session; not checked against uv 0.13.7))

### Open questions

- ruff format: reformat the repo once and add .git-blame-ignore-revs, losing the hand-aligned Field blocks in 20 services/jobs files; exclude those files from formatting; or keep format out of CI? Recommended: reformat once, then gate.
- check_look_literals: convert the 11 over-limit ui files, or accept today's counts with --update and gate from now on? Recommended: --update now in its own commit, then gate and let the allow-list shrink as files convert.
- Static tests in CI: a `site` marker on test_preflight_checks_pass and test_ood_portal_answers, or a separate tests/test_repo.py that reads the templates directly? Recommended: tests/test_repo.py, leaving `./crboost test` as the install self-test.
- Should the serve smoke (start, curl with token, stop) run on every push or only on PRs and main? Recommended: every push to main and every PR; it is about 1 minute.
- Doc links: gate everything, or only README.md + docs/*.md with roadmaps and memory/ warn-only? Recommended: gate README and the install/user docs, warn on roadmaps; also decide separately whether memory/ (174 files) belongs in the public repo.
- Is the requests==2.31.0 / numpy / pandas / scipy pin to the old cluster modules still wanted, now that the env is uv's own Python? Recommended: no; let Dependabot or pip-audit drive bumps, starting with requests>=2.32.4.
- Should CI cache the 692 MB uv cache? Recommended: no cache at first; measure the cold build on the runner, cache .uv/{cache,python,bin} keyed on requirements.txt + uv.toml + .python-version only if it passes about 30 s.

## recon:ci_release_research: Free CI/CD, releases, versioning and project QoL for a public GitHub Python app deployed from a git checkout (GitHub free tier, Dependabot/Renovate fit with the uv-compiled lock, release practice, docs hosting, update notices)

Nothing deployment-shaped exists on GitHub yet. There are no workflows, tags or releases, no branch or tag rules, and no Pages site. Secret scanning, push protection, Dependabot security updates, private vulnerability reporting and CodeQL are all off. GitHub's dependency graph sees 0 manifests, so Dependabot alerts currently show "0" without having scanned anything. Meanwhile OSV lists known advisories against 5 of the 78 locked pins, including the web stack itself: nicegui 3.0.3, starlette 0.48.0, requests 2.31.0 and pillow 12.0.0. Every CI gate in roadmap 39 S4 is free on a public repo, because standard runners are free and unlimited. Run today on tracked files, `ruff check`, `check_boundaries.py` and `py_compile` are clean, but `ruff format --check` would fail on 48 files, so a one-off format commit has to come before CI. The install self-test can partly run in CI. With the template conf and a clean HOME, 3 tests pass and 1 is skipped in 2.7 s once the SLURM preflight test is deselected. Re-running `uv pip compile` with the pinned uv reproduces the lock exactly in about 1 s, which makes a cheap lock-in-sync gate. For dependency updates, Dependabot's `uv` ecosystem fits the requirements.in→requirements.txt layout and keeps the header, but it compiles without uv.toml, so the lock-sync gate is what keeps it honest. Renovate would reject our lock header outright (`--config-file`, `-o`). Commits go straight to main (247 commits and 2 merges in the last 30 days, 24 PRs ever) and are free-form. That rules out release-please and GitHub's PR-based auto-notes; a hand-written CHANGELOG fed by HOTFIXES.md, plus "upgrade notes" the release workflow computes, fits instead. For versioning, CalVer `vYYYY.M.P` with a VERSION file and a `stable` branch that sites `git pull` keeps today's update habit and makes age obvious. Monthly minors plus patches on demand suits 1–2 maintainers. Free-tier limits that matter for containers: release assets are capped at 2 GiB per file (only napari, chimerax and imod SIFs fit), runners have 14 GB SSD, 16 GB RAM and a 6 h job limit, and apptainer is not preinstalled. GHCR public storage and bandwidth are free, but I could not verify whether it caps layer size. For update notices, the unauthenticated GitHub API (60 requests/hour per IP) is already partly used up on the shared headnode IP. Use the `/releases/latest` redirect or the Atom feed once a day per install, opt-in, or read refs after the admin's `git fetch` with no network at all.

### Findings

**CIR-1 [blocker, effort S] No CI at all, and a format gate would fail on day one (48 files)**

There is no .github/ directory and the repo has 0 Actions workflows. I ran the S4 gates on tracked .py files with the lock's ruff 0.15.12, honouring ruff.toml excludes. `ruff check`: All checks passed. `check_boundaries.py`: clean. `py_compile` over every tracked .py: exit 0. `ruff format --check`: 48 files would be reformatted (382 already formatted). `check_look_literals.py` exits 1 with about 11 offender lines (ui/species/rail.py, ui/species/tomograms_tab.py, ui/tomo_gallery.py, ...). The 6 E501 errors roadmap 39 §1 lists are gone. The S4 plan in roadmap 39 still holds; it just was never built.

Evidence:
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:262-269 (S4 plan: lint.yml with ruff check, ruff format --check, check_boundaries, compileall)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:489 (CI also runs an import smoke)
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/actions/workflows -> total_count 0
- git ls-files '*.py' | xargs ruff format --check --no-cache --force-exclude -> '48 files would be reformatted, 382 files already formatted'
- git ls-files '*.py' | xargs ruff check --no-cache --force-exclude -> 'All checks passed!'
- python check_boundaries.py -> 'Boundaries clean (R1..R5)'
- python check_look_literals.py -> exit 1, 11 lines
- ruff.toml:1-3 (line-length 120, target py311, excludes)

Recommendation: (1) Make one mechanical `ruff format .` commit and list its SHA in `.git-blame-ignore-revs`, which GitHub's blame view honours. (2) Add `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, triggered on push and pull_request, with `permissions: contents: read`, a concurrency group with cancel-in-progress, actions pinned by SHA, and runner `ubuntu-24.04` pinned rather than `ubuntu-latest`. Jobs: `lint` runs ruff check, ruff format --check, check_boundaries.py and `python -m compileall -q`. `lock` copies requirements.txt, runs `uv pip compile requirements.in -o requirements.txt` with the pinned uv and uv.toml, then `git diff --exit-code requirements.txt`. `selftest` copies the conf/qsub templates, then runs `pytest --deselect tests/test_static.py::test_preflight_checks_pass` with HOME set to `$RUNNER_TEMP`. `smoke` runs `python -c 'import server_app, backend, drivers.driver_base'`. `shell` runs shellcheck (preinstalled on the runner) over install.sh, crboost, config/qsub.template.sh and containers/**/*.sh, plus actionlint. Run check_look_literals.py as a non-blocking step (continue-on-error) until its offenders are fixed. (3) Add one local entry point, for example a `./crboost check`, that runs exactly the CI gates, so CI and the maintainer never disagree.

**SEC-1 [high, effort S] Every free security toggle is off, the dependency graph is blind, and the web stack has open advisories**

Repo settings read via the API: secret_scanning disabled, secret_scanning_push_protection disabled, dependabot_security_updates disabled, private-vulnerability-reporting enabled=false, CodeQL default setup state 'not-configured' (languages detected: python, javascript-typescript). Dependabot alerts are on (the vulnerability-alerts endpoint returns 204), but the GraphQL dependency graph returns totalCount 0 manifests, so the alerts look at nothing. I sent all 78 lock pins to the OSV querybatch API on 2026-11-19. Flagged: nicegui==3.0.3, 28 OSV entries (4 HIGH, 10 MODERATE; GHSA and PYSEC aliases double-count), fixed by 3.12.0 at the latest. starlette==0.48.0, 12 entries (3 HIGH), fixed by 1.3.1 at the latest. requests==2.31.0, 6 entries, fixed by 2.33.0 at the latest. pillow==12.0.0, about 100 entries (5 CRITICAL, 37 HIGH), fixed by 13.0.0 at the latest. biopython==1.86, 2 entries. The token gate (services/server_auth.py:70) limits exposure, but a public release on a shared headnode should not ship known-vulnerable web-server deps. requirements.in:10 ties requests, numpy, pandas and scipy to the cluster's 2023 modules, a reason that predates the self-contained environment (S6).

Evidence:
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo> .security_and_analysis -> secret_scanning disabled, push_protection disabled, dependabot_security_updates disabled
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/private-vulnerability-reporting -> {"enabled":false}
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/code-scanning/default-setup -> state not-configured
- gh api graphql dependencyGraphManifests -> totalCount 0
- OSV querybatch (https://api.osv.dev/v1/querybatch) over requirements.txt pins -> nicegui 28, starlette 12, requests 6, pillow ~100, biopython 2
- requirements.txt:98 nicegui==3.0.3; :125 pillow==12.0.0; :175 requests==2.31.0; :191 starlette==0.48.0
- requirements.in:3,8,10,17

Recommendation: Turn on in Settings → Security, all free for public repos: secret scanning plus push protection, private vulnerability reporting (paired with a SECURITY.md that names the channel), Dependabot security updates, and CodeQL default setup. Default setup covers Python, the JS in static/, and GitHub Actions workflows once they exist. Find out why the dependency graph shows 0 manifests; the org-level setting is the likely cause. Add a weekly non-blocking `pip-audit --no-deps --disable-pip -r requirements.txt` job; it needs no install because every line is pinned. `uv audit` is preview-only and reads uv.lock, so it does not fit this lock. Before the December tag, bump the patch-level fixes (requests, pillow, starlette through fastapi) and schedule nicegui 3.0.3→3.12.x as its own test pass, since the UI depends heavily on NiceGUI internals.

**DEP-1 [high, effort S] Dependabot's `uv` ecosystem fits the lock; Renovate would reject the lock header**

I read the dependabot-core source. The `uv` ecosystem treats every `.in` file as a compile input (uv/lib/dependabot/uv/file_updater.rb, pip_compile_files) and maps requirements.in to requirements.txt by name when the header has no `--output-file` (compile_file_updater.rb:584-600). It reruns `pyenv exec uv pip compile <options> -P <dep>==<ver>` (lines 125-140) and restores the original header (replace_header_with_original, line 341). It keeps `# via` annotations: `--no-annotate` is added only when the file has none (line 526). It rebuilds options from the file's content, not from uv.toml, and the fetcher does not appear to fetch uv.toml (ecosystem_specific_files covers readme, license, uv.lock, workspace and version files). So its compile skips `python-platform` and `only-binary`, and `required-version ==0.13.7` does not apply to it. Renovate's pip-compile manager (lib/modules/manager/pip-compile/common.ts) throws 'Option ... not supported (yet)' for any flag outside its allow-list. Our header `uv --config-file uv.toml pip compile requirements.in -o requirements.txt` contains `--config-file` and `-o`, both outside it. Dependabot cannot parse Apptainer .def files: its `docker` ecosystem is for Dockerfiles, and has version updates only.

Evidence:
- requirements.txt:2 header '#    uv --config-file uv.toml pip compile requirements.in -o requirements.txt'
- https://github.com/dependabot/dependabot-core/blob/main/uv/lib/dependabot/uv/file_updater/compile_file_updater.rb (lines 125-140, 341, 509-543, 584-600)
- https://github.com/dependabot/dependabot-core/blob/main/uv/lib/dependabot/uv/file_updater.rb (pip_compile_files = *.in)
- https://github.com/renovatebot/renovate/blob/main/lib/modules/manager/pip-compile/common.ts (allowedOptions.uv; throwForUnknownOption)
- https://docs.renovatebot.com/modules/manager/pip-compile/
- https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/ecosystems-supported-by-dependabot/supported-ecosystems-and-repositories (uv row; docker: version updates only)
- uv.toml:5,8,13-17 (required-version, python-preference, python-version, python-platform, only-binary)

Recommendation: Add `.github/dependabot.yml` with two entries. (a) `package-ecosystem: uv`, directory '/', `schedule: monthly`, one `groups` entry so each month gives one lock PR, `cooldown: default-days: 7`, and a separate group for security updates, which ignore cooldown. (b) `package-ecosystem: github-actions`, monthly, grouped; it updates SHA-pinned actions. The CI `lock` gate recompiles with the real uv.toml and pinned uv, so any Dependabot PR that resolved differently fails; the maintainer then runs `./crboost lock` on that branch. Do not adopt Renovate unless the header changes. Track container base images (.def `From:` lines) by hand at each monthly release.

**REL-1 [high, effort S] No version identity: 0 tags, 0 releases, no VERSION; sites track main**

`code_version()` returns `<branch>@<sha8>` read straight from .git without a git binary. It is stamped into project saves, driver run.out headers, the server log and get_server_info. Nothing names a release, though. The install docs clone main and update with `git pull`, so the collaborating site runs whatever main holds at pull time. Roadmap 39 §6.2 notes that a pull mid-campaign changes jobs already queued. Separate version axes already exist: SCHEMA_VERSION (3, 14) for project files and API_VERSION = 1 for the agent API. A release number would tie them together.

Evidence:
- services/code_version.py:47-62 (branch@sha only; None outside a git checkout)
- crboost.py:163; drivers/driver_base.py:55; services/project_state.py:1208; api/handlers.py:97 (code_version stamped)
- services/project_state.py:64 SCHEMA_VERSION = (3, 14); api/handlers.py:44 API_VERSION = 1
- docs/installation.md:15 (git clone, default branch); docs/installation.md:212-228 and README.md:124 (update = git pull)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:476-480 (§6.2 queued jobs run code on disk)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_23-per-user-servers.md:235-241 (stage 6: 'There is no version string in the code')
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/releases, /tags -> none

Recommendation: Use CalVer `YYYY.M.PATCH` with an unpadded month, tagged `v2026.12.0`. It is PEP 440 valid and sorts correctly with `git tag --sort=v:refname`. Add a one-line VERSION file changed only in release commits, and extend code_version() to return `2026.12.0 (stable@abc12345)`, so every existing stamp names the release. Keep a `stable` branch that only fast-forwards to release tags: sites clone with `-b stable` and keep updating with `git pull` (code_version then reads `stable@…`), and main stays the lab's dev line. Tags are annotated and SSH-signed (`git tag -s`). Turn on immutable releases in repo settings so a published tag and its assets cannot move. Don't use moving tags; the stable branch does that job.

**REL-2 [medium, effort M] Release notes: the direct-to-main, free-form workflow rules out release-please and GitHub auto-notes**

There have been 913 commits since 2025-09-22, 247 of them in the last 30 days with only 2 merges, and 24 merged PRs ever. Subjects are free-form ('Roadmap NN:', 'H31:', 'ui:', 'tilt filter:', ...), not Conventional Commits. release-please requires Conventional Commits and is SemVer-only, though `Release-As` can force a version. GitHub's auto-generated notes list merged PRs, so here they would be nearly empty. git-cliff can parse arbitrary subjects with regex commit_parsers, but the output would be a raw list. HOTFIXES.md already has ~31 H-entries written for humans, the natural 'Fixed' source. What the collaborating site needs most per release is upgrade work, and that can be computed by diffing between tags: container_defs/ changes (rebuild needed), config/conf.template.yaml and config/qsub.template.sh changes (installation.md says new settings must be copied by hand), requirements.txt (new env build), SCHEMA_VERSION and API_VERSION.

Evidence:
- git log --format=%s: 913 commits; 247 since 2026-10-19 with 2 merges
- gh pr list --state all -> 24 MERGED
- docs/roadmaps/HOTFIXES.md (### H1..H31 entries)
- docs/installation.md:224-227 ('a setting the template gained since you installed has to be copied over by hand')
- https://github.com/googleapis/release-please (assumes Conventional Commits; follows SemVer)
- https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/automatically-generated-release-notes (lists merged pull requests)
- https://git-cliff.org/docs/ (regex-powered custom parsers)

Recommendation: Keep a hand-written CHANGELOG.md in Keep a Changelog form (Added, Changed, Fixed, plus a mandatory 'Upgrading' block) with an [Unreleased] section filled during the month; H-entries feed Fixed. Add `.github/workflows/release.yml`, triggered on tag push `v*`. It checks that the tag equals VERSION and that CHANGELOG has the section, reruns the CI gates, then appends computed upgrade notes: `git diff --name-only <prev>..<tag> -- container_defs/ config/*.template.* requirements.txt` plus any SCHEMA_VERSION or API_VERSION change. It then runs `gh release create --notes-file` and attaches SHA256SUMS. Optionally it fast-forwards `stable`, or the maintainer pushes it by hand. git-cliff is optional, only to draft the Unreleased list.

**REL-3 [medium, effort S] Cadence for a 1–2 maintainer project, and a December timeline**

Practice elsewhere in the field. RELION: version branches plus tags (5.0.0 2024-12, 5.0.1 2025-09, 5.1.0 pre 2026-03); its docs recommend git clone so updating is easy. Warp: GitHub releases of dev tags about monthly (v2.0.0dev37–41, Apr–Sep 2026). napari: SemVer 0.x with rc tags (v0.10.0 2026-10-30). Open OnDemand: two maintained lines with patches roughly monthly (v4.2.6 2026-11-03, v4.1.7 2026-08-31). cryoSPARC: `cryosparcm update` with dated patch builds (v4.7.1+251124). Active months here run 150–215 commits.

Evidence:
- gh api repos/3dem/relion/releases; warpem/warp; napari/napari; OSC/ondemand (tag names and dates above)
- https://relion.readthedocs.io/en/latest/Installation.html (clone so updating is easy)
- https://cryosparc.com/updates/v4.7.1+251124.md
- https://calver.org/ (pip YY.MINOR.MICRO, Home Assistant YYYY.M.MICRO)
- git log --date=short: 2026-08 203, 2026-10 215, 2026-11 150 commits

Recommendation: Every push: ci.yml, about 2–4 min. Weekly cron (Monday) plus workflow_dispatch: a fresh install.sh with no cache on ubuntu-24.04 and in an EL container (almalinux:10 or rockylinux:9, to look like the headnodes), pip-audit, and a lychee link check of README and docs/*.md, all non-blocking and opening an issue on failure. Monthly: Dependabot's grouped lock PR lands in the first days; `YYYY.M.0` is tagged in the second week after a week of lab use on main; stable fast-forwards; the collaborating site pulls. As needed: `YYYY.M.N` patches from main for HOTFIX entries that change results or block runs, and within a week for HIGH or CRITICAL advisories in the web stack. December: week of 23 Nov, format commit, ci.yml green, security toggles, dependabot.yml, repo cleanup (other lens). Week of 30 Nov: VERSION, CHANGELOG, CITATION.cff, templates, release.yml, `v2026.12.0rc1` for the collaborating site to install from a fresh clone. Mid-December: `v2026.12.0` plus the Zenodo DOI. January: `v2027.1.0`.

**UPD-1 [medium, effort M] Telling a running instance that an update exists: local half unbuilt, upstream half needs a quota-safe design**

Roadmap 23 stage 6 already specifies the local half: the server compares the commit it started from with the install's HEAD every 15 min and shows an 'update installed — Restart' banner. It is not built. The upstream half (a newer release exists) needs an outbound call. Unauthenticated REST API calls are limited to 60 per hour per IP. A probe from this headnode on 2026-11-19 showed x-ratelimit-remaining 37 of 60, so the shared egress IP already eats into the quota, and per-user servers each calling the API would hit it. `https://github.com/<o>/<r>/releases/latest` returns a 302 to the latest tag, and `/releases.atom` returns 200 application/atom+xml; neither counts against the REST quota. `releases/latest` excludes drafts and prereleases. Common practice differs: JupyterLab asks consent before fetching news or update info (opt-in); Grafana's check_for_updates is on by default and can be turned off. install.sh already needs outbound HTTPS (uv from astral.sh, PyPI, CPython downloads), so outbound access is usually there; the question is whether the check is welcome.

Evidence:
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_23-per-user-servers.md:235-241, 283-285 (stage 6 banner)
- curl -sSI https://api.github.com/repos/<public repo>/releases/latest -> x-ratelimit-limit: 60, x-ratelimit-remaining: 37
- curl https://github.com/<public repo>/releases/latest -> 302 to /releases/tag/<tag>; /releases.atom -> 200 application/atom+xml
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/limits (unauthenticated 60 requests/hour per IP)
- https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases#get-the-latest-release (non-prerelease, non-draft)
- https://jupyterlab.readthedocs.io/en/latest/privacy_policies.html (fetch only after the user clicks Yes)
- install.sh:57 (curl astral.sh)

Recommendation: Build stage 6's local banner first: no network, and it covers 'admin pulled, restart'. For upstream there are two levels. (a) No network, the default: read `refs/remotes/origin/stable` and the tags from .git after the admin's `git fetch` (code_version already parses refs and packed-refs) and show 'v2027.1.0 available — ask your admin'. (b) Opt-in `update_check: true` in conf.yaml: one request a day per install, not per user server, to the `/releases/latest` redirect, cached in a shared file. Never send the version or any identifier.

**QOL-1 [medium, effort S] Community and project files missing (health 50%), repo metadata stale**

The community profile reports health_percentage 50: readme and license present; code_of_conduct, contributing, issue_template and pull_request_template absent. There is no SECURITY.md, CITATION.cff, CHANGELOG.md or CODEOWNERS. The repo description reads 'A headnode-centric implementation of cryoboost', with no topics and no homepage. Discussions are off, the wiki is on, there are 11 labels, 0 milestones and 43 open issues (roadmaps filed as issues). There is no branch protection and there are no rulesets. Actions: allowed_actions 'all', sha_pinning_required false.

Evidence:
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/community/profile -> health_percentage 50; code_of_conduct/contributing/issue_template/pull_request_template false
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo> -> description, topics [], homepage '', has_discussions false, has_wiki true, open_issues_count 43
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/rulesets -> []; branches/main/protection -> 404 not protected
- gh api repos/<org>/<repo>/actions/permissions -> allowed_actions all, sha_pinning_required false

Recommendation: Add CONTRIBUTING.md (how to run `./crboost check`, the commit style, and that main is dev while stable is for sites), CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md (Contributor Covenant), and SECURITY.md (private vulnerability reporting, supported = latest release). Add issue forms: a bug form that asks for code_version, `./crboost preflight` output, the cluster's scheduler and Apptainer version, and the tool and container. Add an install-help form and a config.yml linking Discussions. Add a short PR template and CITATION.cff. Turn on Discussions (Q&A for other sites); turn off the wiki unless it is used, so docs live in one place. Rewrite the description, add topics such as cryo-electron-tomography, slurm, apptainer, relion, warp, and set the homepage once a docs URL exists. Add a tag ruleset on `v*` (no deletion, no update) and a `stable` ruleset (no force-push, no deletion). Set the Actions policy to require SHA pinning.

**ZEN-1 [medium, effort S] Zenodo archives every release permanently, so clean the repo before the first release**

With the GitHub integration, each published release is downloaded as a zip and published as a new version under one concept DOI. Records cannot be deleted, and a repo cannot be attached to an existing concept DOI. If both .zenodo.json and CITATION.cff exist, Zenodo uses only .zenodo.json; GitHub's 'Cite this repository' box reads CITATION.cff (APA and BibTeX). For an org-owned repo, an org admin must approve the Zenodo OAuth app. Today the tracked tree includes memory/ (174 files) and docs/reports (64 files), which would be archived for good in the first DOI.

Evidence:
- git ls-files | awk -F/ '{print $1}' -> memory 174, docs 166 (reports 64, roadmaps 93)
- https://help.zenodo.org/docs/github/describe-software/ ('only the .zenodo.json metadata will be used')
- https://support.zenodo.org/help/en-gb/24-github-integration/96-how-does-a-citation-cff-file-affect-metadata-of-my-github-release
- https://help.zenodo.org/docs/github/enable-repository
- https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-citation-files

Recommendation: Order: repo cleanup (removal lens) → CITATION.cff only, no .zenodo.json, with the PI's author list and ORCIDs → enable Zenodo for the repo (needs an org admin) → tag v2026.12.0. Put the concept DOI badge in the README. Test the flow once on sandbox.zenodo.org with a throwaway repo, because published records cannot be removed.

**DOC-1 [low, effort M] Docs hosting: GitHub's markdown rendering is enough for December; Zensical on Pages later**

There are 10 reference docs in docs/*.md; the rest of docs/ is roadmaps (93 files) and reports (64), which are internal records. Tool status: mkdocs-material 9.8.1 (2026-11-07) is classified 'Development Status :: 7 - Inactive' and its release notes say end of life May 5, 2027. MkDocs itself last released 1.6.1 on 2024-08-30. Zensical, the successor from the same authors, is 0.1.2 (2026-11-17), Beta, reads mkdocs.yml, and uses a mike fork for versioning until native versioning ships. Read the Docs is free for OSS with one non-tracking ad and builds versions from branches and tags natively. GitHub Pages: 1 GB site, 100 GB/month soft bandwidth, a 10 builds/hour soft limit that does not apply to custom Actions workflows, and a 10-minute deploy timeout.

Evidence:
- docs/README.md:1-17 (reference docs vs roadmaps/reports)
- PyPI JSON 2026-11-19: zensical 0.1.2 Beta; mkdocs-material 9.8.1 Inactive; mkdocs 1.6.1 (2024-08-30); mike 2.2.0
- https://github.com/squidfunk/mkdocs-material/releases (EOL May 5, 2027)
- https://zensical.org/docs/compatibility/mkdocs/mike/
- https://docs.github.com/en/pages/getting-started-with-github-pages/github-pages-limits

Recommendation: For December: no docs site. Curate README.md (quick start, then a link to docs/installation.md) and docs/README.md, and move internal roadmaps and reports out of the published path or mark them clearly. After December, if a site is wanted: Zensical on GitHub Pages through an Actions workflow, nav limited to the reference docs, one 'latest' version from the stable branch. Skip mike until a second supported release line exists. RTD is the fallback if per-tag versioned docs are wanted without maintaining mike.

**UV-1 [medium, effort S] uv in CI and install.sh: test the real installer; uv is fetched without a checksum**

install.sh downloads uv with `curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/$want/install.sh | sh` and checks only the version string afterwards; no sha256 is pinned. The real product test is install.sh on a fresh machine with no modules loaded (roadmap 39 §4 item 1); a GitHub runner gives that for free each week. astral-sh/setup-uv (examples pinned at v10.3.3) reads `required-version` from uv.toml when `version` is not given, caches by requirements files, and can verify checksums (`checksum`, `require-checksums`). ubuntu-latest is moving to Ubuntu 26.04 in the Oct 19–Nov 19, 2026 window; uv's managed CPython needs glibc ≥ 2.17 (uv.toml comment). Measured: the lock recompiles with identical pins in about 1 s with an empty cache.

Evidence:
- install.sh:48-65 (ensure_uv; curl | sh at :57; version check only)
- uv.toml:5 required-version '==0.13.7'; uv.toml:7-8 managed Python, glibc 2.17
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:440-450 (§4 fresh-clone pass)
- https://github.com/astral-sh/setup-uv (reads required-version from uv.toml; checksum inputs)
- https://github.com/actions/runner-images/issues/14748 and https://github.blog/changelog/2026-09-17-ubuntu-26-generally-available-and-latest-migration/
- Measured: uv pip compile into a copy of requirements.txt -> pins identical, 0.997 s

Recommendation: In CI, run `./install.sh` itself rather than setup-uv plus pip, with actions/cache on `.uv/cache` and `.uv/python` keyed on hash(requirements.txt, .python-version, uv.toml); the weekly job runs uncached. Pin `ubuntu-24.04` explicitly and add an EL container job. In install.sh, download the uv release tarball for x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu directly from uv's GitHub release and check it against a sha256 committed next to required-version, instead of curl | sh. This also gives sites behind a proxy one URL to allow.

**CT-1 [medium, effort S] Free-tier limits that decide how containers can be served (input for the container lens)**

Release assets: each file must be under 2 GiB, up to 1000 assets per release, with no total size or bandwidth limit. Of the SIFs, only napari (0.5 GB), chimerax (1.0 GB) and imod (1.2 GB) fit; relion (13.4), warp_aretomo (12.7), isonet2 (12.4), pymol (8.9), pytom (7.9), miss_alignment (7.6), cryocare (6.8), easymode (5.8) and tilt_filter (4.1) do not. Standard public-repo runners: 4 vCPU, 16 GB RAM, 14 GB SSD documented, 6 h per job, 20 concurrent jobs on the Free plan. Building a 12–13 GB SIF (plus its sandbox or layers) on one exceeds the documented disk. Apptainer is not preinstalled on the Ubuntu 24.04 or 26.04 images (Docker and Podman are). The Actions cache is 10 GB per repo. GitHub Packages: public packages are free (storage and transfer), and Container registry storage and bandwidth are 'currently free' with a month's notice before any change. I found no documented GHCR per-layer cap; one report shows GHCR rejecting request chunks over 4 MiB, which ORAS clients handle by chunking. The weights filterTilts/*.pth (45 MB) would fit as a release asset if the licence allows (D7).

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases ('Each file ... must be under 2 GiB'; 1000 assets; no total or bandwidth limit)
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners (4 CPU / 16 GB / 14 GB SSD, free and unlimited on public repos)
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/limits (6 h job; Free 20 concurrent, 5 macOS; cache 10 GB per repo)
- gh api actions/runner-images Ubuntu2404-Readme.md and Ubuntu2604-Readme.md -> shellcheck, Docker, Podman listed; no apptainer
- https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-packages (public packages free; container registry currently free)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:271-278 (S5 %labels plus weights sha256, not built)

Recommendation: Don't plan on Release assets or free runners for the big SIFs. Options for the container lens: ORAS push of SIFs to GHCR (test one 13 GB push before committing to it), or OCI or Docker images on GHCR that sites `apptainer pull`. Either way, CI on free runners can at least lint the .def files and build the small images (imod, chimerax, napari) to keep the definitions honest. Each release's notes should list which container_defs changed since the previous tag, the 'rebuild or pull' list, which S5's %labels make checkable by preflight.

**QOL-2 [low, effort S] pre-commit.ci and OpenSSF Scorecard: use Scorecard once as a checklist, skip pre-commit.ci**

pre-commit.ci is always free for open-source repos, with weekly autoupdate and autofix PRs. Its hooks pin their own ruff rev, though, a second source of the ruff version next to the lock's `ruff==0.15.12`, which requirements.in deliberately makes the only one so checkouts don't reformat each other. The OpenSSF Scorecard action is free for public repos and publishes a badge with `publish_results: true` and `id-token: write`. With direct pushes and no branch protection it will score low on Code-Review and Branch-Protection, by design for this team size.

Evidence:
- requirements.in:30-31 (ruff pinned in the one lock: 'one version for every checkout')
- https://pre-commit.ci/ ('always be free for open source repositories'; autoupdate weekly by default)
- https://github.com/ossf/scorecard-action ('free for all public repositories')

Recommendation: Skip pre-commit.ci. If local hooks are wanted, use a `repo: local` pre-commit config that calls `./crboost python -m ruff`, so the version comes from the lock, and run the same gates in CI. Run Scorecard once (CLI or a workflow_dispatch) to harvest the cheap items: token permissions, pinned actions, SECURITY.md, signed releases. Add the weekly action and badge only if the score is worth showing.

**QOL-3 [low, effort S] Licence scan is a one-off, not a CI gate; containers are the real licence question**

I scanned the metadata of the 78 installed distributions. The only non-permissive entries are docutils (multi-licensed Public Domain, BSD or GPL), bidict and certifi (MPL-2.0), and orjson (MPL-2.0 AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT)). There is no strong copyleft in the Python environment; it carries no torch, and that stays inside the tilt_filter container. The tool images bundle third-party software whose redistribution terms differ: some are GPL, some research-only. Publishing images is a licensing decision, not something CI can settle.

Evidence:
- importlib.metadata over .uv/env/3.11.18-8febfdb40744 -> 78 dists; flagged docutils, bidict, certifi, orjson
- requirements.in:23-24 (no torch; tilt filter in its container)
- LICENSE (Apache-2.0)

Recommendation: Record the scan result once in docs (or regenerate with pip-licenses at release time as a release asset). For containers, the container lens should list each image's redistribution terms before anything is pushed to a public registry.

**QOL-4 [low, effort S] Public-facing leftovers in .gitignore and the repo root**

.gitignore names a collaborating-site file (`!munich_handoff_note.md`), tools (`.abacusai`, `promptbuilder.py`), and odd patterns (`--git-common-dir`, `**env`), all visible to the public. The repo root has a tracked lab-internal memory/ tree, and check_look_literals.* sits beside the product scripts. This belongs mainly to the removal lens; it is listed here because the first release and Zenodo archive freeze whatever the tree holds.

Evidence:
- .gitignore:9 '**env'; :24-25 '--git-common-dir'; :43 'promptbuilder.py'; :49 '!munich_handoff_note.md'; :51-52 '.abacusai'
- git ls-files | awk -F/ '{print $1}' -> memory 174

Recommendation: Tidy .gitignore (drop site and tool names; keep generic patterns), move dev-only checkers under scripts/ or tools/, and settle memory/ before tagging (removal lens).

### Facts

- Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free and unlimited on public repositories. Public-repo specs: Linux x64 and arm64 4 CPU / 16 GB RAM / 14 GB SSD (ubuntu-22.04, 24.04, 26.04 and -arm), ubuntu-slim 1 CPU / 5 GB, Windows 4/16/14, macOS arm64 3 CPU (M1) / 7 GB. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners (fetched 2026-11-19))
- Actions limits: 6 h per GitHub-hosted job, 35 days per workflow run, 256 matrix jobs, Free plan 20 concurrent jobs (5 macOS), 10 GB cache per repository, unauthenticated API 60 requests/hour per IP, GITHUB_TOKEN 1000 requests/hour per repo. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/limits (fetched 2026-11-19))
- ubuntu-latest moves to Ubuntu 26.04, rolled out between 2026-10-19 and 2026-11-19; ubuntu-24.04 can be pinned. (likely; https://github.blog/changelog/2026-09-17-ubuntu-26-generally-available-and-latest-migration/ ; https://github.com/actions/runner-images/issues/14748)
- Release assets: each file under 2 GiB; up to 1000 assets per release; no limit on total release size or bandwidth. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases (fetched 2026-11-19))
- Immutable releases are generally available (2025-10-28): tags and assets of a published release cannot be changed, and releases carry Sigstore-format attestations checkable with `gh release verify`. (likely; https://github.blog/changelog/2025-10-28-immutable-releases-are-now-generally-available/)
- GitHub Pages limits: 1 GB published site, 100 GB/month soft bandwidth, 10 builds/hour soft limit (not applied to custom Actions workflows), 10-minute deploy timeout. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/pages/getting-started-with-github-pages/github-pages-limits (fetched 2026-11-19))
- GitHub Packages are free for public packages (storage and transfer); Container registry storage and bandwidth are 'currently free', with at least one month's notice before a change. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-packages (fetched 2026-11-19))
- No documented GHCR per-layer size cap was found; one report shows ghcr.io rejecting request bodies over 4 MiB per chunk. (unverified; web search 2026-11-19 (containers/image PR comment); no GitHub doc found)
- Apptainer is not preinstalled on the Ubuntu 24.04 or 26.04 runner images; shellcheck (0.9.0 and 0.11.0), Docker and Podman are. (verified; gh api repos/actions/runner-images/contents/images/ubuntu/Ubuntu2404-Readme.md and Ubuntu2604-Readme.md (read 2026-11-19))
- Dependabot's `uv` ecosystem compiles `.in` files, matches requirements.in to requirements.txt by name or by `--output-file` in the header, reruns `uv pip compile -P dep==ver`, restores the original header, and keeps `# via` annotations; compile options come from the file's content, not uv.toml. (verified; https://github.com/dependabot/dependabot-core/blob/main/uv/lib/dependabot/uv/file_updater/compile_file_updater.rb and file_updater.rb (read 2026-11-19))
- The Dependabot uv fetcher does not appear to fetch uv.toml (ecosystem_specific_files covers readme, license, uv.lock, workspace members and version source files). (likely; https://github.com/dependabot/dependabot-core/blob/main/uv/lib/dependabot/uv/file_fetcher.rb (read 2026-11-19))
- Dependabot lists uv as a supported ecosystem; the github-actions ecosystem gets version and security updates; docker gets version updates only; cooldown is supported for uv, with a 3-day default when unset, and it does not apply to security updates. (likely; https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/ecosystems-supported-by-dependabot/supported-ecosystems-and-repositories ; https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/reference/supply-chain-security/dependabot-options-reference)
- Renovate's pip-compile manager handles `uv pip compile` but throws 'Option X not supported (yet)' for flags outside its uv allow-list. `--config-file` and the short `-o` are not on it, so our lock header would be rejected. (verified; https://github.com/renovatebot/renovate/blob/main/lib/modules/manager/pip-compile/common.ts (read 2026-11-19); https://docs.renovatebot.com/modules/manager/pip-compile/)
- astral-sh/setup-uv reads `required-version` from uv.toml or pyproject.toml when no version is given, has enable-cache and cache-dependency-glob inputs, and can verify checksums; its examples pin v10.3.3 by SHA. (verified; https://github.com/astral-sh/setup-uv (fetched 2026-11-19))
- `uv audit` is a preview feature (`audit-command`) built around uv.lock; for a pinned requirements.txt, `pip-audit -r requirements.txt --no-deps --disable-pip` audits without installing, against PyPI or OSV, with CycloneDX output. (likely; https://docs.astral.sh/uv/concepts/preview/ ; https://github.com/pypa/pip-audit)
- CodeQL default setup is available for public repos with Actions enabled; GitHub Actions workflow analysis (language `actions`) is GA (2025-04-22) and turns on automatically in default setup when workflows exist. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/code-scanning/enabling-code-scanning/configuring-default-setup-for-code-scanning ; https://github.blog/changelog/2025-04-22-github-actions-workflow-security-analysis-with-codeql-is-now-generally-available)
- Push protection has been on by default for new public repos owned by personal accounts since March 2024; existing repos and org-owned new repos are not changed. This repo has secret scanning and push protection disabled. (verified; https://github.blog/changelog/2024-03-11-secret-scanning-and-push-protection-are-enabled-by-default-on-new-public-repositories/ ; gh api repos/<org>/<repo> security_and_analysis)
- pre-commit.ci is always free for open-source repositories; autoupdate defaults to weekly; autofix PRs are on by default. (verified; https://pre-commit.ci/ (fetched 2026-11-19))
- The OpenSSF Scorecard action is free for public repositories; the badge needs publish_results: true and id-token: write. (verified; https://github.com/ossf/scorecard-action (fetched 2026-11-19))
- release-please assumes Conventional Commits and follows SemVer (Release-As footer to override); git-cliff can use regex commit parsers on arbitrary messages. (verified; https://github.com/googleapis/release-please ; https://git-cliff.org/docs/)
- GitHub's automatically generated release notes are built from merged pull requests, with categories configured in .github/release.yml. (likely; https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/automatically-generated-release-notes)
- Zenodo's GitHub integration mints a version DOI per published release under one concept DOI; records cannot be deleted; if .zenodo.json exists, CITATION.cff is ignored for the Zenodo record; GitHub's 'Cite this repository' reads CITATION.cff (APA, BibTeX). (verified; https://help.zenodo.org/docs/github/describe-software/ ; https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-citation-files)
- Material for MkDocs is in maintenance mode (PyPI classifier 'Inactive', 9.8.1 on 2026-11-07, EOL May 5, 2027); MkDocs last released 1.6.1 on 2024-08-30; Zensical is 0.1.2 Beta (2026-11-17) and uses a mike fork for versioning. (verified; https://pypi.org/pypi/{mkdocs-material,mkdocs,zensical}/json (2026-11-19); https://github.com/squidfunk/mkdocs-material/releases ; https://zensical.org/docs/compatibility/mkdocs/mike/)
- Read the Docs Community is free for open-source projects with one non-tracking ad. (likely; https://docs.readthedocs.io/page/about/index.html (via search 2026-11-19))
- CalVer adopters include pip (YY.MINOR.MICRO) and Home Assistant (YYYY.M.MICRO); calver.org recommends an unpadded month where SemVer compatibility matters. (verified; https://calver.org/)
- Comparable tools' release practice: RELION tags 5.0.0 (2024-12-02), 5.0.1 (2025-09-22), 5.1.0 prerelease (2026-03-13); Warp v2.0.0dev37–dev41 (2026-04 to 2026-09); napari v0.10.0 (2026-10-30) with rc and alpha tags; Open OnDemand keeps two lines (v4.2.6 2026-11-03, v4.1.7 2026-08-31); cryoSPARC dated patch builds such as v4.7.1+251124 installed with `cryosparcm update`. (verified; gh api repos/{3dem/relion,warpem/warp,napari/napari,OSC/ondemand}/releases (2026-11-19); https://cryosparc.com/updates/v4.7.1+251124.md)
- `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/releases/latest` answers with a 302 to the latest tag and `/releases.atom` with 200 application/atom+xml; the REST endpoint from this headnode showed x-ratelimit-limit 60 with 37 remaining (shared egress IP). (verified; curl probes against a public repo, 2026-11-19)
- Repo state: public, Apache-2.0, 0 workflows, 0 releases, 0 tags, no rulesets, main unprotected, Pages none, Discussions off, wiki on, community health 50%, 43 open issues, 24 merged PRs ever, CodeQL not configured, private vulnerability reporting off, dependency graph 0 manifests. (verified; gh api reads of the repo, community/profile, rulesets, code-scanning/default-setup, private-vulnerability-reporting; GraphQL dependencyGraphManifests (2026-11-19))
- On tracked files today: ruff check clean, ruff format --check reports 48 files to reformat, check_boundaries.py clean, py_compile clean, check_look_literals.py exits 1. (verified; local runs with .uv/env/3.11.18-8febfdb40744 (ruff 0.15.12), --no-cache --force-exclude, 2026-11-19)
- In a fresh clone with the conf and qsub templates copied and a clean HOME, `pytest --deselect tests/test_static.py::test_preflight_checks_pass` gives 3 passed, 1 skipped in 2.71 s; importing server_app, backend and drivers.driver_base succeeds (about 10 s on the headnode). (verified; scratch clone run, 2026-11-19)
- Recompiling the lock with the pinned uv 0.13.7 and uv.toml into a copy of requirements.txt reproduces identical pins in about 1 s. (verified; local run, 2026-11-19)
- OSV lists advisories affecting the locked versions of nicegui 3.0.3 (28, fixed by 3.12.0 at the latest), starlette 0.48.0 (12, ≤1.3.1), requests 2.31.0 (6, ≤2.33.0), pillow 12.0.0 (~100 including GHSA/PYSEC aliases, ≤13.0.0) and biopython 1.86 (2). (verified; https://api.osv.dev/v1/querybatch and /v1/query, 2026-11-19)
- The Python environment's 78 distributions carry no strong copyleft: only docutils (multi-licensed incl. GPL), bidict and certifi (MPL-2.0), and orjson (MPL-2.0 AND (Apache-2.0 OR MIT)) are not purely permissive. (verified; importlib.metadata scan of .uv/env/3.11.18-8febfdb40744, 2026-11-19)
- JupyterLab fetches news and update information only after the user consents (opt-in); Grafana's check_for_updates is on by default and can be turned off. (likely; https://jupyterlab.readthedocs.io/en/latest/privacy_policies.html ; Grafana defaults.ini via search)

### Open questions

- Versioning scheme: recommended CalVer `vYYYY.M.PATCH` (v2026.12.0) with a VERSION file. Alternative: SemVer 0.x as napari does, worth it only if the agent API is to be the compatibility promise; it already has its own /api/v1 and API_VERSION.
- What sites track: recommended a `stable` branch that only fast-forwards to release tags (sites keep `git pull`). Alternatives: checking out tags (detached HEAD, `git pull` stops working) or tracking main as today.
- Keep pushing straight to main? Recommended yes for 1–2 maintainers, with CI on every push and rulesets only on `v*` tags and `stable`. Requiring PRs would raise the Scorecard score and enable PR-based notes, but slows the current pace.
- Dependency bumps before the December tag: recommended yes for requests, pillow and starlette (via fastapi) now, and nicegui 3.0.3→3.12.x as a dedicated UI test pass. Also recommended: drop the requirements.in:10 rule tying numpy, pandas, scipy and requests to the cluster's 2023 modules, since the environment no longer uses them.
- Update notice: recommended a no-network default (read origin/stable and tags after the admin's `git fetch`) plus an opt-in `update_check: true` that makes one call a day per install to the `/releases/latest` redirect. Alternative: an on-by-default outbound check, Grafana-style.
- Docs site for December: recommended none (curated README and docs index on GitHub). Later: Zensical on GitHub Pages; Read the Docs if per-tag versioned docs are wanted.
- Zenodo DOI with the first release: recommended yes, after the repo cleanup, with CITATION.cff only. The PI must supply the author list and ORCIDs, and an org admin must approve the Zenodo app.
- Format debt: recommended one `ruff format .` commit (48 files) recorded in .git-blame-ignore-revs before CI turns on, rather than excluding files.
- check_look_literals.py in CI: recommended non-blocking until the current offenders are fixed, then blocking.
- Patch releases: recommended tagging patches from main while main is releasable; keep a `release/YYYY.M` branch with cherry-picks only when main is mid-refactor.
- Tag signing: recommended SSH-signed annotated tags plus immutable releases; this needs the maintainer's signing key uploaded to GitHub as a signing key.
- Dependency-update bot: recommended Dependabot (`uv` + `github-actions`, monthly, grouped, 7-day cooldown) with the CI lock-sync gate. Renovate only if the lock header is changed to drop `--config-file` and `-o`.

## recon:onboarding: A new site installs CryoBoost end to end (the Munich case): fresh clone, install, preflight, self-test, images, updates, and what ties it to the current cluster

I cloned HEAD (80a874b) into scratch and installed it exactly as the README says. The Python side works well. `./install.sh` took 6.7 s and fetched uv 0.13.7, CPython 3.11.18 and 78 wheels into a 1.5 GB `.uv/`, with no edits needed. Preflight's messages tell an admin what to fix: on the untouched template it lists 14 failures, each naming the key. The static self-test runs in 1.8 s. The code holds no CBE partition names or paths (roadmap 20 S2e held); the friction is elsewhere.

1. **Tool images (the biggest gap).** The install doc's table points at four old, partly unpinned .def files (RELION on branch `ver5.0`, IsoNet2 at HEAD, Miniconda "latest"), while production runs the `*_sm100` builds. The easymode .def exists only on the lab filesystem. The napari image is not mentioned in the install doc at all. The images in use total 82.4 GB, all built by hand.
2. **Nothing checks that an image is what the code expects.** Preflight only checks that each image path is a file. With the docs' minimal example and three 4-byte fake .sif files, preflight said "All checks passed" and the static test passed.
3. **The template ships CBE-only values that no check catches.** missAlign asks for `gpu:nvidia_b200:1` and `qos: g_medium`, and IsoNet train asks for `g_medium`. Preflight checks only partition names, and the cluster self-test only uses `slurm_defaults`. At a second site, sbatch rejects those jobs the first time they run.
4. **Updates are silent.**
   - There are no tags, no version, no changelog and no update notice; a site follows a `main` that got 393 commits since 2026-09-15.
   - `conf.yaml` is untracked, and the conf template changed 39 times in that period.
   - Test: a conf filled from the template as it stood at the 2026-10-15 handoff loads under HEAD with 0 warnings, yet 61 keys of today's template are missing from it: whole job profiles, `tools.tilt_filter`, `napari`, `ood`. Memory values the newer template raised also stay old in such a conf (tsCtf 8G vs 32G now, tmextractcand 8G vs 24G).
   - Image rebuilds an update needs (6 .def commits since 10-16, two of them new required images) show up only when a job fails.
5. **Site config, code and Python environment share one checkout.** A `git pull` changes the code that running servers and queued jobs run. The launcher and REPO_ROOT already resolve symlinks, so versioned release folders behind a `current` link, with the site config outside them, would fix this cheaply.
6. **Things a site gets by hand with no check.**
   - The tilt-filter weights: unpublished; preflight says nothing; the job fails when DL mode is picked.
   - cisTEM (4.3 GB, but only `simulate` is used).
   - The easymode weights (4.0 GB).
