7. **Node requirements nobody documents or checks:** the MPS daemon (template matching runs 3 lanes by default), an NVIDIA driver new enough for the CUDA 12.9 images, ssh from headnode to compute nodes for ChimeraX, and SLURM accounting.
8. **No end-to-end check after install.** The copia protocol (EMPIAR-12580) is in the repo, but the install doc does not point to it as the last step.

The rest is small: stale command names in messages and docs, misleading lines in the install output, and repo clutter a public visitor sees (the tracked `memory/`, stale branches, `.gitignore` leftovers).

### Findings

**ONB-1 [high, effort M] No release channel: no tags, version, changelog or update notice; sites track a fast-moving main**

A new site clones `main` and updates with a bare `git pull`. The only version is `<branch>@<sha>`, read from .git. There is no CHANGELOG, no tag to pin, and no way for a running site to learn that a new version exists or what it requires. `main` takes about 6 commits a day (393 since 2026-09-15), and per the maintainer's notes many features land 'built, not run'. The roadmap 23 stage 6 update banner is not built.

Evidence:
- gh api repos/<org>/crboost_server: 0 tags; branches main + 6 stale (pipeline_state_expansion 2026-03-04, isonet_auto_and_missalignment_fix 2026-08-14, denovo_picking 2026-08-26, protocols 2026-09-09, k3_implementation 2026-09-15, missalignmnet 2026-10-01); local main == origin/main (0 0)
- services/code_version.py:46-60 (version = branch@sha, None outside a checkout)
- git ls-files: no CHANGELOG/NEWS/RELEASE file
- docs/installation.md:215-228 (Updating = `git pull` with no branch/tag)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_23-per-user-servers.md: Stage 6 'Shared install + update banner' not built (status line: pickup stage 2)
- git log --since=2026-09-15 --oneline | wc -l -> 393

Recommendation: Cut CalVer tags (e.g. v2026.12.0) from main only after a runtime pass at CBE. The install doc then clones/checks out a tag. Each tag gets a CHANGELOG section with a fixed 'Upgrade notes' block: conf keys added/changed/removed, images to (re)build, lock changed y/n, qsub.sh changed y/n. Make `code_version()` report the tag (git describe), and add a read-only `./crboost update --check` that compares it with the newest tag on the remote.

**ONB-2 [high, effort M] Site conf drift on update is never announced: load_warnings flags only unknown keys, not keys the template gained or changed**

`ConfigService.load_warnings` lists keys the models do not read; nothing compares a site's conf.yaml with conf.template.yaml. Test: I took the template as it stood at the Munich handoff (0e1d708, 2026-10-15) and loaded it with HEAD code. It gave 0 warnings, yet 61 key paths of today's template are missing. Missing whole profiles: segment, ctfRefine, maskCreate, postprocess, filamentPick, extractPickList. Also missing: tools.tilt_filter (needed since the tilt-filter network moved into a container), tools.easymode, napari, ood, gpu_kernel_cache, supervisor_slurm.qos, curation.qos. Values that changed are invisible too: tsCtf mem 8G->32G, tmextractcand 8G/0:15 -> 24G/1:00, tsImport 4G/1cpu -> 16G/8cpu, tsReconstruct 64G/1:00 -> 24G/0:20, class3d gpu:4 -> gpu:1, reconstructParticle and subtomoExtraction gpu:1 -> no GPU. The root cause: job_resource_profiles mixes code-owned needs (memory/time tied to how drivers chunk work) with site-owned placement (partition, GRES type, QOS), all in one untracked file. Roadmap 39 S6 records the same failure at the lab itself (the lab conf lacked the g_medium lines).

Evidence:
- services/configs/config_service.py:335-366 (_unread_keys/_config_warnings: unknown keys only)
- services/configs/config_service.py:111-129 (JobResourceProfile all-optional; Config.job_resource_profiles defaults to {} at :308)
- measured: scripts/old_conf_probe.py on `git show 0e1d708:config/conf.template.yaml` -> 'load_warnings on the old conf: 0', '61' missing key paths
- git log --since=2026-09-15 -- config/conf.template.yaml: 39 commits (57 total)
- docs/installation.md:225-227 ('a setting the template gained since you installed has to be copied over by hand')
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:297-298 (lab conf lacked qos g_medium lines)

Recommendation: (a) Move the code-owned half of each profile (mem, cpus_per_task, time, and gres count where it is a code fact, e.g. refine3d gpu:2) into tracked defaults that ship with the code. Keep only placement (partition, GRES type, qos, constraint, prefer, avoid) and paths in the site conf, which overlays the defaults. (b) Add a preflight section 'template vs this conf' that prints keys new in the template and defaults changed since the site's values were set. It announces and never merges, consistent with the no-silent-default rule. (c) Copy the same list into each release's Upgrade notes.

**ONB-3 [high, effort S] Template ships CBE-only GRES types and QOS that preflight and the cluster self-test never validate**

Values in the template, not comments: missAlign `gres: gpu:nvidia_b200:1` + `qos: g_medium`, denoisetrain `qos: g_medium`, curation `partition: c`. The install doc says to change six keys and 'leave everything else as it is'. Preflight validates partitions only, and the cluster tier submits every probe with slurm_defaults, so no profile is exercised. Test: the doc's minimal example passed preflight and the static test with these values untouched. At a site without those GRES/QOS names, the first missAlign or IsoNet-train job is rejected by sbatch. The data needed for the check is already queried elsewhere in the code (sinfo %G, sacctmgr assoc/qos).

Evidence:
- config/conf.template.yaml:91-97 (missAlign gres gpu:nvidia_b200:1, qos g_medium)
- config/conf.template.yaml:139 (denoisetrain qos g_medium)
- config/conf.template.yaml:265 (curation.partition "c")
- docs/installation.md:91-92 ('change these — leave everything else as it is')
- preflight.py:207-227 (only partition values checked)
- tests/test_cluster.py:153 (every probe uses SlurmConfig.from_config_defaults)
- services/computing/slurm_service.py:513 (sinfo %G), :578 (sacctmgr assoc QOS), :605 (sacctmgr qos)
- measured: preflight3.txt 'All checks passed' with those profiles unchanged

Recommendation: Ship neutral values in the template (untyped `gpu:1`, `qos: ""`), with the CBE values as commented examples. In preflight, check every slurm_defaults/supervisor/profile/curation/napari GRES type against `sinfo -h -o '%P %G'` and every qos against the user's `sacctmgr show assoc`, failing with the key name. Optionally the cluster tier runs `sbatch --test-only` once per distinct profile placement.

**ONB-4 [high, effort M] Images: the install doc points at stale, unpinned defs; production uses others; some defs are not in the repo; nothing identifies an image**

The install-doc table maps tools to warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def, relion5.0_tomo.def (checks out branch ver5.0, Miniconda 'latest'), pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def and isonet2.def (IsoNet2 at HEAD). Production runs the *_sm100 defs. Those carry SASS from sm_50/52 up to sm_100 plus PTX, so they also serve older GPUs, and the template-matching driver's GPU-memory model is calibrated to pytom 0.14, not 0.10. The doc's own build example uses the old warp def. The easymode def (easymode_1.2.5.def) exists only on the lab filesystem; the doc says 'not in the repo yet'. The napari image is not mentioned in installation.md, and the repo's napari_GL.def is byte-identical to the lab's napari_GL_v2.def that production runs, so the def name and image name have drifted apart. containers/chimerax_artiax/README.md tells you to build chimerax_artiax.def, which is not tracked, and the runtime error says 'Build it under containers/chimerax_artiax/', where no def exists. Preflight checks only `is_file()`: a 4-byte fake .sif passed. %labels are inconsistent (placeholder authors, no machine key; roadmap 39 S5 not built). All 12 images in use total 82.4 GB, built by hand; the required minimum (warp_aretomo + relion) is 26.1 GB.

Evidence:
- docs/installation.md:57-58, 66-78
- container_defs/relion5.0_tomo.def:38,51 ; container_defs/isonet2.def:21 ; container_defs/pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def:46
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:8-9,95-96 ; warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:73-75,99
- drivers/template_match_pytom.py:210-225 (pytom 0.14 memory model)
- cmp container_defs vs lab defs dir: lab-only easymode_1.2.5.def, napari_GL_v2.def (== repo napari_GL.def), warp_2.0.0dev41_cuda12.9.def and 8 others
- containers/chimerax_artiax/README.md:9,37 ; services/curation/session_service.py:99-100
- preflight.py:155-165 (path existence only)
- measured: ls -l of the 12 images config/conf.yaml points at = 82.4 GB; relion sm100 13.4 GB, warp sm100 12.7 GB
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:271-278 (S5 labels, not built)

Recommendation: Delete the four superseded defs, in line with the settled 'upgrade in place' decision. Add easymode_1.2.5.def. Keep one def per tool key, plus a tracked manifest (e.g. container_defs/images.yaml: tool key -> def file, tool version, label set, published image URL + sha256) that generates the install doc's table and that preflight reads. Build roadmap 39 S5: uniform `%labels crboost.tool/crboost.def/crboost.version`, and preflight warns when `apptainer inspect --labels` disagrees or the file is not a SIF. Offer prebuilt images so a site does not have to build 82 GB; where to host them is the container lens's call.

**ONB-5 [high, effort M] Site config and code share one mutable checkout: no versioned installs, and a pull changes code under running servers and queued jobs**

conf.yaml and qsub.sh are fixed at REPO_ROOT/config/. Drivers run the server's own interpreter with PYTHONPATH set to the checkout. Consequences: (1) After a `git pull`, every job (queued or newly submitted) runs the new code. (2) An un-restarted server keeps its old environment, so if the lock changed, new driver code runs in the old environment and can fail on an import. (3) Two versions cannot sit side by side on one site config. The launcher and install.sh already resolve the checkout with `pwd -P`, and REPO_ROOT uses resolve(). So `releases/vX/` folders behind a `current` symlink would freeze each server and its jobs on the release it started from, once the site config lives outside the checkout.

Evidence:
- services/configs/config_service.py:31 (DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH = REPO_ROOT/config/conf.yaml; no env override)
- services/computing/slurm_service.py:124 (QSUB_TEMPLATE = REPO_ROOT/config/qsub.sh)
- services/jobs/spec.py:565-572 (interpreter = crboost_python or sys.executable; PYTHONPATH = checkout)
- crboost:14 and install.sh:19 (here=$(cd ... && pwd -P))
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:480-484 (queued jobs run the code on disk at start)
- docs/installation.md:224 (restart servers after update; nothing about jobs already queued)

Recommendation: Add `CRBOOST_SITE_DIR` (or `--site`) for conf.yaml + qsub.sh + weights, defaulting to config/ for backward compatibility. Document the layout `<root>/releases/<tag>/`, `<root>/current -> releases/<tag>`, `<root>/site/`, with CRBOOST_ENV_ROOT shared so environments dedupe. Updating a site then means: clone the new tag beside the old one, run install.sh, preflight and `-m cluster`, then flip `current`. Servers already running keep their release; users restart to move. Also do roadmap 39 §6.2: stamp code_version() into run_submit.script and have drivers warn when it differs.

**ONB-6 [medium, effort S] Things fetched by hand with no check and no pointer: DL weights, easymode weights, cisTEM**

(1) The template registers `filterTilts/tiltnet_resnet18_o1034.pth` (45 MB). It is git-ignored and unpublished (roadmap 39 D7 open), and preflight says nothing. The job raises 'weights file not found' only when a user picks DL review/auto; the code default mode is MANUAL. (2) easymode's cache_dir needs `easymode download --all` (4.0 GB at the lab); the template points to 'the image's %help', but that image's def is not in the repo. (3) cisTEM is 'a normal cisTEM install', with no version or URL given; the lab copy is 4.3 GB / 86 binaries, and only `simulate` is used. How the admin finds out about each: weights from a template comment and two doc lines ('ask the maintainers'); easymode from a template comment; cisTEM from one table row.

Evidence:
- .gitignore: '*.pth'
- config/conf.template.yaml:331-338 (model registered by default), :409-415 (easymode cache_dir, `easymode download --all`), :390-393 (cistem bin_path)
- services/jobs/tilt_filter.py:225-226 (raises at job time); :126 (default FilterMode.MANUAL)
- docs/installation.md:101-103, 162, 207 ('doesn't check the tilt-filter weights or the ChimeraX image'), :78 (cistem row)
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:74 (D7 owed)
- measured: du /groups/<lab>/software/cache_and_artifacts/easymode_1.2.5 = 4.0G; cisTEM dir 4.3G, 86 binaries; filterTilts/*.pth 44.8 MB

Recommendation: Publish the weights (D7) with a sha256 in the conf, plus `scripts/fetch_weights.py` (roadmap 39 S5). Preflight warns when a registered model's file is missing or its hash differs, and names the fetch command. Document the easymode weight download and the cisTEM version (only `simulate` is needed) in the image manifest/doc. The curation and napari checks should verify sif_path when it is set, not only at launch.

**ONB-7 [medium, effort S] Compute-node and cluster requirements beyond SLURM+Apptainer are neither documented nor probed**

The doc's 'You need' line names a headnode with SLURM + Apptainer, GPU nodes and a shared filesystem. The code also relies on:
- `nvidia-cuda-mps-control` on the node host, raising if it fails; template matching defaults to 3 lanes under MPS.
- `nvidia-smi` on the host, and MIG profile names in a particular format.
- Passwordless ssh from headnode to compute node with StrictHostKeyChecking=no (ChimeraX REST, on by default).
- /dev/shm (missAlign; falls back with a warning).
- Room in TMPDIR (template matching; falls back).
- sacct/sacctmgr accounting (status reconcile, QOS limits).
- An NVIDIA driver new enough for CUDA 12.9 images.
- Outbound HTTPS from the build host to docker hub, nvcr.io, github.com, conda-forge, repo.anaconda.com, download.pytorch.org, cgl.ucsf.edu and bio3d.colorado.edu.
- `--fakeroot`, or root, for builds.
None of these appears in the cluster tier's probes except per-tool GPU visibility.

Evidence:
- docs/installation.md:6-9
- drivers/array_job_base.py:877-893 (MPS daemon, check=True); services/jobs/template_match.py:134-135 (lanes default 3)
- drivers/driver_base.py:241-260 (nvidia-smi -L, MIG parsing)
- services/curation/session_service.py:1004-1020 (ssh BatchMode, StrictHostKeyChecking=no); services/configs/config_service.py:215 (rest_enabled True)
- drivers/miss_align.py:146-155 (/dev/shm); drivers/template_match_pytom.py:303-315 (TMPDIR room)
- services/computing/slurm_service.py:578,605,830 (sacctmgr, sacct)
- tests/test_cluster.py:99-122 (probes: no MPS, no driver version)
- CUDA 12.x minor-version compatibility floor 525.60.13 (see facts)

Recommendation: Add a 'Site requirements' table to installation.md (host binaries, driver floor, ssh to nodes or `curation.rest_enabled: false`, accounting, outbound hosts for builds, fakeroot). Add an MPS start/stop probe and an `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=driver_version` line to the cluster tier, and have preflight check that `sacctmgr show assoc user=$USER` answers. A read-only `./crboost site-survey` that prints partitions, GRES, QOS, the driver version (via a probe) and Apptainer/SLURM versions would also give Munich a one-command answer sheet to send back.

**ONB-8 [medium, effort S] No end-to-end acceptance step after install**

The install doc ends at `./crboost test -m cluster`, which only proves each tool starts. The repo carries a complete reference protocol (copia, EMPIAR-12580, importmovies -> class3d, template and mask tracked), and roadmap 20's Stage 5 success criterion for Munich is 'copia protocol runs'. Yet the doc does not tell a new site to fetch that dataset and run it, or what success looks like (stages, wall time, expected resolution/outputs).

Evidence:
- docs/installation.md:186-199
- config/protocols/copia-tutorial/protocol.yaml:1,12-15
- docs/protocols.md:92-93
- docs/roadmaps/completed/roadmap_20-site-portability.md:187-188

Recommendation: Add install step 6: download one tilt series of EMPIAR-12580 (exact command), create a project from the copia-tutorial protocol, run it, and compare against stated expectations (each stage's status, rough wall time at CBE, Class3D outcome). Use the same run as the release checklist item at both sites.

**ONB-9 [medium, effort S] Update instructions omit images, conf diff and the cluster self-test**

The documented update is `git pull; ./install.sh; ./crboost preflight`. It does not say which images must be rebuilt (6 container_defs commits since 2026-10-16; two of them make an image newly required: tilt_filter on 11-06, napari on 11-17). It does not ask for `-m cluster` after an update. And it relies on the admin reading the template diff by hand. The cluster tier already encodes one version-feature check (`--dont_premultiply` for Warp dev40+), which shows the probe-per-required-feature pattern works.

Evidence:
- docs/installation.md:215-228
- git log --since=2026-10-16 -- container_defs: 89994c1, e78b44f, b5655df, a58faaa, 273f13d, 7dafde5
- tests/test_cluster.py:100-101 (Warp feature probe)
- services/jobs/tilt_filter.py:227-231 (runtime error when the tilt_filter image is not configured)

Recommendation: Have the release Upgrade notes (ONB-1) name every image to rebuild. A `./crboost update` helper (fetch tags, show the changelog, print the conf diff (ONB-2), list defs changed since the installed tag, build the environment, run preflight) turns the doc's Updating section into one command. For any release that touches images, require `-m cluster`.

**ONB-10 [low, effort S] Stale command names and misleading install output**

These messages and doc lines name commands the install no longer uses, or print misleading text:
- The 'no conf.yaml' error says `Run 'python preflight.py'`. With a system python this fails on imports; the supported command is `./crboost preflight`.
- The docs' upgrade section says `preflight.py` and `pytest -m cluster` instead of `./crboost preflight` / `./crboost test -m cluster`.
- The doc says '1–12 GB per container'; the relion image is 13.4 GB.
- Install output carries two misleading lines: the astral installer prints 'WARN: The following commands are shadowed by other commands in your PATH: uv uvx' (harmless; the system uv is 0.11.19), and uv prints 'Activate with: source .uv/env/<name>.tmp-<pid>/bin/activate', a directory renamed away seconds later.
- When the Python download fails offline, uv's raw error stack is all the admin sees. It has no pointer to UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_MIRROR, which the docs describe.
- The uv-fetch failure, by contrast, is a clear message.

Evidence:
- services/configs/config_service.py:383
- docs/installation.md:232,255
- docs/installation.md:9
- install.sh:57 (curl ... | sh), install.sh:157 (uv venv), install.sh:156 (uv python install)
- measured install.stderr lines 1-12; offline run: 'error: Failed to install cpython-3.11.18-linux-x86_64-gnu ... Connection refused (os error 111)'
- measured offline: 'install: could not fetch uv 0.13.7 from astral.sh; put a uv 0.13.7 binary at .../.uv/bin/uv, or point CRBOOST_UV at one' (good)

Recommendation: Fix the strings. Silence the astral installer's PATH notice (check which variable or flag does this in that installer version) and run `uv venv -q`. Wrap `uv python install` so a failure prints the UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_MIRROR hint and the docs anchor.

**ONB-11 [low, effort S] Public-repo clutter that misleads a new admin**

What a public visitor or new admin sees:
- 174 tracked files of the maintainer's session memory (memory/).
- Six stale remote branches.
- `.gitignore` re-including `munich_handoff_note.md` (no longer tracked) and ignoring `--git-common-dir` (an accident).
- A README whose start instructions are the lab's own install path.
- Def %labels with placeholder or tool-generated author names and a personal email (relion sm100 def).
- A containers/chimerax_artiax/README titled for one cluster, describing partitions c/g.
- An install doc that does not say which branch to clone.

Evidence:
- git ls-files memory | wc -l -> 174
- gh branches (see ONB-1)
- .gitignore: '!munich_handoff_note.md', '--git-common-dir', '--git-common-dir/**'
- README.md:12-17,78
- container_defs/imod.def, pymol.def, relion5.0_tomo.def %labels (placeholder authors); relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:20 (maintainer email)
- containers/chimerax_artiax/README.md:1,9-11

Recommendation: Move memory/ out of the public tree (a private repo or a git-ignored folder). Delete the stale branches. Clean `.gitignore`. Make the README quick start site-neutral, with the lab path as one example. Normalise %labels to the crboost.* set from ONB-4 and drop personal contact data from them.

**ONB-12 [low, effort S] Three defs pull Anaconda's defaults channel and auto-accept its terms; imod.def disables TLS checks**

cryocare.def and imod.def install Miniconda from repo.anaconda.com and run `conda tos accept` non-interactively. relion5.0_tomo.def also uses Miniconda. Telling other institutes to build these accepts Anaconda's commercial terms on their behalf. Per Anaconda's FAQ, use of the defaults channel needs a paid licence at organisations with 200 or more staff; 2024 reporting says this also covers non-profits and government. imod.def downloads IMOD and Miniconda with `wget --no-check-certificate`. The sm100 defs already moved to Miniforge (conda-forge only).

Evidence:
- container_defs/cryocare.def:22,27,32-33
- container_defs/imod.def:39,54,65-66
- container_defs/relion5.0_tomo.def:38
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:11 ('Miniforge, conda-forge only (was Anaconda Miniconda, which now needs a ToS acceptance)')

Recommendation: Move cryocare.def and imod.def to Miniforge/conda-forge, as the sm100 defs did. Drop `--no-check-certificate`. Delete relion5.0_tomo.def (ONB-4).

**ONB-13 [low, effort S] pymol image is 8.9 GB because of unused, unpinned packages**

pymol.def pip-installs numpy, scipy, matplotlib, pandas, scikit-learn, mrcfile, pillow, scikit-image, starfile, pymol-open-source, cryodrgn and biopython, all unpinned. crboost only runs PyMOL scripts through it and never uses cryodrgn, which pulls in torch. A new site builds or downloads 8.9 GB for a template-from-PDB feature, and the build cannot be reproduced.

Evidence:
- container_defs/pymol.def (pip install lines, From: python:3.9-slim)
- services/templating/pdb_service.py:57 (tool_name="pymol"); git grep cryodrgn -- '*.py' -> no hits
- tests/test_cluster.py:114 (probe: import pymol)
- measured: pymol.sif 8,893,399,040 bytes

Recommendation: Slim it to pymol-open-source plus what pdb_service needs, with pinned versions; it should drop to well under 1 GB.

**ONB-14 [low, effort S] Cluster self-test takes ~17 min because every probe queues for a GPU**

`./crboost test -m cluster` took 16:43 at the lab for 4–26 s of actual tool calls. Every probe asks for slurm_defaults (a GPU), so the CPU-only tools wait in the GPU queue. The maintainer already wanted this changed (roadmap 39 §6.5); it is not built. At a new site this is the step an admin repeats most often while fixing images.

Evidence:
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:204,491-494
- tests/test_cluster.py:153

Recommendation: Run the probes that do not touch a GPU (relion version, isonet help, imod, pymol, cistem, chimerax) on supervisor_slurm resources. Add `-k <tool>` guidance to the docs so one fixed image can be retested alone.

**ONB-15 [low, effort S] Half of the 1.5 GB install is uv's download cache inside the checkout**

Breakdown: .uv/cache 692M, .uv/env 665M, .uv/python 89M, .uv/bin 36M. On a shared install, every new lock adds an environment beside the old one, and the cache keeps growing until someone prunes it. `./crboost clean` removes environments but never prunes the cache.

Evidence:
- measured du in the scratch clone (see facts)
- install.sh:43-46 (UV_CACHE_DIR=$here/.uv/cache)
- install.sh:76-118 (clean: environments only)

Recommendation: Make `./crboost clean --yes` also run `uv cache prune` (or `--ci` after a build). Document CRBOOST_ENV_ROOT and the cache location for sites that keep installs on a quota'd filesystem.

### Facts

- A fresh local clone of HEAD 80a874b installs with `./install.sh` in 6.7 s wall (uv 0.13.7 fetched from astral.sh, CPython 3.11.18 from python-build-standalone release 20261026, 78 packages, 6328 .pyc compiled), exit 0, environment 3.11.18-8febfdb40744. (verified; measured on the CBE login node, <scratch>/recon/onboarding/install.stderr)
- Disk after install: .uv = 1.5 GB (cache 692 MB, env 665 MB, python 89 MB, bin 36 MB); clone working tree incl. .git ~75 MB (local hardlinked clone). (verified; du in the scratch clone (deleted afterwards))
- `./crboost preflight` first run creates config/conf.yaml and config/qsub.sh and exits 1 in 0.05 s. On the untouched template the second run exits 1 with 14 [FAIL] lines in 3.3 s, each naming the key: DefaultProjectBase, 11 tool paths, and 2 partitions, plus the sinfo partition list. `./crboost test` (static tier) exits 1 in 1.8 s (1 failed, 3 passed, 1 skipped) with one DeprecationWarning from starlette's testclient. (verified; <scratch>/recon/onboarding/preflight2.txt, test1.txt)
- With the install doc's 'only Warp, RELION and PyTOM' example applied and three 4-byte fake .sif files, preflight prints 'All checks passed' and the static test gives 4 passed, 1 skipped. The template's missAlign gpu:nvidia_b200:1 / g_medium, denoisetrain g_medium and napari.sif_path placeholder are untouched and unflagged. (verified; <scratch>/recon/onboarding/preflight3.txt, test2.txt)
- Offline headnode: with no uv, install.sh stops with a clear, actionable message. With uv present but no network, it stops with uv's raw 'Failed to install cpython-3.11.18 ... Connection refused' and no hint about UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_MIRROR. (verified; measured with https_proxy pointed at a closed port in a second scratch clone)
- The conf template as of 0e1d708 (2026-10-15, the Munich handoff) loads under HEAD code with 0 load_warnings, while 61 key paths of the HEAD template are absent from it. Absent: 6 whole job profiles, tools.tilt_filter, tools.easymode, napari, ood, gpu_kernel_cache, supervisor_slurm.qos, curation.qos, local.SshLoginHost. (verified; scripts/old_conf_probe.py run against `git show 0e1d708:config/conf.template.yaml`)
- conf.template.yaml has 57 commits in total, 39 of them since 2026-09-15. Since 2026-10-16: 6 container_defs commits and 5 commits touching requirements.txt/.python-version/uv.toml. The repo has 393 commits since 2026-09-15. (verified; git log on main)
- The 12 images the lab conf uses total 82.4 GB: relion sm100 13.4, warp sm100 12.7, isonet2 sm100 12.4, pymol 8.9, pytom 0.14 sm100 7.9, miss_alignment 7.6, cryocare 6.8, easymode 5.8, tilt_filter 4.1, imod 1.2, chimerax_GL 1.0, napari_GL_v2 0.5. The required minimum (warp_aretomo + relion) is 26.1 GB. Extra hand-fetched data: easymode weights 4.0 GB, cisTEM install 4.3 GB (86 binaries; only `simulate` is used), DL weights 44.8 MB. (verified; ls -l / du on the lab containers/sifs, cache_and_artifacts and cisTEM directories)
- The lab defs folder holds 11 defs that are not in the repo (incl. easymode_1.2.5.def and napari_GL_v2.def). The repo's napari_GL.def is byte-identical to the lab's napari_GL_v2.def. cryocare.def, imod.def, isonet2.def, napari_GL.def and chimerax_artiax_GL.def differ between repo and lab copies. The lab defs folder is mode 0777. (verified; cmp/diff of container_defs/ against the lab defs directory; ls -la)
- The CBE cluster runs SLURM 26.05.3 and Apptainer 1.5.4 and has partitions c, g, m. GPU GRES types on g: nvidia_b200, nvidia_b200_1g.23gb, nvidia_a100-sxm4-40gb, nvidia_a100_3g.20gb, quadro_rtx_6000. QOS: c_/g_/m_ {long,medium,rapid,short}, interactive, normal. A second site's names will differ. (verified; sbatch --version, apptainer --version, sinfo -h -o '%P %G', sacctmgr show assoc (read-only))
- No hard-coded CBE partition, GRES type, QOS or filesystem path remains in Python/shell code. CBE values appear only in conf.template.yaml values (lines 92, 97, 139, 265), in comments, in docs/README, and in containers/chimerax_artiax/README.md. (verified; git grep over *.py *.sh *.yaml *.def excluding docs/memory/notes)
- The repo's default branch is main, and local main equals origin/main. There are 0 tags, 6 other remote branches (last commits 2026-03-04 to 2026-10-01) and 43 open issues. (verified; gh api repos/<org>/crboost_server (read-only))
- The cluster self-test took 16:43 on the lab install for 4–26 s of tool calls, because every probe uses slurm_defaults (a GPU). (verified; docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:204,491)
- CUDA 12.x applications run on a Linux driver of at least 525.60.13 under minor-version compatibility. The sm100 images (CUDA 12.9, SASS sm_50/52..sm_100 + PTX) should therefore run on older GPUs given a driver at least that new; whether PTX JIT works under minor-version compatibility was not confirmed. (likely; https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.0.0/cuda-toolkit-release-notes/index.html ; https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/cuda-compatibility/forward-compatibility.html)
- Anaconda's defaults channel needs a paid licence for organisations with 200 or more employees. Anaconda's FAQ says large universities are still free. The Register's 2024 reporting says government and non-profit entities over 200 staff must pay, with an exemption for teaching. conda-forge is not covered. (likely; https://anaconda.com/?p=14186 ; https://www.theregister.com/2024/08/08/anaconda_puts_the_squeeze_on/ ; https://www.datacamp.com/blog/navigating-anaconda-licensing)
- The tilt filter job defaults to MANUAL mode in code, so missing weights break only a DL review/auto run, at job time, with 'weights file not found'. (verified; services/jobs/tilt_filter.py:126, :225-226)
- Template matching defaults to 3 lanes under MPS, which needs `nvidia-cuda-mps-control` on the compute-node host; the driver raises if the daemon does not start. (verified; services/jobs/template_match.py:134-135 ; drivers/array_job_base.py:877-893 ; drivers/template_match_pytom.py:592)

### Open questions

- Release channel: should sites install tagged releases (CalVer, cut from main after a CBE runtime pass) instead of following main? Recommended: yes. Docs clone a tag, main stays the development branch, and each tag carries Upgrade notes covering conf keys, images, lock and qsub.
- Should the site config (conf.yaml, qsub.sh, weights) move outside the checkout, via CRBOOST_SITE_DIR, so releases can be installed side by side behind a `current` symlink? Recommended: yes, keeping config/ as the default for existing installs.
- Should job_resource_profiles be split into code-owned needs (mem/cpus/time, tracked and shipped) and site-owned placement (partition, GRES type, qos, constraint, prefer/avoid in conf.yaml)? Recommended: yes. Otherwise every memory/time fix keeps missing every existing site, the lab included.
- Delete the four superseded defs (warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9, relion5.0_tomo, pytom_match_pick_0.10.0, isonet2) and keep one def per tool key? Recommended: yes. It follows the settled upgrade-in-place rule, and the sm100 builds also carry SASS for older GPUs. Confirm Munich's GPU models and NVIDIA driver version first (driver must be at least 525.60.13).
- Bring easymode_1.2.5.def from the lab filesystem into container_defs/, and document its weight download? Recommended: yes. Without it a second site cannot get the Segment job at all.
- Should the template ship neutral GRES/QOS values (untyped gpu:1, qos "") with the CBE values as comments, and should preflight validate GRES types and QOS against sinfo/sacctmgr? Recommended: yes to both.
- DL weights (roadmap 39 D7): may they be published (model author's licence), and where? Recommended: Hugging Face, with a sha256 in the conf and a fetch script; until then the template ships `tilt_filter: {models: {}}`, or preflight warns that the registered weights are missing.
- Facts only Munich can give, needed before the plan is final: GPU models and driver version; partition, GRES and QOS names; whether MPS is allowed; whether login nodes reap long-running processes; outbound internet on the headnode and build host; whether --fakeroot works; Apptainer and SLURM versions; whether sacct/accounting is enabled; ssh from headnode to compute nodes. Recommended: send a read-only survey script and a one-page form rather than asking piecemeal.
- Move memory/ (174 files of session notes) out of the public repo? Recommended: yes, to a private location. It confuses outside readers and carries lab paths.

## recon:containers_inventory: Tool containers as they exist today (local facts only): container_defs/, containers/, the images the live config runs, how the code finds and checks them, licences, size, and what a CI build of each would take

The images that run in production and the defs a new site is told to build do not match. docs/installation.md (lines 57-77) and README.md:120 point Munich at four LEGACY defs: warp aretomo1, relion5.0_tomo, pytom 0.10.0 and isonet2. None of them is the recipe of an image in use. They lack sm_100, and relion5.0_tomo and isonet2 build unpinned branch HEADs. Two in-use images have no def in the repo: easymode_1.2.5 (its def exists only on the lab share) and the CPU ChimeraX image that the template's curation default names. The repo's miss_alignment def is not what built the running image either: the image has cuda-toolkit 12.9 plus nsight-compute, while the def says cuda-runtime. That image is now also the runtime of the H26 Warp-geometry bridge for RELION STA, and the bridge imports warpylib and starfile, which arrive only as unpinned transitive dependencies. No image is reproducible from its def alone. Every conda def downloads the latest Miniforge or Miniconda, every From: line uses a moving tag, and conda solves float: the Warp env picked up pytorch 2.14.1 and dotnet-sdk 10.0.401 at build time. cryoCARE, PyMOL and ArtiaX are installed unpinned. Two retrofits make them reproducible without rebuilding. (1) Apptainer embeds the exact def in each SIF and records the base digest (apptainer 1.5.4 builds), and `apptainer inspect` reads both in about 20 ms. (2) `conda list --explicit` and `pip freeze` can be exported read-only from every image; I did this for 8 envs. Together these make S5 (image provenance check) buildable today without labels or rebuilds. On redistribution: the ChimeraX image must never be published (the UCSF non-commercial licence forbids distribution). Six images sit on NVIDIA CUDA devel bases under the NGC container licence, and how that combines with GPL tools is unclear. Four of those six never compile anything and do not need the devel base, which alone accounts for ~8.5 GB uncompressed per image. Size is dominated by duplicated CUDA: a devel base plus a conda or pip CUDA in the same image. PyMOL is 8.9 GB because of an unused cryodrgn/torch install (crboost only calls pymol2). The in-use images total ~82 GB, plus 4.0 GB of easymode weights, 4.3 GB of cisTEM and the hand-copied tilt-filter weights. Builds take 2-16 min on a 16-core CBE.next CPU node and need network but no GPU. The build and verify scripts live in the maintainer's scratch, not the repo. Only napari, tilt_filter, imod and a slimmed pymol are obviously small enough and clean enough to build and publish from free CI as they stand.

### Findings

**C01 [blocker, effort S] Install docs send new sites to legacy defs that no running image was built from**

docs/installation.md section 2 is the only build guidance a new site gets, and its table names the superseded defs. warp_aretomo -> warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def: no sm_100 for AreTomo2 (it stops at sm_86 with no PTX, per the successor def:5-7), and its %test checks never fail a build. relion -> relion5.0_tomo.def: no running image comes from it (relion-sm100 def:5-6 says the old image's recipe was the embedded one, not this file). It builds branch `ver5.0` HEAD (def:51) on nvidia/cuda:12.2.0-devel with no CUDA_ARCH, so it falls back to sm_50 (relion-sm100 def:8-9). It also uses Miniconda latest (def:38), RHEL openmpi paths in an Ubuntu image (def:96-97) and has 'Author Gemini' (def:5). pytom -> pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def: crboost now runs 0.14.0, whose results differ (0.12 wedge fix, 0.14 whitening; pytom-0.14 def:9-12), and cupy/pytorch are unpinned (def:34-43). isonet -> isonet2.def: unpinned HEAD (def:21, no checkout), CUDA 12.1 conda torch without sm_100. easymode: 'not in the repo yet'. The build line also uses `--fakeroot --nv` without `--ignore-fakeroot-command`, which CBE.next needs. Munich would therefore run different software than the maintainer validates, with no warning anywhere (see C04).

Evidence:
- docs/installation.md:51-81 (table rows 68-77)
- README.md:120 'Build the tool containers from container_defs/ (warp_aretomo and relion are required)'
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:4-12
- container_defs/relion5.0_tomo.def:2,5,38,51,70-75,96-97,107
- container_defs/warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:5-7
- container_defs/warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def:53-57,65-68
- container_defs/pytom-match-pick-0.14.0_cupy-13.6.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:9-12
- container_defs/pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def:34-46
- container_defs/isonet2.def:2,19-22
- live config/conf.yaml:363-416 uses the *_sm100 / 0.14.0 images

Recommendation: Add a single manifest (e.g. container_defs/manifest.yaml or a table in code) that maps each tools: key and viewer image to its one current def. Rewrite docs/installation.md section 2 from that manifest with the CBE.next-proven build line. Delete the four legacy defs (isonet2.def, pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def, relion5.0_tomo.def, warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def); git history keeps them, and this follows the settled 'upgrade in place, no parallel keys' rule.

**C02 [blocker, effort M] The miss_alignment image in use was not built from the repo's def, and the H26 bridge depends on its unpinned transitive packages**

The repo def installs `cuda-runtime=12.9` (def:57-71, with a long comment explaining the change). The SIF's embedded def says `cuda-toolkit=12.9`, and the image's conda-meta holds cuda-toolkit-12.9.2, cuda-nvcc-12.9.86 and nsight-compute-2025.2.1.3. So the repo def is an untested edit: a rebuild would produce a different, probably smaller, image that may hit the `ImportError: libcu*.so.12` the def's own comment warns about. miss-alignment and torch-projectors are installed unpinned (def:79-84) and resolved to 0.2.0 and 0.12.0+cu129. warpylib 1.0.1 and starfile 0.5.13 come in only transitively. The image is no longer missAlign-only: drivers/warp_bridge.py runs containers/miss_alignment/warp_trajectories.py inside it, importing warpylib, starfile and torch. Every RELION driver that cuts from tilt images calls the bridge (CLAUDE.md, HOTFIXES H26), so a site needs this image for STA whenever its Warp XMLs carry local geometry. The def's own verification is soft: a failed import only prints WARN (def:90-92), and %test does not fail a build.

Evidence:
- container_defs/miss_alignment_torch2.8.0_cuda12.9.def:57-71,76-84,90-92
- apptainer inspect --deffile miss_alignment_torch2.8.0_cuda12.9.sif -> line 61 'cuda-toolkit=12.9' (13 differing lines vs repo def)
- apptainer exec ... ls conda-meta -> cuda-toolkit-12.9.2, cuda-nvcc-12.9.86, nsight-compute-2025.2.1.3
- importlib.metadata in image: miss-alignment 0.2.0 requires warpylib (unversioned), torch>=2.2; torch-projectors 0.12.0+cu129; warpylib 1.0.1; starfile 0.5.13
- drivers/warp_bridge.py:5-7,18,58-60
- services/particles/warp_geometry.py:45 WARP_GEOMETRY_TOOL = "miss_alignment"
- containers/miss_alignment/warp_trajectories.py:4,30-35

Recommendation: Pin miss-alignment, torch-projectors, warpylib and starfile explicitly in the def, and make every import check hard (fail %post). Then rebuild from the repo def (cuda-runtime), run the cluster probe and one bridge run on a B200, and only then make it the shipped image. Until that happens, restore the def to what built the running image. Add a probe for the bridge's imports to tests/test_cluster.py PROBES['miss_alignment'].

**C03 [high, effort S] In-use images with no def in the repo, and def/image names that disagree**

(a) easymode_1.2.5.sif, the Segment and filament-pick tool: its def lives only on the lab share at containers/defs/easymode_1.2.5.def (178 lines), and is byte-identical to the SIF's embedded def. docs/installation.md:73 says 'not in the repo yet', yet the template names the image (conf.template.yaml:411-419). (b) The CPU ChimeraX image chimerax_artiax.sif is the template's default curation image (conf.template.yaml:264-265, partition c) and is described in containers/chimerax_artiax/README.md:13,40-41. Its def exists only on the share (containers/chimerax_artiax.def, which also differs from the def embedded in the shared chimerax_artiax.sif) and lacks the GL def's scipy fix. (c) The repo's napari_GL.def is the recipe of napari_GL_v2.sif, the image the live conf uses; napari_GL.sif (v1) was built from an earlier version (15 differing lines). (d) A stale message: session_service.py:99-100 says 'Build it under containers/chimerax_artiax/', but the defs are in container_defs/. (e) There is no def at all for cisTEM (native, see C13).

Evidence:
- /groups/<lab>/software/containers/defs/easymode_1.2.5.def == apptainer inspect --deffile easymode_1.2.5.sif (diff empty)
- docs/installation.md:73
- config/conf.template.yaml:264-265,411-419
- containers/chimerax_artiax/README.md:13,33-41
- /groups/<lab>/software/containers/chimerax_artiax.def differs from inspect --deffile chimerax_artiax.sif
- cmp container_defs/napari_GL.def <share>/defs/napari_GL_v2.def -> identical; live config/conf.yaml:322 sif_path napari_GL_v2.sif
- services/curation/session_service.py:96-100,823-827

Recommendation: Copy easymode_1.2.5.def into container_defs/ verbatim (it is the image's exact recipe). Decide whether a CPU ChimeraX variant ships: if yes, bring its def in with the GL def's fixes; if no, change the template default to the GL image and partition g. Name each def after the image it builds, so the manifest (C01) can be checked mechanically. Fix the session_service message.

**C04 [high, effort M] Nothing records or checks which def or version built an image (S5 not built), but the data to do so is already embedded in every SIF**

Today's checks only test existence. preflight checks that each tools: path is a file or dir (preflight.py:155-165) and does not check curation.sif_path or napari.sif_path at all, only curation.partition (128-129); docs/installation.md:207 admits this. The wrapper only checks that container_path is non-empty (container_service.py:91-92), so a missing file fails inside the SLURM job. ConfigService.container_status reports exists true/false for the landing dot (config_service.py:535-547). session_service checks a viewer image's existence at launch (96-100, 823-827). The cluster self-test checks capability, not version (tests/test_cluster.py:25-48); Warp is the only exception, via --dont_premultiply. The only runtime version record is the Segment driver writing easymode/ais versions into its manifest (drivers/segment.py:74,255-279). A stale or wrong image is therefore invisible. However: (1) `apptainer inspect --deffile <sif>` returns the exact def that built it, in ~20 ms. For 7 current images it matches the repo def modulo trailing whitespace: warp-sm100, relion-sm100, pytom-0.14, isonet-sm100, tilt_filter, pymol, napari_GL_v2. cryocare and imod differ in comments only, chimerax_GL in comments plus one comment-in-line, and miss_alignment substantively. So a normalised-def hash comparison works retroactively, with no rebuild and no labels. (2) Apptainer 1.5.4 builds record org.opencontainers.image.base.digest. (3) The %labels that exist are inconsistent: 'Tool'/'Version' keys in 7 defs, with values like 'WarpTools+AreTomo2' rather than the tools: key; none at all in chimerax_GL; template leftovers in the rest (Author YourName / User / Gemini). Plug points for S5: preflight.check_config's tools loop (preflight.py:155-165) plus new checks for curation.sif_path and napari.sif_path; ConfigService.container_status (config_service.py:535-547) for the landing dot and settings markers; ToolConfig (config_service.py:148-161) if an expected-def override per site is wanted; tests/test_cluster.py PROBES for version asserts; TiltFilterModelConfig (config_service.py:277-292) for the weights sha256 (roadmap 39 S5:277-278).

Evidence:
- preflight.py:128-129,155-165
- services/computing/container_service.py:91-92
- services/configs/config_service.py:148-161,277-292,473-481,535-547
- tests/test_cluster.py:25-53
- drivers/segment.py:74,255-279
- docs/installation.md:207
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_39-python-environment-and-setup.md:45-46,271-278
- time apptainer inspect --deffile relion-...sif -> real 0m0.022s
- apptainer inspect --labels on 10 images (Tool/Version keys vs Author YourName vs none)

Recommendation: Build S5 on the embedded def rather than on new labels. preflight (and the landing dot) computes sha256(normalised `apptainer inspect --deffile`) for every tools: image and both viewer images, then compares it with the def the manifest (C01) names for that key. It warns, never blocks (as S5 says), naming the def and its build line. Also add `%labels crboost.tool <key>` and `crboost.def <file>` for human-readable `inspect` output, and record the base digest. Add sha256 for the tilt-filter weights and an easymode cache check (models.sha256 exists already). Fix CLAUDE.md, which still says get_tool_config has 'legacy alias support'; config_service.py:473-476 has none.

**C05 [high, effort M] No image is reproducible from its def; a lock can be exported from every running image**

Floating inputs: Miniforge 'latest' (warp-sm100:51, relion-sm100:75, pytom-0.14:51, isonet-sm100:33, miss:52, and the share's easymode def:67) and Miniconda 'latest' (cryocare:22, imod:54). Every From: uses a moving tag, never @sha256. Conda solves float: the Warp env resolved pytorch 2.14.1 cuda129 and dotnet-sdk 10.0.401 on 2026-11-04, and a rebuild today may differ. cryoCARE is unpinned (pip `cryoCARE`, resolved 0.3.0). PyMOL is fully unpinned (resolved pymol-open-source 3.2.0a0, an alpha). ArtiaX is installed by `toolshed install ArtiaX` (resolved 0.6.0). AreTomo2 and pytom-match-pick come by git tag, not commit (v1.0.0, 0.14.0). apt packages are never pinned. Roadmap 20 section 1.7 asked for exactly this pinning 'with the next rebuild'; the 2026-11-04 rebuild kept Miniforge latest and TZ=Europe/Vienna. Retrofit, verified read-only: `conda list -p <env> --explicit --md5` and `python -m pip list --format=freeze` inside each SIF give a complete lock. I exported 8 (warp 149 conda/23 pip lines, relion 26/192, pytom 52/21, isonet 262/141, miss_alignment 185/110, cryocare 31/57, easymode 30/87, imod 282/139). The napari image already carries its pip freeze at /opt/napari/requirements.lock (112 lines). Base digests are recorded for 4 images: cuda:12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04 sha256:bd4e2680a261c212f1e2fea241606f71497dc67a417f73175d794ec8212b5ba8 (warp, relion, pytom, isonet), python:3.11-slim-bookworm sha256:a83ba18aef0f865b1a4a611c2cf460fe446f2795673bbb9fe43be570439e3b39 (tilt_filter), and ubuntu:24.04 sha256:73b999393d07f2bf65a40373778a44dc9a6d54ad7718d29247d8ff9413c4d6a8 (napari v2). Images built with apptainer 1.1.9 (cryocare, imod, easymode, miss_alignment, chimerax_GL, pymol) have no digest label.

Evidence:
- container_defs/*.def From: lines (line 2 of every def)
- warp-sm100 def:51,60-62,69-71; relion-sm100 def:75,83-85; pytom-0.14 def:51,64-65; isonet-sm100 def:33,38-40; cryocare.def:22,43; imod.def:54; pymol.def:43-47; chimerax_artiax_GL.def:166
- image conda lock: pytorch-2.14.1-cuda129_generic_py311_h22acca5_200, dotnet-sdk-10.0.401, warp-2.0.0dev41-py311ha998128_0 (warpem channel)
- docs/roadmaps/completed/roadmap_20-site-portability.md:141-146,178-180
- apptainer inspect --labels -> org.opencontainers.image.base.digest values above
- exported locks: <scratch>/recon/containers/locks/

Recommendation: For each current image, commit a lock next to its def (container_defs/locks/<image>.conda.txt + .pip.txt, exported from the image that runs). Make the def install from the lock: `conda create --file <explicit>`, `pip install -r`, with the file brought in via %files. Pin From: by the recorded digest, Miniforge by release version and sha256, and git sources by commit sha. Drop TZ=Europe/Vienna. Then rebuild once and check that the new embedded def and lock match.

**C06 [high, effort S] The ChimeraX image may not be redistributed; its build accepts UCSF's licence on the builder's behalf**

The image contains /usr/lib/ucsf-chimerax/share/docs/license.html, the 'UCSF ChimeraX Non-Commercial Software License Agreement'. It grants use 'for academic, non-profit, or government-sponsored research purposes' only, says the licensee 'shall not distribute or transfer the Software or Improvements to any person or third parties without prior written permission from The Regents', and requires that a request for a copy be referred to the UCSF ChimeraX web page. The def fetches the .deb by POSTing choice=Accept to UCSF's download CGI and scraping the redirect (chimerax_artiax_GL.def:106-123). Whoever builds it, including a CI runner, accepts the licence, and the build breaks if UCSF changes that page. ArtiaX itself is GPLv3 (its dist-info LICENSE; the classifier says 'Freeware'), so it is not the problem. Commercial users of crboost cannot use the curation feature under this licence.

Evidence:
- apptainer exec chimerax_artiax_GL.sif cat /usr/lib/ucsf-chimerax/share/docs/license.html (General, Permitted Use and Restrictions paragraphs)
- container_defs/chimerax_artiax_GL.def:53,106-125,165-169
- /opt/cx/share/ChimeraX/1.9/lib/python3.11/site-packages/ChimeraX_ArtiaX-0.6.0.dist-info/LICENSE = GPL-3.0; METADATA Classifier: License :: Freeware

Recommendation: Never push this SIF to a registry, CI artefact or public share. Ship the def and a 'build it yourself' step that tells the builder they accept UCSF's licence (better: make acceptance an explicit build arg, `--build-arg ACCEPT_UCSF_LICENSE=yes`, so CI cannot accept silently). Mark curation as optional and non-commercial in the docs. Pin ArtiaX (download the release wheel by version) so the build stops depending on toolshed's 'latest'.

**C07 [high, effort M] Six images sit on NVIDIA CUDA devel bases (NGC container licence), and four of them never use the devel toolkit**

The cuda devel bases carry /NGC-DL-CONTAINER-LICENSE. Section 1c allows distributing a 'Compatible derived CONTAINER that includes the entire CONTAINER plus other software', subject to section 2: a notice, terms at least as protective, notifying NVIDIA of misuse. Section 4c forbids distributing it 'as a stand-alone product', and 4g forbids using it 'in any manner that would cause it to become subject to an open source software license' that requires redistribution at no charge. Putting GPL tools in the same image (RELION GPLv2, Warp GPL-3.0-only, IsoNet2 GPLv3, pytom GPLv2, easymode/ais/pom GPLv3) makes publishing these images legally unclear. The devel base is used to compile only in warp (nvcc for AreTomo2, def:76) and relion (cmake/nvcc, def:102-110). pytom gets NVRTC 12.9 from conda (def:57-62), isonet gets torch cu129 wheels (def:47), cryocare gets cudatoolkit 11.0 and cudnn 8.0 from conda-forge (its lock is 100% conda-forge), and easymode gets cudatoolkit 11.2 and cudnn 8.1 from conda. The miss_alignment (ubuntu:22.04) and tilt_filter (python slim) defs already avoid an NVIDIA base. Their CUDA comes from conda-forge cuda-* packages or PyPI nvidia-* wheels, which are under NVIDIA's own EULA terms (not read here).

Evidence:
- apptainer exec relion-...sif sed -n 20,110p /NGC-DL-CONTAINER-LICENSE (sections 1a-1c, 2a-2d, 4a-4h)
- From: nvcr.io/nvidia/cuda:12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04 in warp-sm100:2, relion-sm100:2, pytom-0.14:2, isonet-sm100:2; nvcr.io/nvidia/cuda:11.8.0-devel-ubuntu20.04 in cryocare.def:2; nvcr.io/nvidia/cuda:11.2.2-cudnn8-devel-ubuntu20.04 in share easymode_1.2.5.def:2
- container_defs/pytom-match-pick-0.14.0_cupy-13.6.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:56-62
- container_defs/miss_alignment_torch2.8.0_cuda12.9.def:14-16
- container_defs/tilt_filter_torch2.8.0_cuda12.8.def:4-8
- /opt/relion-5.0/LICENSE GPLv2; warp conda-meta license GPL-3.0-only; /opt/IsoNet2/LICENSE GPLv3; pytom-match-pick metadata GPLv2

Recommendation: Rebase pytom, isonet, cryocare and easymode on ubuntu (or a micromamba base) with CUDA from conda or pip only, as miss_alignment and tilt_filter already do. Build warp and relion as Apptainer multi-stage images: compile in the devel stage, then copy the binaries plus a conda env with cuda-cudart, libcufft etc. into a non-NVIDIA final stage. This takes the NGC licence out of the publish question entirely and removes ~8.5 GB uncompressed per image. Until then, publish defs and locks, not SIFs, for these six.

**C08 [medium, effort M] Image size is mostly duplicated CUDA and unused packages; measured contributors per image**

Measured with du inside each image (uncompressed GB; SIF size in brackets). warp [12.7]: cuda-12.9 devel 7.3 + /opt/nvidia (nsight) 1.2 + conda env 12 (pytorch cuda129, dotnet SDK, its own CUDA runtime) + pkgs 0.7; AreTomo2 is 6 MB. relion [13.4]: devel 7.3 + nsight 1.2 + env 8.7 (torch cu129 wheels) + /opt/relion-5.0 3.0 (source, .git and the whole build tree, never `make install`ed). pytom [7.9]: devel 7.3 + nsight 1.2 against an env of 3.3, so roughly 70% is unused base. isonet [12.4]: devel 7.3 + nsight 1.2 + env 9.1 + 1.1 of pkgs left after `conda clean`. miss_alignment [7.6]: env 13, with full cuda-toolkit and nsight-compute. cryocare [6.8]: cuda-11.8 devel 5.2 + nsight 0.9 + env 4.6 (cudatoolkit 11.0 again). easymode [5.8]: cuda-11.2 devel 3.4 + env 5.2 (cudatoolkit 11.2 again). pymol [8.9]: site-packages 7.5, almost all cryodrgn -> torch 2.8 + 14 nvidia-* wheels, plus pandas, scikit-learn and matplotlib, while crboost uses only `pymol2` (load, get_extent, count_atoms, get_coords, load_coords, save). imod [1.2]: IMOD 0.37 + conda env 1.6 (numpy, scipy, pandas, scikit-image, jupyter, ipython) + openjdk-11, while crboost only runs point2model. tilt_filter [4.1]: torch + nvidia wheels 6.5, already minimal for a GPU torch. napari [0.47] and chimerax_GL [1.0] are fine. The in-use total is ~82 GB (76.7 GiB).

Evidence:
- apptainer exec <sif> du -sh /usr/local/cuda-* /opt/* /opt/*/envs/* (numbers above)
- ls -la /groups/<lab>/software/containers/sifs/ (byte sizes)
- services/templating/pdb_service.py:46-57,99-183 (pymol2 calls only)
- services/visualization/imod_vis.py:100-104; ui/particles/pick_viewer.py:3729 (IMOD: point2model only)
- container_defs/pymol.def:43-47; container_defs/imod.def:20-31,72-86
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:100-110 (build in place, no install/cleanup)

Recommendation: Estimates, not measured: pymol drops to <1 GB (python slim + pinned pymol-open-source + numpy, no cryodrgn, no build toolchain). pytom drops to ~2-3 GB, and isonet, cryocare and easymode each lose ~4-8 GB, by dropping the devel base. warp and relion lose ~8.5 GB each from a multi-stage build; relion also loses ~2-3 GB by `make install` into a prefix and deleting source/build. miss_alignment shrinks with cuda-runtime once verified (C02). imod shrinks to ~0.5 GB by dropping the unused conda env and the JRE if IMOD's point2model does not need it (check). Run `conda clean -afy` last in every def.

**C09 [medium, effort S] Build knowledge and scripts live outside the repo, and older defs will not rebuild on CBE.next as they are**

CBE.next needs `apptainer build --fakeroot --ignore-fakeroot-command`: there is no subuid range, and the faked daemon fails on GLIBC_ABI_DT_RELR (see the share's slurm-1376540.out). It also needs APPTAINER_TMPDIR on scratch, or /dev/shm for Ubuntu 24.04 because apt fails on BeeGFS with EBUSY. Inside %post it needs apt's sandbox user set to root and chown/chgrp dpkg-diverted. The 5 defs rebuilt in November carry that block (warp-sm100:26-34, relion-sm100:40-48, pytom-0.14:29-37, isonet-sm100:13-21, napari:42-50). The 6 older current defs (cryocare, imod, pymol, miss_alignment, chimerax_GL, easymode) were built on the old cluster with apptainer 1.1.9, according to their labels, and lack it, so they may fail at apt on CBE.next. The working build and verify scripts are in the maintainer's scratch, not the repo: /scratch/users/<maintainer>/container_verify/build.sh (sbatch on partition c, 16 CPUs, 64G, refuses to overwrite), verify_gpu.sh (a B200 run per kind: aretomo/torch/relion/cupy), build_tilt_filter.sh. Measured build times on a CBE.next c-node with 16 CPUs: warp 14m51s, relion 15m50s, isonet 10m38s, pytom 5m47s, tilt_filter 3m12s, napari_GL_v2 2m05s. Every def moved its real checks into %post because 'a failing %test does not fail an Apptainer build' (e.g. relion-sm100:112); the older defs (cryocare:67-75, imod:108-127, pymol:52-54, miss:112-118) still rely on %test. The share's build logs slurm-1376270.out and slurm-1376540.out are failed-build leftovers.

Evidence:
- /scratch/users/<maintainer>/container_verify/build.sh:1-23, verify_gpu.sh:1-40, build_tilt_filter.sh:1-18
- build logs build_build-{warp_1376608,relion_1376610,isonet_1376609,pytom_1376747,tilt-filter_1432979,napari_GL_v2_1764414}.log 'building ... at' / 'built ... at' lines
- /groups/<lab>/software/containers/slurm-1376540.out (faked GLIBC_ABI_DT_RELR, 'try --ignore-fakeroot-command')
- apptainer inspect --labels: org.label-schema.usage.apptainer.version 1.1.9-1 for cryocare, imod, easymode_1.2.5, miss_alignment, chimerax_artiax_GL, pymol
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:112-121

Recommendation: Add container_defs/build.sh (site-neutral, with the CBE.next flags as options), container_defs/verify.sh and a README covering the build line, the tmpdir rules, and the fact that %test does not fail a build. Move every check into %post as a hard failure. Add the fakeroot block to the six older defs when they are next touched (it is harmless under real root, e.g. on a CI runner). Delete the share's failed-build logs.

**C10 [medium, effort S] Licence/ToS acceptance and an insecure fetch in the build**

cryocare.def:27-33 and imod.def:64-66 install Miniconda from repo.anaconda.com and run `conda tos accept` for the Anaconda channels. Yet cryocare's resolved env is 31/31 conda-forge packages, and imod's 281/282 conda-forge with 1 from repo.anaconda.com. relion-sm100 already moved to Miniforge to avoid this ToS (def:11). imod.def:39 downloads the IMOD installer and imod.def:54 downloads Miniconda with `wget --no-check-certificate` and no checksum, so a site's image depends on unauthenticated downloads.

Evidence:
- container_defs/cryocare.def:20-33
- container_defs/imod.def:39-41,54-56,64-66
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:11
- exported locks: cryocare 31 lines all conda.anaconda.org/conda-forge; imod 281 conda-forge + 1 repo.anaconda.com/pkgs

Recommendation: Switch both defs to a pinned Miniforge (conda-forge only) and drop the ToS lines. Fetch IMOD over TLS and check a sha256 committed in the def.

**C11 [medium, effort M] Weights and caches that a site needs but no script can fetch**

(a) easymode weights cache: 4.0 GB at <share>/cache_and_artifacts/easymode_1.2.5, filled once with `easymode download --all` (HF_HUB_OFFLINE=0) and then frozen with `find models -type f -exec sha256sum {} + | sort -k2 > models.sha256`. crboost reads models/registry.json and models.sha256 (services/segmentation/easymode.py:1-7,54-75) and records the sha256 in the manifest. Weights are not in the image (the def comment says the hosted weights have been replaced in place before). (b) Tilt-filter weights: filterTilts/tiltnet_resnet18_o1034.pth (45 MB), git-ignored (.gitignore:56-57), copied by hand. Publication and licence are D7 (open), and preflight does not check them (docs/installation.md:207). (c) gpu_kernel_cache: one subdirectory per image stem (container_service.py:96-99), ~1.6 GB per TensorFlow image. It stays a per-site cache, and documenting it is enough. (d) The Ais cache (<share>/cache_and_artifacts/ais_1.2) used by the share's tests is lab-wrapper state, not crboost's.

Evidence:
- du -sh /groups/<lab>/software/cache_and_artifacts/easymode_1.2.5 -> 4.0G (hf, models, models.sha256, settings.txt)
- services/segmentation/easymode.py:1-7,26-35,54-80
- docs/roadmaps/completed/roadmap_37-segmentations-v1.md:80,93-98,300
- share easymode_1.2.5.def:19-24,40-43
- config/conf.template.yaml:331-338,349-357,409-415
- .gitignore:54-57

Recommendation: Add one fetch script per artefact that verifies the committed sha256. For easymode: run `easymode download --all` in the image, then check against a models.sha256 committed in the repo (it is a few KB). Mirror the frozen weights to a stable host once the licence allows, because the HF copy changes in place. For the tilt filter: the D7 host plus a sha256 in conf (roadmap 39 S5).

**C12 [medium, effort S] Several images are code-free runtimes for the checkout, which decouples them from crboost releases but makes their dependencies implicit**

tilt_filter runs `python -m filterTilts.deepLearning.predict` from the checkout, with REPO_ROOT/filterTilts and the weights dir bound in and PYTHONPATH set (drivers/tilt_filter.py:74-85). napari runs containers/napari/open_tomogram.py from the server's checkout; the worker binds the launcher's directory (session_service.py:753,871-878). The colleague's companion viewer also comes from a checkout (conf napari.companion). miss_alignment runs containers/miss_alignment/warp_trajectories.py (drivers/warp_bridge.py:18,58-60). The ChimeraX/napari worker curation_session.sh runs on the host, not in an image (session_service.py:202). All of them need the checkout on a path the compute nodes see at the same path, which container_binds normally covers. Upside: a crboost release does not need new images unless a Python dependency of these scripts changes. Downside: the scripts' dependencies (warpylib, starfile, torch, napari, mrcfile, dask) are declared nowhere a check can read.

Evidence:
- drivers/tilt_filter.py:16,74-85
- services/curation/session_service.py:202,741-753,871-881
- drivers/warp_bridge.py:5-7,18,58-60
- containers/napari/open_tomogram.py:1-5 ('imports nothing from crboost: the image holds napari (with dask), mrcfile and numpy')
- container_defs/napari_GL.def:12-17

Recommendation: Record in the manifest (C01) which repo scripts each image must run and which imports they need, and make those imports the image's %post check and its cluster probe. Then image versions and crboost releases can move independently, while a release still states the image def versions it was tested with.

**C13 [low, effort M] cisTEM is a native install with no provenance; only `simulate` is used**

tools.cistem is exec_mode binary with bin_path <share>/cisTEM/bin: a 4.3 GB directory dated 2024-04-02, with no README, licence or version file. The binary's strings say 2.0.0-alpha-295-b21db55-dirty, i.e. built from a dirty tree. crboost calls only `simulate`, for templates from PDB (pdb_service.py:236,414-453), and the probe is `command -v simulate`. The template requires a site to supply its own cisTEM (conf.template.yaml:390-393). The licence is not visible locally.

Evidence:
- config/conf.yaml:389-392; config/conf.template.yaml:390-393
- ls -la --time-style=long-iso <share>/cisTEM/bin/simulate (2024-04-02); du -sh -> 4.3G; strings simulate -> 2.0.0-alpha-295-b21db55-dirty
- services/templating/pdb_service.py:236,414-453
- tests/test_cluster.py:41

Recommendation: Document the cisTEM release a site should install (upstream binaries), and record its version from `simulate` output in the self-test. Optionally give `simulate` a small def of its own later, so template simulation is reproducible across sites.

**C14 [low, effort S] Personal data and placeholders in public defs and baked image labels**

relion-sm100 def:20 puts the maintainer's personal email (a gmail address) in %labels Maintainer, and it is baked into the running image's labels. Author names of the maintainer and the PI appear in cryocare.def:85, miss_alignment def:121, pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def:72 (%help) and relion5.0_tomo.def:107 (%help). Placeholders: 'Author YourName' (imod.def:130, pymol.def:5, and the cryocare image's embedded def), 'Author Gemini' (relion5.0_tomo.def:5), 'Author User' (isonet2.def:48). The repo memory records that the Author credit lines were left deliberately and flagged.

Evidence:
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:20
- container_defs/cryocare.def:85; container_defs/miss_alignment_torch2.8.0_cuda12.9.def:121; container_defs/pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def:72; container_defs/relion5.0_tomo.def:5,107
- container_defs/imod.def:129-132; container_defs/pymol.def:4-7; container_defs/isonet2.def:47-50
- memory/feedback_public_repo_no_names_no_swearing.md ('The container_defs Author lines (credit) were left in place, flagged to the user')

Recommendation: Replace Maintainer/Author labels with project labels (crboost.project = repo URL, crboost.def, crboost.tool), and put credit in CITATION/README. Most of the offending lines go with the legacy-def deletion (C01).

**C15 [low, effort M] Outdated bases and hacks for a cluster that no longer exists**

ubuntu:20.04 bases: cryocare, imod, easymode. python:3.9-slim: pymol. TF 2.4/CUDA 11 (cryocare) and TF 2.11/CUDA 11.2 (easymode) have no sm_100 code, so on B200 each JIT-compiles from PTX for 12-22 min per job unless gpu_kernel_cache is set (conf.template.yaml:349-357; memory notes that no TF release has sm_100 SASS). easymode on an EL10 host needs `image_libs_first: [/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu]` because the host libGL needs glibc >= 2.36 (conf.template.yaml:416-419). chimerax_artiax_GL.def:133-160 strips ELF ABI tags and pins manylinux2014 scipy 1.11.4 for 'the cluster's CentOS-7 kernel 3.10', but CBE.next is EL10. TZ=Europe/Vienna (warp-sm100:23, warp aretomo1:19, relion-sm100:37) is site-specific; roadmap 20 section 1.7 asked to drop it. GPU architecture coverage of the current images: AreTomo2 sm_52-sm_100 + compute_100 PTX; RELION sm_50-sm_100 + compute_100 PTX; torch cu129/cu128 wheels sm_50/70-sm_120; cupy NVRTC 12.9 JIT. So one image set should serve both CBE.next and Munich, given that Munich's driver supports CUDA 12.9.

Evidence:
- From: lines of cryocare.def, imod.def, share easymode_1.2.5.def, pymol.def
- config/conf.template.yaml:349-357,416-419
- container_defs/chimerax_artiax_GL.def:133-160
- container_defs/warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:23,95-99; relion-sm100 def:14-20,37
- container_defs/tilt_filter_torch2.8.0_cuda12.8.def:38-44; isonet-sm100 def:87-92

Recommendation: Set a base policy in the manifest (e.g. ubuntu 22.04/24.04 LTS, Python >= 3.11 where the tool allows). Remove the EL7 sweep and the TZ lines on the next rebuild. Ask Munich for GPU models, driver version and host OS before freezing CUDA 12.9 as the floor.

**C16 [low, effort S] The lab-share container docs contradict reality and are for the lab's CLI wrappers, not crboost**

<share>/containers/Containers-and-Wrappers.md:19-20 says 'Every image is built from a version-pinned .def file... the .def is the source of truth', which is contradicted by C02, C03 and C05. Its wrappers run `--cleanenv --no-home` (_common.sh, BASE_SINGULARITY_FLAGS), whereas crboost's wrapper does not pass --no-home (container_service.py:117-127). That is why tilt_filter and napari set PYTHONNOUSERSITE (tilt_filter def:27-29, napari def:28-29), while cryocare, imod, pymol and easymode do not. runscripts/WarpTools still runs the dev36 image (line 34), kept on purpose for the CLI wrappers per the maintainer's memory. TLDR.md is mis-indented, user-facing wrapper help. Worth bringing into the repo: the def conventions checklist (pin, verify in %post, cache mount points, %labels/%help, build line in the header) and the 'patch upstream config, assert the patch' pattern already in the easymode def. Not worth bringing: the wrappers themselves (crboost renders its own commands), the foldseek/muscle/reseek/foldmason defs, and ais/pom/easymode-old images.

Evidence:
- /groups/<lab>/software/containers/Containers-and-Wrappers.md:14-21,121,143,186-192
- /groups/<lab>/software/containers/runscripts/_common.sh:1-80; runscripts/WarpTools:34
- services/computing/container_service.py:117-127 (exec --nv --cleanenv, no --no-home)
- container_defs/tilt_filter_torch2.8.0_cuda12.8.def:27-29; container_defs/napari_GL.def:28-29

Recommendation: Put a container_defs/README.md in the repo with the conventions and the manifest, and replace the share's claim with a link to it. Decide whether `--no-home`, or PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 set by the wrapper for every image, should be standard: a user's ~/.local site-packages can currently shadow packages in images that do not set it.

**IMG-warp_aretomo [high, effort M] Image row: warp_aretomo = warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.sif (CURRENT, 12.7 GB)**

WarpTools 2.0.0dev41 (warpem conda; pytorch 2.14.1 cuda129 and dotnet-sdk 10.0.401 resolved at build) + AreTomo2 v1.0.0 built from source with sm_100 SASS and compute_100 PTX added by sed on makefile11. Base nvcr.io/nvidia/cuda:12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04 by tag (digest recorded in labels). Sources: Miniforge latest, conda channels warpem, nvidia/label/cuda-12.9.0 and conda-forge, AreTomo2 by git tag. Build: network, no GPU (CONDA_OVERRIDE_CUDA=12.9), CBE.next fakeroot block, 14m51s on 16 CPUs. %labels Tool/Version/CUDA/GPU; %help yes; checks in %post (Warp's --dont_premultiply, cuobjdump for sm_100). Reproducible: PARTLY (unpinned conda solve, Miniforge latest, AreTomo2 tag not sha). Redistributable: UNCLEAR (NGC devel base + Warp GPL-3.0-only + AreTomo2, whose licence is not on disk because the source is deleted after build). CI: needs ~35 GB free disk (rootfs ~22 GB uncompressed + 12.7 GB SIF) and a nvcr.io pull. Feasible on a self-hosted runner or the cluster; on free hosted runners only after the multi-stage shrink (C07/C08), and even then disk-bound.

Evidence:
- container_defs/warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:1-110
- config/conf.yaml:364-366
- apptainer inspect --labels/--deffile on the SIF; conda-meta warp license GPL-3.0-only
- build_build-warp_1376608.log 17:11:21 -> 17:26:12

Recommendation: Multi-stage build (compile AreTomo2 in devel, final stage without the NVIDIA base) plus a committed conda lock and the AreTomo2 commit sha. Check the AreTomo2 licence upstream before publishing a SIF.

**IMG-relion [high, effort M] Image row: relion = relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.sif (CURRENT, 13.4 GB)**

RELION at commit b16a35b0038336e6631b7a97b922f8c629b0b6f7 (5.0.1), GPU code sm_50-sm_100 + compute_100 PTX via a CMakeLists sed, MPI, GUI=ON, FETCH_WEIGHTS=OFF, and RELION's environment.yml with torch 2.8.0+cu129 and torchvision 0.23.0+cu129 from the PyTorch index. Base cuda:12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04 by tag. Miniforge latest. munge left out. Build 15m50s on 16 CPUs (make -j nproc, 10 archs). Labels include a personal email (C14). Reproducible: PARTLY (commit pinned; conda part of the env solved at build; Miniforge latest). Redistributable: UNCLEAR (NGC devel base; RELION GPLv2 itself is fine). crboost calls relion_refine(_mpi), tomo_subtomo, tomo_refine_ctf, tomo_reconstruct_particle, mask_create, postprocess, image_handler, particles_from_warp, python_tomo_import and import. It never uses Blush/ModelAngelo/DynaMight, so the image's torch serves only RELION's own python programs. CI: ~35 GB disk; compile time on 4 vCPU likely ~4x the 16-CPU time (estimate).

**IMG-legacy [medium, effort S] Legacy rows: defs and images that should not ship**

Defs in the repo: warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def (predecessor of warp-sm100, no sm_100 for AreTomo2); relion5.0_tomo.def (recipe of no image, ver5.0 HEAD, CUDA 12.2, sm_50 fallback); pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def (CUDA 12.1, NVRTC without sm_100, unpinned cupy/pytorch); isonet2.def (HEAD, CUDA 12.1 conda torch, no sm_100). All four are reproducible: NO. All four are named by docs/installation.md (C01). Images on the share that crboost no longer uses: napari_GL.sif (v1), chimerax_artiax.sif (CPU; still the template's default), easymode.sif (pre-1.2.5), and warp_2.0.0dev36_aretomo1.0.0_cuda11.8_glibc2.31.sif (kept for the lab's CLI wrapper runscripts/WarpTools, by the maintainer's choice). Also ais.sif, pom_cryoet.sif, foldseek/foldmason/muscle/reseek: lab wrapper tools, not crboost. The share's defs/ also holds warp_2.0.0dev41_cuda12.9.def (Warp-only, from the abandoned parallel 'warp_export' key) and the dev36 def.

- In-use image sizes (bytes): warp-sm100 12685086720; relion-sm100 13420818432; pytom-0.14 7947608064; isonet-sm100 12409401344; miss_alignment 7637553152; cryocare 6843924480; easymode_1.2.5 5810106368; tilt_filter 4065054720; imod 1204801536; napari_GL_v2 473100288; chimerax_artiax_GL 1013112832; pymol 8893399040. Total about 82.4e9 bytes (76.7 GiB). (verified; ls -la /groups/<lab>/software/containers/sifs/)
- The SIF's embedded def (apptainer inspect --deffile) matches the repo def modulo trailing whitespace for warp-sm100, relion-sm100, pytom-0.14, isonet-sm100, tilt_filter, pymol and napari_GL_v2 (built from container_defs/napari_GL.def). cryocare (7 lines) and imod (2 lines) differ in comments/Author only; chimerax_artiax_GL differs in comments; miss_alignment differs substantively (cuda-toolkit in the image vs cuda-runtime in the repo). (verified; apptainer inspect --deffile on each SIF, diffed against container_defs/*.def; outputs in <scratch>/recon/containers/embedded/)
- apptainer inspect --deffile / --labels on a 13.4 GB SIF takes about 0.01-0.02 s (it reads the SIF descriptor, not the squashfs). (verified; time apptainer inspect on relion-5.0.1-...sif on the headnode (apptainer 1.5.4-1.el10_2))
- Images built with apptainer 1.5.4 record the base image digest: cuda:12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04 = sha256:bd4e2680a261c212f1e2fea241606f71497dc67a417f73175d794ec8212b5ba8 (warp, relion, pytom, isonet); python:3.11-slim-bookworm = sha256:a83ba18aef0f865b1a4a611c2cf460fe446f2795673bbb9fe43be570439e3b39; ubuntu:24.04 = sha256:73b999393d07f2bf65a40373778a44dc9a6d54ad7718d29247d8ff9413c4d6a8. Images built with apptainer 1.1.9 (cryocare, imod, easymode, miss_alignment, chimerax_GL, pymol) have no digest label. (verified; apptainer inspect --labels (org.opencontainers.image.base.digest, org.label-schema.usage.apptainer.version))
- Full conda (--explicit --md5) and pip locks can be exported read-only from every conda-based image. Exported line counts (conda/pip): warp 149/23, relion 26/192, pytom 52/21, isonet 262/141, miss_alignment 185/110, cryocare 31/57, easymode 30/87, imod 282/139. napari already carries /opt/napari/requirements.lock (112 lines). (verified; conda locks and importlib.metadata inside the images)

(verified; /scratch/users/<maintainer>/container_verify/build_build-*.log 'building ... at' and 'built ... at' lines)
