- The nvcr.io CUDA devel bases carry the NVIDIA Deep Learning Container License. 1c permits distributing a Compatible derived container that includes the entire container plus other software, subject to section 2. 4c forbids distribution as a stand-alone product. 4g forbids use that makes the container subject to an open-source licence requiring redistribution at no charge. (verified; /NGC-DL-CONTAINER-LICENSE inside relion-5.0.1-...sif, lines 20-110)
- The AreTomo2 licence is not visible locally: the def deletes the source after building (warp-sm100 def:80-81). (verified; container_defs/warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:65-81)
- cisTEM on the share is a 4.3 GB native directory with simulate built 2024-04-02, version string 2.0.0-alpha-295-b21db55-dirty, and no licence or provenance file; crboost uses only simulate. (verified; ls/du/strings on /groups/<lab>/software/cisTEM/bin; services/templating/pdb_service.py:236,414-453)
- The easymode weights cache is 4.0 GB (hf/, models/, models.sha256, settings.txt), filled by `easymode download --all`; crboost reads models/registry.json and models.sha256. (verified; du -sh /groups/<lab>/software/cache_and_artifacts/easymode_1.2.5; services/segmentation/easymode.py:1-7,54-80)
- Headnode apptainer 1.5.4 supports --build-arg/--build-arg-file templating, push to oras:// and library:// URIs, and sign/verify. (verified; apptainer build --help; apptainer push --help; apptainer help)
- No current def needs a GPU at build time: conda CUDA solves use CONDA_OVERRIDE_CUDA, torch arch checks use torch._C._cuda_getArchFlags() (which works without a GPU), and pytom only checks the NVRTC version. (verified; warp-sm100 def:58-62; pytom-0.14 def:56-73; relion-sm100 def:117-121; isonet-sm100 def:65-66; tilt_filter def:23-24)
- No current def uses %files: every build input is fetched over the network (docker/nvcr.io base, GitHub, conda channels, PyPI, the PyTorch and warpem wheel indexes, bio3d.colorado.edu, cgl.ucsf.edu). (verified; grep -l %files container_defs/*.def (none); URLs in each def)
- Standard GitHub-hosted runners have roughly 14 GB of free disk by default, too little for the warp/relion/isonet builds (~35 GB peak) without cleanup or larger/self-hosted runners. (unverified; general knowledge, not checked in this lens)
- GHCR may limit the size of a single layer (~10 GB); an `apptainer push oras://` of a 12-13 GB SIF is a single blob and may be refused. (unverified; general knowledge, not checked in this lens)
- Apptainer def files support multi-stage builds (`Stage: <name>` and `%files from <stage>`), which allow compiling in a CUDA devel stage and shipping a slim final stage. (likely; general knowledge of the Apptainer definition-file format; not exercised here)

### Open questions

- Publish built SIFs at all, or only defs + committed locks + a build script? Recommended: publish SIFs only for images whose whole stack is clearly redistributable and small (napari, tilt_filter, imod, slimmed pymol, then pytom/isonet/cryocare/easymode/miss_alignment once rebased off the NVIDIA base). Ship warp and relion as defs + locks until they are multi-stage and their licences (AreTomo2, NGC) are read. Never publish ChimeraX.
- ChimeraX curation at other sites: build-it-yourself only, with an explicit licence-acceptance build arg, and documented as non-commercial? Recommended: yes. Also decide whether the CPU variant ships (recommended: no; template default -> GL image on a GPU partition, or curation off).
- miss_alignment: rebuild from the repo def (cuda-runtime) and verify it, or revert the def to the cuda-toolkit recipe that built the running image? Recommended: pin miss-alignment/torch-projectors/warpylib/starfile, rebuild with cuda-runtime, run the cluster probe and one Warp-bridge run on a B200, then make it the shipped image.
- Delete the four legacy defs (isonet2.def, pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def, relion5.0_tomo.def, warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def) from main? Recommended: yes, in the same change that rewrites docs/installation.md section 2 from the manifest.
- S5 design: compare a hash of the embedded def (`apptainer inspect --deffile`, works for every existing image without a rebuild), or add crboost.* %labels and rebuild? Recommended: the embedded-def hash as the check, plus crboost.tool/crboost.def labels for human readability on the next rebuild of each image. Warn, never block.
- Remove the personal email and the author names from defs and image labels (credit moves to CITATION/README)? Recommended: yes; the email is in relion-sm100 def:20 and in the running image's labels.
- PyMOL image: drop cryodrgn and the rest of the unused stack (8.9 GB -> <1 GB)? Recommended: yes, since crboost only calls pymol2.
- What are Munich's GPU models, NVIDIA driver version and host OS/glibc? Needed to confirm CUDA 12.9 images run there (driver floor), whether image_libs_first applies, and whether ubuntu 20.04 images (cryocare, imod, easymode) load their host GL. Recommended: ask before freezing the December image set.
- Where do the easymode weights (4.0 GB, changed in place upstream before) and the tilt-filter weights (D7) live for other sites? Recommended: a fetch script per artefact that checks committed sha256 lists; mirror the frozen easymode set once its licence allows; tilt-filter weights wait on the model author's licence (D7).
- Should crboost's wrapper pass --no-home, or set PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 for every image (today only tilt_filter and napari set it in their defs)? Recommended: set PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 in container_service._container_env for all tools; keep home bound, because tools write there.

## recon:hygiene: Repo hygiene and the public face

A stranger who opens the repo today finds a lab's working notebook more than a product. Of the 816 tracked files, 331 (41%, 4.5 MB) are developer notes: memory/ (174 assistant-memory files, 145 Claude session ids, a personal profile of the maintainer), docs/roadmaps (93 files, up to 228 KB each) and docs/reports (64 files, 40 of them scripts with the maintainer's home path). These also hold unpublished lab results (AGG412/412-capsid, including a 'paper_preliminary' report). Whether those stay public is the PI's call and should be made before any announcement. The user-facing docs (installation, architecture, agent, protocols) are nearly free of site details. Site specifics that matter for Munich sit in the config template and in UI strings: conf.template.yaml ships the B200 GRES type and the g_medium QOS for missAlign/denoisetrain, and preflight checks partitions but never QOS or GRES, so a Munich conf passes preflight and then fails at sbatch. Some job help texts also tell users about 'CBE g3/g4'. Three hygiene traps block the December deliverables: (1) .gitignore line 46 `/*.md` silently ignores CHANGELOG.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, SECURITY.md and CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md at the root (verified with git check-ignore); (2) `**env` ignores any path ending in 'env'; (3) static/molstar/embed.js is a 2.57 MB Mol* (MIT) bundle with no licence notice, built from a source folder that no longer exists, so it cannot be rebuilt from anything in the repo. The front page is a lab-internal user guide: lab paths at README lines 12-16/78/89, no 'what it is / what it needs', no screenshots (zero images tracked), no version, no citation, a one-line GitHub description, community health 50%, an empty wiki enabled, 6 stale branches, and colleague first names in 5 public issues. The repo lacks CHANGELOG, CITATION.cff, CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY, NOTICE and issue templates; the LICENSE is stock Apache-2.0 with no copyright line anywhere. Size is not a problem: the checkout is 21 MB, and a fresh main clone is ~36 MiB, 53% of it one deleted EMDB map in history. Rewriting history would break every existing install and every SHA reference to save ~19 MB, so it is not worth it. A secret scan of all 5,987 historical blobs found no credentials. The cheapest high-value moves, in order: move the notes out, rewrite .gitignore, add the standard files with NOTICE and the Mol* licence, de-site the template plus a QOS/GRES preflight check, rewrite the README top, and set the GitHub settings (private vulnerability reporting, branch protection, About, topics).

### Findings

**HY1 [high, effort M] Developer notes ship as the product: memory/, docs/roadmaps, docs/reports = 41% of tracked files**

memory/ has 174 files (0.84 MB): 107 project_*, 34 feedback_*, 31 reference_*, MEMORY.md, and user_<maintainer>.md, a personal profile of the maintainer ('supremely confused' by Warp, etc.). Those files carry 145 originSessionId fields. docs/roadmaps has 41 open + 52 completed files (3.0 MB, incl. HOTFIXES.md at 69 KB; roadmap_04-stage0-census.md alone is 228 KB). docs/reports has 64 files (0.62 MB; 40 are scripts). Together: 331 of 816 tracked files, 4.47 MB, touched by 280 of 913 commits. They contain 209 occurrences of the maintainer's home path (mostly docs/reports/*/scripts and completed/roadmap_04*), internal hostnames (clip-login-1.cbe..., ood.cbenext..., storage.vbc..., cbe-login-0.inst.atlas...), an internal IP (roadmap_55:52), session handoffs ('Claude's sandbox → the maintainer mounts or pastes'), and 154 'Claude' mentions in docs. memory/ is no longer the live auto-memory: <assistant home>/memory is now a real 42-file directory, and the repo copy is read by convention ('reference_in_repo_memory'). Coupling to watch when moving: 42 roadmap/report references in 26 code files (mostly comments: ui/dashboard/css.py 5, services/jobs/template_match.py 4, ...). One is a runtime message: drivers/driver_base.py:618 tells users to 'See docs/reports/orchestrator/jobnum-off-by-one-hypotheses.md'. docs/particle-data-flow.md has 4 roadmap refs and CLAUDE.md 3.

Evidence:
- git ls-files memory docs/roadmaps docs/reports | wc -l -> 331 of 816; du -> 4,466,999 bytes
- memory/user_<maintainer>.md:1-15
- git grep -c originSessionId -- memory -> 145
- git grep -h -oE '/users/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+' -> 209 x one home path
- docs/roadmaps/roadmap_55-napari-companion-through-ondemand.md:52 (internal IP)
- docs/reports/412-capsid/412-sta-refinement-playbook.md:736 ('Claude's sandbox')
- drivers/driver_base.py:583,618 (runtime pointer into docs/reports)
- services/job_links.py:15 (pointer into docs/roadmaps)

Recommendation: Move memory/, docs/roadmaps/ (incl. HOTFIXES.md and completed/) and docs/reports/ into a separate notes repo (private by default) cloned beside the checkout, e.g. ~/dev/crboost_notes; point the auto-memory 'in-repo memory' note at it. Keep only docs/*.md reference docs in the product repo. Before the move, promote what users need: the off-by-one report behind driver_base.py:618 becomes a docs/known_bugs.md entry, or the message is reworded. Strip roadmap/report pointers from code comments (the repo's own 'plain code comments' rule) and from docs/particle-data-flow.md. Lighter fallback if the maintainer wants the notes in-tree: add .gitattributes `memory/ export-ignore`, `docs/roadmaps/ export-ignore`, `docs/reports/ export-ignore`, so GitHub's git-archive release tarballs leave them out.

**HY2 [high, effort S] Unpublished lab results are public in docs and memory**

124 files in docs/ and memory/ mention AGG412/412 705 times: resolutions (e.g. 9.2 Å, 8.35 Å), sample names (MyrGFP), dataset paths under the lab group dir, comparisons with the PI's own processing. docs/reports/miss-alignment/agg412_grid2_paper_preliminary.md is named as pre-paper material. docs/reports/412-capsid/412-sta-refinement-playbook.md (98 KB) names the maintainer 12 times and records an agreed plan between the maintainer and the PI. 7 code files mention AGG412 in comments or examples (drivers/extract_candidates_pytom.py, services/configs/mdoc_service.py, services/jobs/tilt_filter.py, services/particles/warp_geometry.py, services/sta_kitchen/loupe.py, services/tilt_series/keep_rule.py, ui/analytics/readout_panel.py). The repo has been public since at least the September share, so this is already exposed in history; moving it out stops further exposure and keeps it off the announced front page.

Evidence:
- git grep -l -E 'AGG412|\b412\b' -- docs memory | wc -l -> 124 (705 hits)
- docs/reports/miss-alignment/agg412_grid2_paper_preliminary.md
- docs/reports/412-capsid/412-sta-refinement-playbook.md:16,25,567,698,913
- git grep -c -E 'AGG412|MyrGFP' -- services ui drivers -> 7 files

Recommendation: Ask the PI before the public announcement. Default: the reports go with HY1 into the notes repo; code comments use a neutral example name. Do not rewrite history for it unless the PI asks; say plainly that history keeps it.

**HY3 [high, effort S] Config template ships CBE.next-only GRES/QOS values that preflight cannot catch**

config/conf.template.yaml is copied into conf.yaml by preflight. Its live keys carry one cluster's values: missAlign gres 'gpu:nvidia_b200:1' (L92), qos 'g_medium' (L97 missAlign, L139 denoisetrain), curation partition 'c' (L265). preflight.py validates partitions against sinfo (L207-223) but has no QOS or GRES check (grep for qos|gres|sacctmgr in preflight.py returns nothing). So a Munich install passes preflight and then fails at sbatch ('Invalid qos specification' / invalid gres) the first time it runs missAlign or IsoNet/denoisetrain. The template comments name CBE.next 12 more times as examples (L17, 38, 69, 108, 119, 149, 159, 169, 185, 211, 218, 268); those are acceptable as examples but read as lab notes.

Evidence:
- config/conf.template.yaml:92
- config/conf.template.yaml:97
- config/conf.template.yaml:139
- config/conf.template.yaml:265
- preflight.py:207-223 (partitions only)
- grep -n -iE 'qos|gres|sacctmgr' preflight.py -> no output

Recommendation: Give the template generic values: gres 'gpu:1', qos '', partition ''. Move the CBE.next tuning into a worked example, either config/examples/cbe-next.yaml or a section of docs/installation.md, so Munich can add its own beside it. Extend preflight to check every configured qos against `sacctmgr -nP show qos format=Name` and every typed gres against `sinfo -h -o %G`. That keeps preflight's promise that a clean preflight means jobs will submit.

**HY4 [high, effort S] .gitignore blocks the standard root files and over-matches**

Line 46 `/*.md` (with !README.md/!CLAUDE.md) silently ignores CHANGELOG.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, SECURITY.md and CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md at the root, so `git add` of any of them does nothing without -f. Line 9 `**env` is equivalent to `*env` with no slash, so it matches any file or directory whose name ends in 'env', at any depth (drivers/conda_env/x.py is ignored). Lines 24-25 `--git-common-dir` are leftovers of a mistyped command. Line 49 `!munich_handoff_note.md` re-includes a file that no longer exists. Line 43 `promptbuilder.py` and lines 51-52 `.abacusai` are personal tool entries. Line 54 `**.sif` is just `*.sif`. .ruff_cache/ stays out of git only because ruff writes its own .gitignore inside it. No .gitattributes exists.

Evidence:
- git check-ignore -v --no-index CHANGELOG.md -> .gitignore:46:/*.md
- git check-ignore -v --no-index drivers/conda_env/x.py -> .gitignore:9:**env
- .gitignore:24-25
- .gitignore:43
- .gitignore:46-49
- .gitignore:51-52

Recommendation: Rewrite .gitignore: `/.env`, `/venv`, `/.uv/`, `__pycache__/`, `.pytest_cache/`, `.ruff_cache/`, `.nicegui/`, `/notes/`, `q_*.md`, `PLAN_*.md`, `REPORT_*.md`, `ISSUE_*.md`, `/config/conf.yaml`, `/config/qsub.sh`, `*.sif`, `*.pth`, `**/*.mrc` plus `!config/protocols/*/assets/*.mrc`, `projects/`, `auth/`. Drop `/*.md`, `**env`, `--git-common-dir`, `!munich_handoff_note.md`. Move promptbuilder.py and .abacusai to .git/info/exclude. Add .gitattributes: `static/molstar/embed.js linguist-vendored -diff` and `*.mrc binary`, plus the export-ignore lines from HY1 if the notes stay.

**HY5 [high, effort M] Mol* bundle shipped without its licence and without buildable source**

static/molstar/embed.js (2,570,931 bytes, minified Vite bundle, loaded by ui/template_workbench.py:99) bundles Mol*, which is MIT ('Copyright (c) 2017 - now, Mol* contributors'). MIT requires the notice in all copies or substantial portions. The bundle carries no Mol* header; only a few transitive MIT/BSD headers survive minification. Per memory/project_template_refactor_pr1.md:356-363, the bundle was built from a TypeScript project (Vite, molstar ^4.0.0) under the maintainer's home dir. That directory does not exist now, and no embed bridge.ts was found under ~/dev, so nothing in the repo can rebuild the 2.5 MB file. Three icons (static/icons/cross.svg, play.svg, stop.svg) and an inline SVG at ui/pipeline_builder/pipeline_constants.py:13 use the 15x15 geometry of Radix Icons (MIT), which would also need a notice; this is likely, not verified.

Evidence:
- static/molstar/embed.js (2,570,931 B; grep shows no 'Mol*' copyright)
- ui/template_workbench.py:99
- memory/project_template_refactor_pr1.md:356-363
- ls ~/dev/template-workbench -> missing (2026-11-19)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/molstar/molstar/master/LICENSE

Recommendation: Now: add static/molstar/LICENSE (Mol* MIT text) and list Mol* and its bundled packages in a THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES/NOTICE file. Then find the TS source (backups, other machines) and put it in-repo, e.g. web/molstar_embed/ with package.json, lockfile and a build script, recording the exact molstar version. If it is lost, plan a rebuild from a pinned molstar release. Confirm where the icons come from and add a notice, or redraw them.

**HY6 [high, effort M] Front page is a lab-internal user guide; GitHub About/settings are bare**

README.md:12-16 and :78 hardcode the lab's install path ('Klumpe lab: /groups/<lab>/software/crboost_server'), and :89 uses lab data paths in the example prompt. There is no one-paragraph 'what it is / for whom', no requirements block in the README (they are in docs/installation.md:6-9), no supported-cluster statement, no status/version, no screenshot (zero images tracked), no citation, no word on how this relates to CryoBoost v1 (the copia protocol cites the v1 tutorial). The Claude Code section comes before installation. The only badge is DeepWiki (README.md:1). On GitHub: description 'A headnode-centric implementation of cryoboost', no topics, no homepage, community profile health 50% (README+LICENSE only), wiki enabled but empty (the .wiki.git repo does not exist), 43 open issues of which ~24 are roadmap mirrors whose bodies carry /groups and /users paths. Issue titles still carry an informal '[v0.1]'/'[v0.2]' scheme (#86, #87, #89, #96), and #69 'Establish a release mechanism for the lab' has been open since 2026-03-03.

Evidence:
- README.md:1
- README.md:12-16
- README.md:78
- README.md:89
- docs/installation.md:6-9
- gh repo view -> description 'A headnode-centric implementation of cryoboost', repositoryTopics null, homepageUrl ''
- gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/community/profile -> health 50
- git ls-remote <repo>.wiki.git -> Repository not found
- gh issue list -> #69, #86, #87, #89, #96

Recommendation: Restructure the README: (1) a 3-line pitch and a screenshot or GIF of the workspace; (2) 'What it needs' (Linux headnode, SLURM, Apptainer/Singularity, GPU nodes, shared FS under one path, ~2 GB plus containers); (3) 'Status and versions' (latest tag, supported clusters: the lab's and the Munich site once its install passes); (4) quick start for a new site, which links installation.md; (5) 'Using an existing install', the current content with `<install dir>` in place of the lab path (the lab path moves to a site note or the startup banner); (6) Claude Code; (7) cite, licence, acknowledgements of the wrapped tools. Badges: CI, latest release, licence, DOI. Set the About description, topics (cryo-electron-tomography, cryo-et, slurm, apptainer, relion, warp, hpc, subtomogram-averaging) and homepage. Disable the empty wiki. Label the roadmap-mirror issues 'roadmap', or close them if the notes move out.

**HY7 [medium, effort S] Missing standard files for a public scientific tool**

The repo has no CHANGELOG.md, CITATION.cff, CONTRIBUTING.md, SECURITY.md, CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md, NOTICE/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES, .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE, PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE or CODEOWNERS, and no .github/ directory at all. SECURITY.md matters more than usual here: the server is a token-gated web app that submits SLURM jobs as the user, and it has an Agent API. GitHub private vulnerability reporting is off. There have been no PRs since 2026-09-09 (346 direct commits to main since 2026-10-01, 9 merges), so GitHub's PR-based auto-generated release notes would come out empty. The changelog has to be written by hand, from HOTFIXES entries and finished roadmaps.

Evidence:
- gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/.github -> 404
- gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/private-vulnerability-reporting -> enabled false
- gh pr list --state all -> last PR #110 2026-09-09
- git log main --since=2026-10-01 --no-merges | wc -l -> 346

Recommendation: Add CITATION.cff (GitHub then shows 'Cite this repository'; link the Zenodo DOI once releases are archived). Add CHANGELOG.md in Keep-a-Changelog form, hand-written per tag. Add CONTRIBUTING.md: dev setup via ./crboost, ruff, check_boundaries.py, check_look_literals.py, the exception/result idioms, and a pointer to CLAUDE.md. Add SECURITY.md: report privately via GitHub advisories; state the threat model (per-user server, token, localhost tunnel). Add NOTICE (see HY8/HY5). Add issue templates: a bug report that asks for `git describe`, `./crboost preflight` output, the conf excerpt and the job's run.out/run.err; a site-install question. Add a PR template with a lint checklist. CODE_OF_CONDUCT (Contributor Covenant) is optional but cheap. Note that HY4 must land first, or the root .md files will not be added.

**HY8 [medium, effort S] No copyright line anywhere; LICENSE is the untouched Apache template**

LICENSE is the full Apache-2.0 text (201 lines). Its appendix still reads 'Copyright [yyyy] [name of copyright owner]' (LICENSE:189). That is the standard how-to-apply appendix and legally fine to ship verbatim, but no file in the repo states who holds the copyright: there is no NOTICE, no SPDX headers, no copyright lines in sources. Apache-2.0 §4(d) makes NOTICE the conventional place for attributions, and it is also where the Mol* and other third-party notices (HY5) belong.

Evidence:
- LICENSE:189
- git grep -n -iE 'copyright|SPDX' -- services ui drivers api -> none

Recommendation: Keep LICENSE verbatim. Add NOTICE: 'CryoBoost Server. Copyright 2025-2026 <holder>. This product includes software developed by ... (Mol*, MIT; ...)'. Optionally add `# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` headers later; this is not needed for December.

**HY9 [medium, effort S] Names and personal info still in tracked files and public issues**

container_defs Author lines give the maintainer's and the PI's full names: cryocare.def:85, miss_alignment_torch2.8.0_cuda12.9.def:121, pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def:72, relion5.0_tomo.def:107. The maintainer's personal email is the Maintainer label in relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:20. Placeholder or AI authors: relion5.0_tomo.def:5 'Author Gemini', imod.def:130 and pymol.def:5 'Author YourName', isonet2.def:48 'Author User'. memory/user_<maintainer>.md is a personal profile. The maintainer's first name appears 12 times in docs/reports/412-capsid/412-sta-refinement-playbook.md, and memory/reference_cryoboost_v1_location.md:25 has home-dir paths. A user-visible tooltip names the lab: ui/tomo_dashboard_dialog.py:951 ('The Klumpe-lab Titan convention is -1'). On GitHub, colleague first names appear in the titles/comments of issues #69 (comment), #73, #91, #92 and #108. No swearing was found (grep for common curses -> 0).

Evidence:
- container_defs/cryocare.def:85
- container_defs/relion5.0_tomo.def:5
- container_defs/relion5.0_tomo.def:107
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:20
- container_defs/imod.def:130
- container_defs/pymol.def:5
- container_defs/isonet2.def:48
- ui/tomo_dashboard_dialog.py:951
- memory/user_<maintainer>.md:2
- gh issue list -> #73, #91, #92, #108 titles; #69 comment

Recommendation: Replace every container Author/Maintainer label with project labels: `Author CryoBoost maintainers`, `URL https://github.com/<org>/<repo>`, `Version`/`Tool` as in the newer defs. Personal credit goes in CITATION.cff (an author list is normal credit, not a breach of the roles rule). Reword the tooltip to 'a common Titan Krios convention is -1' or read it from config. Rename the five issues to use roles. memory/ leaves with HY1.

**HY10 [medium, effort S] Site-specific advice in user-visible strings and container recipes**

Code mentions CBE/CBE.next 56 times (mostly comments; acceptable but they read as lab notes). Some reach users directly: job help texts at services/jobs/segment.py:65 ('on CBE: g3 = RTX 6000, g4 = A100') and services/jobs/miss_align.py:499 ('on CBE.next'), QOS names in help/errors at miss_align.py:455 and :816 ('g_long'), and services/particles/analytics.py:626 (a cluster hostname as the docstring example). Container recipes hardcode `TZ=Europe/Vienna` (relion-5.0.1...:37, warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2...:23, warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1...:19) and describe build workarounds as 'CBE.next builds run with --fakeroot --ignore-fakeroot-command'. scripts/file_roadmap_issues.py:24-25 hardcodes the org/repo. The user-facing docs are clean: installation.md has 1 site ref, architecture/agent/protocols/binning have 0.

Evidence:
- services/jobs/segment.py:65
- services/jobs/miss_align.py:455
- services/jobs/miss_align.py:499
- services/jobs/miss_align.py:816
- services/particles/analytics.py:626
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:37
- container_defs/warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:23
- scripts/file_roadmap_issues.py:24-25

Recommendation: Reword user-visible strings generically ('a card with >= 24 GB', 'a longer QOS'), and take any per-site advice from conf.yaml. In defs, drop TZ or use UTC, and phrase workarounds as 'on hosts without a subuid range'. Leave comment-level CBE mentions where they explain a measured number, but name the hardware ('on B200 MIG 1g.23gb') rather than the cluster.

**HY11 [medium, effort S] Superseded container defs ship beside current ones; the install guide points at the old ones**

There are 15 defs for 11 tools. Four tools have two defs each: pytom (0.10.0 and 0.14.0_sm100), relion (5.0_tomo and 5.0.1-b16a35b_sm100), isonet2 (plain and a266ff4_sm100), warp (aretomo1 and aretomo2_sm100). docs/installation.md:58 and its table at :68-72 tell a new site to build the OLD ones. The settled decision is that a newer version upgrades the container in place, so the old defs are dead weight that misleads. Gaps: no easymode def (installation.md:73, 'not in the repo yet'); the template references chimerax_artiax.sif (conf.template.yaml:264) but only chimerax_artiax_GL.def exists; chimerax_artiax_GL.def and pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def have no %labels. The containers lens owns build and serving; this is the hygiene half.

Evidence:

- docs/installation.md:58
- docs/installation.md:68-73
- config/conf.template.yaml:264
- grep -c '^%labels' container_defs/chimerax_artiax_GL.def container_defs/pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def -> 0

Recommendation: Delete the four superseded defs (git history keeps them). Give the survivors stable names per tool key (container_defs/relion.def, warp_aretomo.def, ...) and carry the versions in %labels. Fix the installation table. Add the easymode def or say plainly it is separate. Add %labels everywhere (roadmap 39 S5).

All of these are free on public repos.

Recommended: a separate private notes repo cloned beside the checkout, with the auto-memory pointer updated, and only docs/*.md reference docs left in the product repo. Fallback: keep them in-tree with export-ignore so release tarballs exclude them.

- Keep CLAUDE.md public at the root? Recommended: yes. It is the best contributor and architecture guide in the repo. Fix its broken docs/picking_ui reference and link it from CONTRIBUTING.md.

## recon:containers_research: the same brief, re-run on its own after the first session stopped

GHCR is free for public packages, both storage and bandwidth, and GitHub promises at least one month's notice before that changes. It caps each layer at 10 GB and each upload at 10 minutes. `apptainer push oras://` stores a SIF as exactly one layer, so today's three largest SIFs (relion 13.4 GB, warp_aretomo 12.7, isonet2 12.4) cannot go to GHCR as they are. This corrects recon-2026-11-19.md:548, which found no documented cap. Recommended architecture: the canonical artefact per tool is a multi-layer OCI image on GHCR. It is built on free ubuntu-latest runners with docker buildx (registry cache on GHCR) as a two-stage build: devel stage, then a runtime or base stage. The CI job then converts it to a SIF and also pushes that via ORAS when the SIF is under about 9.5 GB. This is exactly what CZII's public relion-docker does for RELION 5.1 (sm_80..sm_120): 21 OCI layers (largest 4.6 GB), a 6.9 GB SIF, and builds of 15–107 minutes on the free 4-vCPU runner. Sites pull `oras://` (an exact copy, no conversion) or fall back to `docker://` (converted locally, layers cached). They pin by digest from a per-release lock file. GPU smoke tests run on the cluster with the existing `crboost test -m cluster` before a tag is promoted. They do not run on a self-hosted runner, which GitHub says should almost never be used with public repositories. Fallback: publish the .def plus a build script and let the site build its own image (needed anyway for ChimeraX); archive release SIFs on Zenodo (50 GB per record, a DOI per version); a site that mounts CVMFS can get the OCI images unpacked through a wishlist pull request to unpacked.cern.ch. Licensing gates publication more than size does. ChimeraX may not be redistributed. The CUDA EULA keeps developer tools such as nvcc for internal use, so ship images whose final stage is runtime or base, not devel. The NVIDIA Deep Learning Container licence allows derived containers that add real functionality and pass the terms on. The GPL tools need their corresponding source offered from the same place. cryocare.def and imod.def still pull from Anaconda's defaults channel and must move to Miniforge before anything is published. Provenance is cheap to adopt: digests, OCI labels, buildx provenance/SBOM, `actions/attest` (free for public repos) and keyless cosign. The 45 MB weights fit a GitHub release asset (under 2 GiB per file, no bandwidth limit) or Zenodo.

### Findings

**CR1 [blocker, effort M] GHCR caps a layer at 10 GB and an upload at 10 minutes, and an ORAS-pushed SIF is one layer: the 3 biggest SIFs cannot be pushed as they are**

GitHub's Container registry docs say: 'The Container registry has a 10 GB size limit for each layer' and 'a 10 minute timeout limit for uploads'. A community report confirms it: a layer over 10 GB keeps retrying, while a 9 GB layer goes through, and a 25 GiB image went through once split into 7–8 GiB layers. Apptainer pushes a SIF as an OCI artifact with a single layer of media type application/vnd.sylabs.sif.layer.v1.sif. Its ORAS download path rejects any manifest that does not have exactly one layer (`if len(manifest.Layers) != 1`). The SIF is already compressed squashfs, so the layer size equals the file size. relion (13.4 GB), warp_aretomo (12.7) and isonet2 (12.4) are over the cap. pymol (8.9), pytom (7.9) and miss_alignment (7.6) are under it, but each needs at least 13 MB/s sustained to finish inside the 10-minute timeout. That is easy from a GitHub runner (CZII pushes a 6.9 GB SIF from one routinely) and unknown from an HPC uplink. This corrects docs/reports/public-release/recon-2026-11-19.md:548 ('No documented GHCR per-layer size cap was found') and confirms the guess at :1280.

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/packages/working-with-a-github-packages-registry/working-with-the-container-registry (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/77429 (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/apptainer/apptainer/main/internal/pkg/client/oras/oras.go (seen 2026-11-19)
- docs/reports/public-release/recon-2026-11-19.md:548
- docs/reports/public-release/recon-2026-11-19.md:1280

Recommendation: Do not plan on pushing the current SIFs to GHCR over ORAS. Either shrink each image below about 9.5 GB with a two-stage build (CR3), or ship it as a multi-layer OCI image whose layers each stay under 10 GB (CR2). Sizes and push times come from CI, not from the lab's filesystem.

**CR2 [high, effort L] Recommended serving architecture: a multi-layer OCI image on GHCR is canonical, a SIF over ORAS is its exact-copy mirror, sites pull by digest**

Per tool: (1) A Dockerfile is built by docker buildx on a free ubuntu-latest runner and pushed to ghcr.io/<org>/<tool>:<toolversion>-cuda12.9 plus :sha-<commit>, with registry cache in ghcr.io/<org>/<tool>-buildcache (mode=max). (2) In the same workflow, `apptainer pull x.sif docker://ghcr.io/<org>/<tool>@sha256:...` and then `apptainer push x.sif oras://ghcr.io/<org>/<tool>-sif:<tag>` when the SIF is under about 9.5 GB. (3) A per-crboost-release lock file maps each tool to its OCI digest and SIF digest. (4) A site runs one pull step: oras:// is preferred because it gives a bit-exact SIF with no conversion and no rate limit concerns; docker:// is the fallback for images over the ORAS limit and is converted locally with layers cached. The SIF goes into the group's shared images directory that config/conf.yaml tools.*.container_path points at, which keeps the settled 'upgrade the container in place' model. (5) Before a tag is promoted (re-tagging an existing digest uploads nothing), the maintainer runs the existing cluster self-test (tests/test_cluster.py: one short SLURM job per configured tool, GPU checked) on that digest. CZII's czimaginginstitute/relion-docker runs this exact pattern for RELION 5.x CUDA images: build-push with buildx and GHCR registry cache, then publish-sif with eWaterCycle/setup-apptainer, `apptainer pull docker://` and `apptainer push oras://ghcr.io/...`. It also deletes superseded digests after 14 days and runs a Trivy scan. Measured from the public API: the RELION 5.1 amd64 image has 21 layers, the largest 4.58 GB, 8.85 GB compressed in total; its SIF on GHCR is one 6.92 GB layer. The fallback when a tool cannot be published (ChimeraX), or does not yet fit: publish the .def and a build script, and the site builds its own image (`apptainer build` with sudo or --fakeroot). For papers, freeze the release's SIFs on Zenodo with a DOI.

Evidence:
- https://github.com/czimaginginstitute/relion-docker (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/czimaginginstitute/relion-docker/blob/main/.github/workflows/dockerhub-relion.yml lines 155-180, 279-289 (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://ghcr.io/v2/czimaginginstitute/relion-sif/manifests/5.1-cuda12.8 (anonymous manifest read, seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/1.5/registry.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/latest/docker_and_oci.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- config/conf.template.yaml:360-401
- tests/test_cluster.py:1-8

Recommendation: Adopt OCI-on-GHCR as the canonical image with an ORAS SIF mirror. Ship a lock file of digests with every crboost release, and add a small 'pull containers' step to install/preflight that fetches by digest and checks %labels. Start with one image (pytom or warp, which compile nothing heavy) to prove the pipeline end to end before the big ones.

**CR3 [high, effort M] Two-stage builds (devel → runtime/base) get every image under the cap and remove nvcc from what is published**

CUDA 12.9.1 base images on Docker Hub, compressed amd64 sizes: devel-ubuntu22.04 5.67 GB, runtime 2.44 GB, base 0.14 GB. Five defs start FROM nvcr.io/nvidia/cuda:12.9.1-devel (relion, warp x2, pytom 0.14, isonet2 sm100), so about 3 GB of every image is the toolchain. Several of those tools bring their own CUDA libraries: the Warp conda package ('brings its own CUDA 12.9 runtime'), torch cu129 wheels (isonet2), and conda-forge cupy with cuda-version 12.9 (pytom). Those can end FROM cuda base or plain ubuntu, with only AreTomo2 compiled in a devel stage and copied across. RELION needs cudart/cufft at run time, so it ends FROM runtime, as CZII's Dockerfile.relion does (`FROM nvidia/cuda:${CUDA_VERSION}-devel-ubuntu22.04 AS build` ... `FROM ...-runtime-ubuntu22.04 AS runtime`, `COPY --from=build`). That yields the 6.9 GB SIF above against our 13.4 GB. Apptainer .def files support the same thing (`Stage:` plus `%files from <stage>`) if the .def stays the source. Squashfs compression is another lever (`--mksquashfs-args '-comp zstd'`), but Apptainer warns that non-gzip squashfs may not work on some installations, so it is second choice.

Evidence:
- https://hub.docker.com/v2/repositories/nvidia/cuda/tags/12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04 (and -runtime, -base; seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/czimaginginstitute/relion-docker/blob/main/Dockerfile.relion lines 4, 80, 100-106 (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/latest/definition_files.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/1.5/build_a_container.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:2
- container_defs/warp-2.0.0dev41_aretomo2-1.0.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:2,10,64-77
- container_defs/pytom-match-pick-0.14.0_cupy-13.6.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:2,57
- container_defs/isonet2-a266ff4_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:2,45

Recommendation: Rewrite the published images as two-stage builds whose final stage is runtime (RELION) or base/ubuntu (Warp, pytom, IsoNet2, miss_alignment, tilt_filter). Keep the in-%post verification lines (cuobjdump sm_100 checks, --help probes) as RUN steps in the final stage.

**CR4 [medium, effort M] The free GitHub-hosted runner is enough to build these images, provided disk is cleaned first and the registry, not the Actions cache, holds the build cache**

A standard runner for a public repo is Linux x64 with 4 CPU, 16 GB RAM and 14 GB of SSD as documented. In practice the root disk is larger: 84 GB with about 25–31 GB free, and up to more than 60 GB with preinstalled software removed (jlumbroso/free-disk-space frees about 31 GB in about 3 minutes; easimon/maximize-build-space merges / and /mnt with LVM). Other limits: 6 hours per job, 20 concurrent jobs on Free, 256 jobs per matrix, and 10 GB of Actions cache per repository (billed above that only if the limit is raised). Standard runners cost nothing for public repos. The 10 GB Actions cache is too small for CUDA layers, so use a buildx registry cache on GHCR instead (free storage), as CZII does. Evidence that it works: CZII's matrix of three RELION builds (one with 7 CUDA architectures) finishes in 15–34 minutes per job with a warm cache. The slowest successful uncached runs in its history took 90–107 minutes for the whole workflow, and SIF conversion plus ORAS push takes 12–18 minutes per image. Apptainer installs with eWaterCycle/setup-apptainer (Ubuntu 22.04/24.04/26.04, needs sudo, which hosted runners have). With sudo, fakeroot is not needed. Ubuntu 24.04's AppArmor restriction on unprivileged user namespaces only matters for unprivileged builds.

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/limits (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/jlumbroso/free-disk-space (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/easimon/maximize-build-space (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/eWaterCycle/setup-apptainer (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/admin/1.5/user_namespace.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- GitHub API: repos/czimaginginstitute/relion-docker/actions/runs/43819722537/jobs and runs/38794646366/jobs (read 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: Build each image in its own matrix job on ubuntu-latest. Free disk first, set APPTAINER_TMPDIR/CACHEDIR to $RUNNER_TEMP, and use a GHCR registry cache. Trigger on a tag, or on a change to that image's recipe (path filter), plus workflow_dispatch; no weekly rebuilds unless upstream branches are tracked. Pin third-party actions by SHA. Budget a first uncached build of up to about 2 hours for RELION; the rest are mostly downloads.

**CR5 [medium, effort S] Feasibility table per step (free tier, public repo)**

Step | Feasible? | Why. (a) Lint/parse the .def/Dockerfiles on every PR | yes | trivial, no secrets. (b) Build the small images (napari 0.5, chimerax 1.0, imod 1.2, tilt_filter 4.1, cryocare 6.8) on a free runner | yes | well within 14+ GB of disk, 6 h and 16 GB RAM; ChimeraX is built but never pushed (CR9). (c) Build RELION with CUDA for sm_70..sm_100 on a free runner | yes, with disk cleanup | CZII does 7 architectures in 15–34 minutes warm, about 1.5 hours cold. (d) Build the Warp/pytom/IsoNet2/miss_alignment conda images | yes | mostly downloads; set CONDA_OVERRIDE_CUDA (already in the defs). (e) Push a multi-layer OCI image to GHCR | yes | each layer under 10 GB (the CUDA devel base alone is 5.67 GB in several layers); free storage. (f) Push a SIF under 9.5 GB over ORAS to GHCR | yes | a single blob; CZII pushes 6.9 GB. (g) Push a 12–13.4 GB SIF over ORAS to GHCR | no | 10 GB layer cap (CR1). (h) Docker-to-SIF conversion in CI | yes | about 12–18 minutes per image; needs about 3x the image size of free disk (estimate). (i) GPU smoke test in CI | no on the free tier | no GPU on standard runners; the GPU larger runner is $0.052/min and only for Team/Enterprise orgs; use the cluster self-test instead. (j) Self-hosted runner on the cluster for builds or GPU tests | technically yes, not advised | GitHub: 'should almost never be used for public repositories' (CR13). (k) Signing, attestation, SBOM | yes | free for public repos; Sigstore public-good instance. (l) Weights (45 MB) as a release asset | yes | under 2 GiB per file, no bandwidth limit. (m) A split 13 GB SIF as release assets | possible, ugly | 2 GiB per file, 1000 assets per release; only a last resort.

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/actions-runner-pricing (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/packages/working-with-a-github-packages-registry/working-with-the-container-registry (seen 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: Use this table to scope the CI roadmap step: (a), (b), (e), (f), (h) and (k) in December. (c) and (d) follow once the two-stage rewrites (CR3) are done. (i) and (j) stay on the cluster through the self-test.

**CR6 [medium, effort S] Docker Hub is a poor primary for HPC: anonymous pulls are rate-limited per IP, and a cluster's nodes often share one NAT address**

Docker Hub pull limits per 6 hours: 100 per IPv4 address (or IPv6 /64) unauthenticated, 200 for free personal accounts, unlimited for paid. Apptainer's own docs warn about Docker Hub API limits on docker:// runs and recommend pulling once to a SIF. The Docker-Sponsored Open Source programme removes the limits for all users pulling from the project's namespace. It requires public source, OSI licensing and 'no pathway to commercialization', and must be renewed yearly; one forum report questions how fully the exemption applies. GHCR publishes no pull rate limit (absence of documentation, not a guarantee). Quay.io says it does not restrict anonymous pulls except in severe abuse cases.

Evidence:
- https://docs.docker.com/docker-hub/usage/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.docker.com/docker-hub/repos/manage/trusted-content/dsos-program/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://forums.docker.com/t/which-images-have-no-pull-limits/139959 (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://access.redhat.com/articles/5531191 (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/latest/docker_and_oci.html (seen 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: GHCR is primary. If a second registry is wanted for resilience, mirror to Quay.io (no pull limits, free public repos) rather than Docker Hub. Either way, sites pull once into a shared directory and never `apptainer exec docker://` from jobs.

**CR7 [medium, effort S] Other homes: Sylabs is too small; Zenodo is for archiving; Hugging Face suits the weights, not the images; Harbor or S3 only if a site hosts them**

Sylabs SCS Library free tier: 11 GiB of storage in one project, plus 500 remote-build minutes per 30 days (60 minutes per build max); the figures are dated but no newer ones were found. Apptainer has no remote build. That cannot hold even relion alone. Zenodo: 50 GB and 100 files per record by default, with a one-time increase to 200 GB on request; a DOI per version plus a concept DOI. Good for freezing the exact SIFs of a release or paper, not for frequent updates. Hugging Face Hub: public storage is 'best-effort' for free accounts and meant for AI artefacts; files up to 500 GB hard limit (under 200 GB recommended). It fits the 45 MB tilt-filter weights, not a container registry role. GitLab.com: the registry is excluded from the 10 GiB per-project repository limit, but a planned 5 GiB per-namespace Free limit that would include it is listed in older docs and its status is unclear, so it is a risky home. Harbor (self-hosted, OCI and ORAS) is what I2PC uses for Scipion's prebuilt Apptainer images, refreshed quarterly; it is only an option if a site runs it. Institutional S3 or HTTPS works for plain SIF downloads but needs someone to own it. Comparable projects: CZII relion-docker (GHCR OCI + ORAS SIF + Docker Hub); napari (ghcr.io/napari/napari); Rosalind Franklin Institute vEM containers (Quay.io, run with apptainer docker://); BioContainers (quay.io/biocontainers mirrored to SIFs at depot.galaxyproject.org and the CVMFS repo singularity.galaxyproject.org); Scipion (Harbor + ORAS); SBGrid (its own licensed distribution, not public images). No official images were found for WarpTools, pytom-match-pick, membrain-seg or TomoTwin; they ship via conda or pip.

Evidence:
- https://docs.sylabs.io/guides/latest/user-guide/cloud_library.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://sylabs.io/2022/08/singularity-container-workflow-part-1-introduction-to-singularity-container-services/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://support.zenodo.org/help/en-gb/1-upload-deposit/80-what-are-the-size-limitations-of-zenodo (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/storage-limits (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.gitlab.com/user/storage_usage_quotas (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/I2PC/scipion-containers (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://napari.github.io/howtos/docker.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://rosalindfranklininstitute.github.io/volume-em-container-documentation/software/napari (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/BioContainers/singularity-build-bot (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.galaxyproject.org/en/latest/admin/special_topics/mulled_containers.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://www.sbgrid.org/news/separately-licensed-software-supported-by-sbgrid (seen 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: GHCR for images, Zenodo for frozen release snapshots (one record per tool per release, each under 50 GB), and a GitHub release asset or Zenodo for the weights. Skip Sylabs and GitLab.

**CR8 [low, effort S] CVMFS route: unpacked.cern.ch can ingest public OCI images through a GitHub wishlist pull request, if the collaborating site mounts it**

unpacked.cern.ch is a public CernVM-FS repository that stores container images unpacked, deduplicated across images and fetched on demand. Apptainer runs them straight from /cvmfs/unpacked.cern.ch/<registry>/<image>:<tag>. Images are added with a pull request to the external wishlist (github.com/cvmfs/images-unpacked.cern.ch, recipe.yaml), which accepts full registry URLs and tag wildcards. Ingestion takes about 15 minutes and updates are polled. The external wishlist already carries non-HEP entries, including ghcr.io, quay.io and CUDA/NVIDIA images (e.g. an AlphaFold image and nvcr.io tritonserver). DUCC ingests OCI images, not SIFs, so this route depends on CR2's OCI images existing. Our cluster runs CVMFS for EESSI, but whether the collaborating site mounts cern.ch repositories is unknown. Lazy loading suits 12 GB images whose jobs touch only a fraction of the files.

Evidence:
- https://cvmfs.readthedocs.io/en/2.14/cpt-containers/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://www.eessi.io/docs/training-events/2025/tutorial-best-practices-cvmfs-hpc/containers/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/cvmfs/images-unpacked.cern.ch/blob/master/recipe.yaml (read via API 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: Not for December. Once the OCI images exist, ask the collaborating site whether it mounts unpacked.cern.ch; if so, one wishlist PR gives both sites a zero-copy install.

**CR9 [blocker, effort S] ChimeraX cannot be redistributed: publish chimerax_artiax_GL.def only, and make the site's build accept the licence explicitly**

The UCSF ChimeraX Non-Commercial License says the licensee 'shall not distribute or transfer the Software or Improvements to any person or third parties without prior written permission', and that a request for a copy must be referred to the UCSF ChimeraX page. Plugins (such as ArtiaX) may be distributed. A UCSF developer confirmed in Oct 2023: 'The ChimeraX license does not allow redistribution' (exceptions such as SBGrid were granted case by case). He also advised against containerized or remote-display use. Our def accepts the licence automatically by POSTing choice=Accept to the UCSF download CGI during %post. When a site builds the image itself, the person running the build accepts the licence. When CI builds and publishes it, that is redistribution. I2PC's Scipion images likewise leave ChimeraX (and CryoSPARC, PHENIX) out because they 'can not be legally bundled'.

Evidence:
- https://www.rbvi.ucsf.edu/chimerax/docs/license.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://mail.cgl.ucsf.edu/mailman/archives/list/chimerax-users@cgl.ucsf.edu/thread/NNRAISWVDVBWVI33OAI3LAD53ZT6RU3E/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/I2PC/scipion-containers (seen 2026-11-19)
- container_defs/chimerax_artiax_GL.def:106-122

Recommendation: Never push a ChimeraX image. CI may build it to check the recipe, but must not upload it. Gate the licence step on a build argument the builder sets on purpose (e.g. --build-arg chimerax_accept_license=yes, failing otherwise) and say so in the site install doc.

**CR10 [high, effort S] CUDA licensing: a derived image may be redistributed under NVIDIA's container licence, but the CUDA EULA keeps nvcc for internal use, so publish images with a runtime or base final stage**

nvidia/cuda images are 'governed by' the NVIDIA Deep Learning Container License. Its §1(c) permits you to 'Develop and extend the CONTAINER to create a Compatible derived CONTAINER' and 'distribute such derived CONTAINER to run applications, subject to the distribution requirements'. Those requirements are §2(a) material additional functionality and §2(c) distribution under terms at least as protective. §4(c) forbids distributing the container 'as a stand-alone product', so the bare NVIDIA base must not be mirrored. The CUDA Toolkit EULA (12.9.1 and current 13.5) adds: 'Unless a developer tool is identified in this Agreement as distributable, it is delivered for your internal use only'. Attachment A lists runtime libraries (cudart, cuBLAS, cuFFT, NVRTC, nvJitLink, the driver libs libcuda.so and libnvidia-ptxjitcompiler.so, many static libs) but not nvcc. Older EULA text found by search tied driver-library redistribution to Docker Hub or NGC; the 12.9.1 and current texts have no such clause. So devel-derived public images are a grey zone, and runtime/base-derived ones are the conventional and safer form. This is also what CZII ships.

Evidence:
- https://hub.docker.com/r/nvidia/cuda (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://scancode-licensedb.aboutcode.org/nvidia-dlc-2021.LICENSE (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.1/eula/index.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/eula/index.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/does-the-cuda-docker-container-license-allow-distribution/286430 (seen 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: With CR3, publish only images whose final stage is runtime or base. Put NVIDIA's licence notices in /opt/licenses, and add a NOTICE to the README that the images contain NVIDIA software under NVIDIA's terms. Do not mirror unmodified nvidia/cuda images. This is not legal advice; if the institute has a tech-transfer office, a one-line confirmation is cheap.

**CR11 [medium, effort S] The GPL tools in the images need their corresponding source offered from the same place**

Licences from GitHub/PyPI metadata (read 2026-11-19): RELION GPL-2.0; Warp GPL-3.0; IsoNet2 GPL-3.0 (IsoNet v1 was MIT); pytom-match-pick GPL-2.0; easymode GPL-3.0; IMOD mostly GPL-2.0 with exceptions (LAPACK BSD-style, HDF5). Permissive: AreTomo2 and AreTomo3 BSD-3-Clause; cryoCARE BSD; miss-alignment BSD-3-Clause; napari BSD-3; membrain-seg BSD; TomoTwin MPL-2.0; cisTEM Janelia licence (BSD-like); Open-Source PyMOL a permissive notice-retaining licence. GPLv2 §3 says that when object code is distributed by 'offering access to copy from a designated place', offering equivalent access to the source 'from the same place' counts as distributing the source. So publishing the pinned source tarballs, or at least the exact commits, next to each image satisfies it. A bare upstream link is weaker under GPLv2 than under GPLv3 §6(d). CZII records the RELION commit in /opt/licenses/RELION-SOURCE-COMMIT.txt and keeps a THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md.

Evidence:
- https://github.com/3dem/relion (licence via API, 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/warpem/warp (licence via API, 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/IsoNet-cryoET/IsoNet2 (licence via API, 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/SBC-Utrecht/pytom-match-pick (licence via API, 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/mgflast/easymode/blob/main/LICENSE.txt (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://bio3d.colorado.edu/imod/source.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/czimaginginstitute/AreTomo2 (licence via API, 2026-11-19)
- https://pypi.org/pypi/miss-alignment/json (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/timothygrant80/cisTEM/blob/master/COPYING (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/schrodinger/pymol-open-source/blob/master/LICENSE (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://www.gnu.org/licenses/old-licenses/gpl-2.0.html (section 3, read via the PyPI text 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: Each image gets /opt/licenses/ (upstream licence texts plus SOURCE-COMMIT files), and the release attaches or links source tarballs of the GPL tools at the pinned commits. Add a THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md next to the recipes.

**CR12 [high, effort S] cryocare.def and imod.def still install Miniconda and accept Anaconda's defaults-channel ToS: switch to Miniforge before publishing**

cryocare.def:22,32 and imod.def:54,65 download Miniconda3 and run `conda tos accept` for repo.anaconda.com/pkgs/main and pkgs/r. Anaconda's terms make the defaults channel free only for organisations under 200 employees. Educational use at accredited universities is exempt, but an earlier Anaconda statement says 'non-university research entities and national laboratories' are not covered. Anaconda also says mirroring or redistributing significant portions of its repository needs a commercial licence. A public image built from defaults ties every collaborating site to that question. The newer defs (RELION, IsoNet2, Warp, pytom, miss_alignment) already use Miniforge with conda-forge only; the RELION def's header says so.

Evidence:
- container_defs/cryocare.def:22,32
- container_defs/imod.def:54,65
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:11
- https://www.anaconda.com/pricing/terms-of-service-faqs (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://www.anaconda.com/blog/is-conda-free (seen 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: Port cryocare and imod to Miniforge with conda-forge only, as already done for the CUDA 12.9 defs. Check that conda-forge carries what cryoCARE's TF 2.4/cudatoolkit 11.0 pins need, or move the image to a newer TF. Add a CI grep that fails on repo.anaconda.com in any recipe.

**CR13 [high, effort S] Do not attach a self-hosted cluster runner to the public repo: run GPU checks on the cluster with a pull-based self-test**

GitHub's secure-use reference says self-hosted runners 'should almost never be used for public repositories'. The reason: they 'can be persistently compromised by untrusted code in a workflow', and 'any user can open pull requests against the repository and compromise the environment', which on an HPC headnode means a shared filesystem and SLURM under a real user's account. Mitigations exist (just-in-time ephemeral runners, runner groups, approval for outside collaborators' fork PRs), but GitHub notes that environment protection does not isolate a self-hosted runner. Standard runners have no GPU. The GPU larger runner ($0.052/min) is only for Team or Enterprise Cloud organisations and is billed even for public repos. The repo already has the right tool: tests/test_cluster.py submits one short SLURM job per configured tool and checks the GPU.

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/actions-runner-pricing (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/runners/larger-runners (seen 2026-11-19)
- tests/test_cluster.py:1-8

Recommendation: Promotion flow: CI pushes :sha-<commit>. The maintainer, or the site admin, pulls that digest on the cluster and runs `crboost test -m cluster` against it, then re-tags it to the release tag (a re-tag uploads nothing). If a runner is ever wanted, put it in a separate private repo that only consumes tags from the public one.

**CR14 [medium, effort S] Cheap provenance: digests, OCI labels, buildx provenance and SBOM, actions/attest, keyless cosign, SIF %labels**

Artifact attestations are 'available in public repositories for all current GitHub plans'. They use the Sigstore public-good instance with a public transparency log and are verified with `gh attestation verify`. actions/attest-build-provenance is now a wrapper over actions/attest. buildx already emits provenance attestation manifests by default; CZII's relion index carries an 'attestation-manifest' entry. Keyless cosign stores signatures as OCI 1.1 referrers. Cosign signs by registry digest, so it should also cover the ORAS SIF artifact, but that was not tested; apptainer pull does not check cosign, so verification is a separate step on the site. Apptainer's own SIF signing is PGP by default, with PEM-key support. GHCR links a package to its repository through the org.opencontainers.image.source label, and the package then inherits the repo's access permissions. Today's %labels are uneven: placeholders in imod.def:130 ('Author YourName'), pymol.def:5 and isonet2.def:48 ('Author User'), and a personal maintainer email in the relion def's %labels (:20). Those labels become public metadata.

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/artifact-attestations (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://github.com/actions/attest-build-provenance (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.sigstore.dev/cosign/signing/signing_with_containers/ (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/1.3/signNverify.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/packages/learn-github-packages/connecting-a-repository-to-a-package (seen 2026-11-19)
- container_defs/imod.def:130
- container_defs/pymol.def:5
- container_defs/isonet2.def:48
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:20

Recommendation: December minimum: digest-pinned lock file, standard OCI labels (source, revision, version, licenses) mirrored in SIF %labels, buildx provenance and SBOM on, and actions/attest on the pushed digests. Add keyless cosign once a site asks for it. Replace placeholder and personal labels with a project contact.

**CR15 [medium, effort S] NVIDIA deletes CUDA image tags after an update or EOL: pin the base by digest, and treat the published images as the reproducible artefact**

The nvidia/cuda page says tags 'will be deleted Six Months after' the last supported recommended driver goes EOL or a newer update release appears for the same CUDA version. All current defs use `From: nvcr.io/nvidia/cuda:12.9.1-devel-ubuntu22.04` (or 11.8/12.1/12.2 in the legacy defs) by tag, and they also fetch 'latest' Miniforge/Miniconda installers. So a rebuild in a year may fail or come out different. Mirroring the bare NVIDIA base is not an option (CR10 §4(c)), so the published derived images are the long-term reference, with Zenodo snapshots for papers.

Evidence:
- https://hub.docker.com/r/nvidia/cuda (seen 2026-11-19)
- container_defs/relion-5.0.1-b16a35b_torch-2.8.0_cuda12.9_sm100.def:2,75
- container_defs/cryocare.def:2
- container_defs/isonet2.def:2

Recommendation: Pin FROM by @sha256 digest and pin the Miniforge installer version. Keep superseded image digests on GHCR for at least as long as a crboost release that references them is supported. Do not adopt CZII's 14-day deletion policy for tool images that crboost lock files point at.

**CR16 [low, effort S] Recipe inventory needs pruning before CI: legacy duplicates, a missing easymode recipe, private-by-default packages**

container_defs/ holds 15 recipes, including superseded ones: relion5.0_tomo.def (CUDA 12.2), isonet2.def (CUDA 12.1), pytom_match_pick_0.10.0.def and warp_2.0.0dev41_aretomo1.0.0_cuda12.9.def next to their sm100 successors. easymode (5.8 GB image, GPL-3.0) has no recipe in the repo. GHCR detail: 'When you first publish a package, the default visibility is private', and 'Once you make a package public, you cannot make it private again'. Each new package therefore needs one manual flip to public (or org settings) before sites can pull anonymously.

Evidence:
- container_defs/ (listing 2026-11-19)
- https://docs.github.com/en/packages/learn-github-packages/configuring-a-packages-access-control-and-visibility (seen 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: Mark the legacy recipes as legacy, or move them out, so the CI matrix is the published set. Write an easymode recipe or leave easymode out of the public release. Name packages once (ghcr.io/<org>/crboost-<tool>) and flip each to public on first push.

**CR17 [low, effort S] Sites pulling with docker:// need scratch space and a shared cache, and get a SIF that differs from ours**

Apptainer's docker:// pull 'extracts the layers ... and creates a SIF file from them', which is a build behind the scenes. 'Pulling the same Docker image twice gives different files', while 'A SIF pulled from oras:// or library:// is an exact copy'. The converted SIF lands in the Apptainer cache, which defaults to $HOME. On the free runner, CZII's pull, conversion and push takes about 12–18 minutes per RELION image. Expect similar times on a headnode, plus temporary disk of roughly 2–3 times the image size (estimate, not measured). Our headnode's Apptainer is 1.5.4 (recon :1276).

Evidence:
- https://apptainer.org/docs/user/latest/docker_and_oci.html (seen 2026-11-19)
- docs/reports/public-release/recon-2026-11-19.md:1276
- GitHub API: czimaginginstitute/relion-docker publish-sif job timings (read 2026-11-19)

Recommendation: The site pull step uses oras:// when the image has a SIF. Otherwise it sets APPTAINER_CACHEDIR/TMPDIR to group scratch and pulls once into the shared images directory. Record the source digest in a sidecar file, since the SIF hash will not match ours.

**CR18 [low, effort S] DL tilt-filter weights (45 MB): a GitHub release asset or Zenodo, fetched by checksum; the licence is still open**

GitHub release assets: 'Each file included in a release must be under 2 GiB', with no limit on total size or bandwidth and up to 1000 assets per release. Zenodo gives a DOI per version, which makes the weights citable. Hugging Face Hub also works (free public storage, a model card) but is a third place to maintain. The weights are git-ignored today and copied by hand; conf.template.yaml describes converting a checkpoint into the configured path inside the tilt_filter image.

Evidence:
- https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://support.zenodo.org/help/en-gb/1-upload-deposit/80-what-are-the-size-limitations-of-zenodo (seen 2026-11-19)
- https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/storage-limits (seen 2026-11-19)
- config/conf.template.yaml:322-336

Recommendation: Publish the converted weights as a Zenodo record (DOI) and attach the same file to the crboost release. The installer downloads it and checks a sha256 kept in the repo. Settle who owns the weights and under what licence first.

### Facts

- GitHub Packages is free for public packages; Container registry storage and bandwidth are 'currently free', with at least one month's notice before any change. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-packages)
- The GitHub Container registry has a 10 GB size limit for each layer and a 10-minute timeout for uploads; public images can be pulled anonymously. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/packages/working-with-a-github-packages-registry/working-with-the-container-registry)
- Apptainer's ORAS client requires exactly one layer of SIF media type in a pushed manifest (download rejects len(manifest.Layers) != 1), so a SIF is pushed as a single blob. (verified; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/apptainer/apptainer/main/internal/pkg/client/oras/oras.go)
- GHCR does not document a pull rate limit; a figure of 5,000 requests per hour appears only in secondary blogs. (unverified; https://docs.github.com/en/packages/working-with-a-github-packages-registry/working-with-the-container-registry)
- A first-published GHCR package is private; once made public it cannot be made private again; the org.opencontainers.image.source label links a package to a repo. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/packages/learn-github-packages/configuring-a-packages-access-control-and-visibility)
- A standard GitHub-hosted runner for public repos is Linux x64/arm64 with 4 CPU, 16 GB RAM and 14 GB SSD; private repos get 2 CPU and 8 GB. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners)
- In practice a hosted runner's root disk is about 84 GB with about 25–31 GB free; removing preinstalled software frees about 31 GB in about 3 minutes, and LVM-merging / and /mnt can give more than 60 GB. (likely; https://github.com/easimon/maximize-build-space)
- Hosted jobs run up to 6 h each (self-hosted up to 5 days); Free plan has 20 concurrent standard jobs; a matrix can have 256 jobs; Actions cache is 10 GB per repo. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/limits)
- Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free in public repositories; larger runners are billed even for public repositories and are only for Team or Enterprise Cloud organisations; the Linux 4-core GPU runner is $0.052/min. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/actions-runner-pricing)
- The self-hosted runner platform fee announced for March 2026 was postponed; public-repo runner usage was always to stay free. (likely; https://github.blog/changelog/2025-12-16-coming-soon-simpler-pricing-and-a-better-experience-for-github-actions/)
- GitHub says self-hosted runners 'should almost never be used for public repositories' because untrusted PR code can persistently compromise them. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use)
- eWaterCycle/setup-apptainer installs Apptainer on Ubuntu 22.04/24.04/26.04 x64 runners using sudo, with caching. (verified; https://github.com/eWaterCycle/setup-apptainer)
- Apptainer .def files support multi-stage builds (Stage: and %files from <stage>) and build-time templating (%arguments, --build-arg). (verified; https://apptainer.org/docs/user/latest/definition_files.html)
- Apptainer cannot push Docker/OCI multi-layer images; its only push to OCI registries is a SIF over oras://. (verified; https://apptainer.org/docs/user/1.5/registry.html)
- apptainer pull docker:// converts layers into a SIF (a build); pulling twice gives different files; a SIF pulled via oras:// is an exact copy. (verified; https://apptainer.org/docs/user/latest/docker_and_oci.html)
- CZII's relion-docker publishes RELION 5.x CUDA images to GHCR (OCI) and Docker Hub, and SIFs via oras://ghcr.io/czimaginginstitute/relion-sif. Images are built on ubuntu-latest with buildx and a GHCR registry cache, as a devel to runtime two-stage Dockerfile, and rebuilt weekly. (verified; https://github.com/czimaginginstitute/relion-docker)
- CZII's RELION 5.1-cuda12.8 amd64 OCI image has 21 layers, the largest 4.58 GB, 8.85 GB compressed in total; its SIF on GHCR is one 6.92 GB layer. (verified; https://ghcr.io/v2/czimaginginstitute/relion/manifests/5.1-cuda12.8)
- CZII's RELION build jobs take 15–34 min with a warm cache on free runners; the slowest successful workflow runs took 90–107 min; SIF conversion plus ORAS push takes 12–18 min per image. (verified; https://github.com/czimaginginstitute/relion-docker/actions)
- Compressed amd64 sizes of nvidia/cuda 12.9.1 Ubuntu 22.04 images: devel 5.67 GB, runtime 2.44 GB, base 0.14 GB. (verified; https://hub.docker.com/r/nvidia/cuda)
- nvidia/cuda tags are deleted six months after the last supported recommended driver goes EOL or a newer update release appears for the same CUDA version. (verified; https://hub.docker.com/r/nvidia/cuda)
- nvidia/cuda images are governed by the NVIDIA Deep Learning Container License, which permits distributing a Compatible derived container with material additional functionality under terms at least as protective, and forbids distributing the container as a stand-alone product. (likely; https://scancode-licensedb.aboutcode.org/nvidia-dlc-2021.LICENSE)
- CUDA EULA (12.9.1 and 13.5): developer tools not identified as distributable are for internal use only; Attachment A lists runtime libraries, NVRTC/nvvm, libcuda.so and libnvidia-ptxjitcompiler.so as distributable, not nvcc; neither version has a Docker Hub/NGC-only condition. (verified; https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.9.1/eula/index.html)
- The UCSF ChimeraX licence forbids distributing the software to third parties without prior written permission (plugins may be distributed); a UCSF developer confirmed redistribution is not allowed, with case-by-case exceptions such as SBGrid. (verified; https://www.rbvi.ucsf.edu/chimerax/docs/license.html)
- Licences: RELION GPL-2.0, Warp GPL-3.0, IsoNet2 GPL-3.0, pytom-match-pick GPL-2.0, easymode GPL-3.0, AreTomo2 and AreTomo3 BSD-3-Clause, cryoCARE BSD, miss-alignment BSD-3-Clause, napari BSD-3-Clause, membrain-seg BSD, TomoTwin MPL-2.0, cisTEM Janelia licence 1.2 (BSD-like), Open-Source PyMOL permissive notice-retaining licence. (verified; https://api.github.com/repos/3dem/relion (and the other repos' licence endpoints, PyPI JSON))
- IMOD has been GPL-2.0 open source since 4.0.26, with exceptions for bundled components (LAPACK-derived code, HDF5). (likely; https://bio3d.colorado.edu/imod/source.html)
- Anaconda's defaults channel is free only for organisations under 200 employees; accredited-university curricular and research use is exempt; non-university research entities were stated as not exempt; mirroring or redistributing the repository requires a commercial licence. (likely; https://www.anaconda.com/pricing/terms-of-service-faqs)
- Docker Hub pull limits per 6 h: 100 per IPv4 address or IPv6 /64 (unauthenticated), 200 for free personal accounts, unlimited for paid accounts. (verified; https://docs.docker.com/docker-hub/usage/)
- The Docker-Sponsored Open Source programme removes pull rate limits for everyone pulling the project's public images; it requires OSI-licensed public source and no commercialisation path, and must be renewed yearly. (likely; https://docs.docker.com/docker-hub/repos/manage/trusted-content/dsos-program/)
- Quay.io does not limit public repositories or anonymous pulls except for severe abuse. (likely; https://access.redhat.com/articles/5531191)
- Sylabs SCS Library free tier: 11 GiB of storage in one project, plus 500 remote-build minutes per 30 days (max 60 min per build); Apptainer has no remote build. (likely; https://sylabs.io/2022/08/singularity-container-workflow-part-1-introduction-to-singularity-container-services/)
- Zenodo: 50 GB and 100 files per record by default, a one-time increase to 200 GB; a DOI per version plus a concept DOI. (likely; https://support.zenodo.org/help/en-gb/1-upload-deposit/80-what-are-the-size-limitations-of-zenodo)
- Hugging Face Hub: free public storage is best-effort; single files have a 500 GB hard limit (under 200 GB recommended); 100 GB of private storage for free accounts. (verified; https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/storage-limits)
- GitHub release assets must each be under 2 GiB, with no limit on total release size or bandwidth and up to 1000 assets per release. (verified; https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases)
- unpacked.cern.ch takes images through a GitHub wishlist PR (cvmfs/images-unpacked.cern.ch), publishes them in about 15 minutes, and Apptainer runs them directly from /cvmfs; the external wishlist includes ghcr.io and non-HEP images. (verified; https://cvmfs.readthedocs.io/en/2.14/cpt-containers/)
- BioContainers mirrors quay.io/biocontainers to SIFs at depot.galaxyproject.org/singularity and the CVMFS repository singularity.galaxyproject.org. (verified; https://github.com/BioContainers/singularity-build-bot)
- Scipion serves prebuilt Apptainer images from a self-hosted Harbor registry with ORAS, refreshed about quarterly, and leaves out tools that cannot legally be bundled (ChimeraX, CryoSPARC, PHENIX). (verified; https://github.com/I2PC/scipion-containers)
- napari publishes Docker images at ghcr.io/napari/napari (and napari-xpra). (verified; https://napari.github.io/howtos/docker.html)
- No official container images were found for WarpTools, pytom-match-pick, membrain-seg or TomoTwin; they are distributed via conda or pip. (likely; https://github.com/SBC-Utrecht/pytom-match-pick)
- GitHub artifact attestations are free in public repositories on all current plans, using the Sigstore public-good instance with a public transparency log; actions/attest-build-provenance v4 wraps actions/attest. (verified; https://github.com/actions/attest-build-provenance)
- Cosign keyless signatures are stored as OCI 1.1 referrers next to the signed digest; signing an ORAS-pushed SIF artifact by digest should work the same way but was not tested. (likely; https://docs.sigstore.dev/cosign/signing/signing_with_containers/)
- Apptainer build accepts --mksquashfs-args (e.g. zstd) to shrink SIFs, but non-gzip squashfs may not work on some installations. (likely; https://apptainer.org/docs/user/1.5/build_a_container.html)

### Open questions

- What is the source of truth for published images: Dockerfiles (needed for buildx, multi-layer OCI and CVMFS) or the existing .def files (SIF-only, a 10 GB cap, no OCI)? Recommended: Dockerfiles for every published tool. Keep a .def only for def-only tools (ChimeraX) and as a thin `Bootstrap: docker` wrapper for sites that want to build a SIF from the OCI image.
- Under which GitHub namespace do the packages live: a personal account or an organisation? Recommended: an organisation, so packages, permissions and the attestation identity outlive one person, with each package flipped to public on first push.
- Which tools are published as images, and which are recipe-only? Recommended: publish everything except ChimeraX (licence) and easymode until it has a recipe. Do not ask UCSF for a redistribution exception.
- Is a two-stage rewrite (devel to runtime/base) acceptable for RELION, Warp and IsoNet2, given that it changes what is in the image (no nvcc at run time)? Recommended: yes. Check that no tool JIT-compiles with nvcc at run time; NVRTC and PTX JIT stay available in runtime.
- Is a 12–13 GB image still needed after the two-stage rewrite? Recommended: measure. Anything still over 9.5 GB ships OCI-only (docker://) until it shrinks, rather than splitting SIFs into release-asset chunks.
- What is the tag and promotion policy? Recommended: <toolversion>-cuda<ver> plus sha-<commit> tags. A crboost release lock file pins digests, and a digest is promoted only after `crboost test -m cluster` passes on our cluster and on the collaborating site's.
- Does the collaborating site mount CVMFS (unpacked.cern.ch), and what NVIDIA driver and GPU generations do its nodes run (CUDA 12.9 images and sm_100 PTX need a recent driver)? Recommended: ask before December; it decides between the CVMFS and ORAS routes for that site.
- Who owns the DL tilt-filter weights, and under what licence may they be published? Recommended: settle it, then publish them on Zenodo (DOI) mirrored as a release asset.
- Should there be a second registry for resilience if GHCR's free policy changes (at least one month's notice)? Recommended: not for December. Later, a Quay.io mirror, plus Zenodo snapshots of each release's SIFs.

## recon:ops_security: the same brief, re-run on its own after the first session stopped

No live secret is in the repo or its history: the token scan found no ghp_/github_pat_/sk-/AKIA/private keys, and the maintainer's actual ~/.crboost/token and session secret appear in neither history nor working tree. So no history rewrite is needed, and with 24 PRs pinning old commits on GitHub a rewrite would not purge them without Support anyway. The thing most likely to endanger a release is how installs are operated, not the code. The lab's shared install is world-writable (777 on drivers/, config/conf.yaml, config/qsub.sh, .uv/env, .git and .claude/settings.local.json, under a 777 root-owned group dir on NFSv3). Every lab member's server and SLURM jobs run that code, so anyone who can write there can run code as all of them. Updating by `git pull` under running servers mixes versions: there are 475 function-local repo imports, spawned workers, and queued drivers that read the checkout at start. Tagged release directories plus a `current` symlink fit the existing design almost for free, because both launchers resolve `pwd -P` and REPO_ROOT/sys.executable get baked into each job. Three small changes are needed first: key the port and the already-running check on the unresolved install path, share .uv across releases, and keep site config outside the checkout. The network surface is tight: one ASGI token gate in front of everything, raw-file routes limited to loaded projects, agent writes bearer-only, and the MCP forwarder checks the listener's uid. Two things are loose. The server binds all interfaces over plain HTTP by default, and the ChimeraX/VNC curation sessions are reachable by other users on the same compute node: REST with no auth, and a VNC password kept in a group-readable project folder. The pinned stack carries 85 known advisories (Pillow 61, NiceGUI 14, Starlette 6, requests 3, biopython 1). Nearly all sit behind the token gate, but they are the first thing a reviewer or Dependabot will show. GitHub-side protections are off: secret scanning, push protection, Dependabot security updates, private vulnerability reporting, branch protection; the dependency graph seems off too.
